# Chilean Civil Code and Related Laws \- Consolidated Text

## DFL 1, JUSTICE (2000)

### Decree

**Santiago, May 16, 2000**

**Article 1:** The D.F.L. No. 1, of October 28, 1999, of the Ministry of Justice, approved by the Comptroller General of the Republic, unpublished, is hereby repealed.

**Article 2:** The following consolidated, coordinated, and systematized text of the Civil Code is hereby established.

---

# CIVIL CODE

## PRELIMINARY TITLE

### § 1\. Of the Law

**Article 1:** The law is a declaration of sovereign will which, expressed in the form prescribed by the Constitution, commands, prohibits, or permits.

**Article 2:** Custom does not constitute law except in cases where the law refers to it.

**Article 3:** Only the legislator may explain or interpret the law in a generally binding manner.

Judicial sentences have binding force only with respect to the cases in which they are pronounced.

**Article 4:** The provisions contained in the Commercial Code, Mining Code, Army and Navy Code, and other special codes shall apply preferentially over those of this Code.

**Article 5:** The Supreme Court and the Courts of Appeal, in March of each year, shall report to the President of the Republic on doubts and difficulties they have encountered in the understanding and application of the laws, and on the gaps they notice in them.

### § 2\. Promulgation of the Law

**Article 6:** The law is binding only after being promulgated in accordance with the Political Constitution of the State and published according to the following precepts.

The supreme decree promulgating a law initiated by a motion shall contain, following the name of the law, the names of the deputies or senators who authored said initiative.

**Article 7:** Publication of the law shall be made by its insertion in the Official Gazette, and from the date of this it shall be considered known by all and shall be binding.

For all legal purposes, the date of the law shall be its publication date in the Official Gazette.

However, any law may establish different rules regarding its publication and the date or dates on which it shall enter into force.

**Article 8:** No one may plead ignorance of the law after it has entered into force.

### § 3\. Effects of the Law

**Article 9:** The law may only provide for the future and shall never have retroactive effect.

However, laws that merely declare the meaning of other laws shall be understood as incorporated into them; but they shall in no way affect the effects of final judicial sentences in the intervening time.

**Article 10:** Acts prohibited by law are null and void; except insofar as it expressly designates another effect than nullity for the case of contravention.

**Article 11:** When the law declares an act null, with the express or tacit purpose of preventing fraud, or of providing for some object of public or private convenience, the law shall not be disregarded even if it is proven that the act it annuls has not been fraudulent or contrary to the purpose of the law.

**Article 12:** Rights conferred by law may be waived, provided they only concern the individual interest of the renouncer, and that their renunciation is not prohibited.

**Article 13:** The provisions of a law relating to particular things or businesses shall prevail over the general provisions of the same law when there is opposition between them.

**Article 14:** The law is binding on all inhabitants of the Republic, including foreigners.

**Article 15:** Chileans shall remain subject to national laws that regulate civil obligations and rights, notwithstanding their residence or domicile in a foreign country:

1. In matters relating to the status of persons and their capacity to perform certain acts that are to have effect in Chile;  
2. In the obligations and rights arising from family relationships; but only with respect to their spouses and Chilean relatives.

**Article 16:** Property located in Chile is subject to Chilean laws, even if its owners are foreigners and do not reside in Chile.

This provision shall be understood without prejudice to stipulations contained in contracts validly executed in a foreign country.

But the effects of contracts executed in a foreign country to be performed in Chile shall be governed by Chilean laws.

**Article 17:** The form of public instruments is determined by the law of the country in which they were executed. Their authenticity shall be proven according to the rules established in the Code of Procedure.

Form refers to external solemnities, and authenticity to the fact of having been actually executed and authorized by the persons and in the manner expressed in such instruments.

**Article 18:** In cases where Chilean laws require public instruments for evidence to be presented and have effect in Chile, private writings shall not be valid, whatever their force may be in the country where they were executed.

### § 4\. Interpretation of the Law

**Article 19:** When the meaning of the law is clear, its literal text shall not be disregarded under the pretext of consulting its spirit.

But one may, to interpret an obscure expression of the law, resort to its intention or spirit, clearly manifested in the law itself, or in the trustworthy history of its establishment.

**Article 20:** The words of the law shall be understood in their natural and obvious sense, according to the general usage of the same words; but when the legislator has expressly defined them for certain matters, they shall be given their legal meaning in these matters.

**Article 21:** Technical words of any science or art shall be taken in the sense given to them by those who practice the same science or art; unless it clearly appears that they have been taken in a different sense.

**Article 22:** The context of the law shall serve to illustrate the meaning of each of its parts, so that there is due correspondence and harmony among all of them.

Obscure passages of a law may be illustrated by other laws, particularly if they deal with the same subject.

**Article 23:** The favorable or odious nature of a provision shall not be taken into account to expand or restrict its interpretation. The extent to be given to any law shall be determined by its genuine sense and according to the preceding rules of interpretation.

**Article 24:** In cases where the preceding rules of interpretation cannot be applied, obscure or contradictory passages shall be interpreted in the manner that seems most in accordance with the general spirit of the legislation and natural equity.

### § 5\. Definition of Various Words Frequently Used in Laws

**Article 25:** The words man, person, child, adult, and other similar words that in their general sense apply to individuals of the human species, without distinction of sex, shall be understood to include both sexes in the provisions of the laws, unless the nature of the provision or the context manifestly limits them to one.

On the contrary, the words woman, girl, widow, and other similar words that designate the female sex shall not apply to the other sex, unless the law expressly extends them to it.

**Article 26:** The following are called infants or children: all who have not completed seven years of age. Puberty: the male who has not completed fourteen years and the female who has not completed twelve years. Adult: one who has ceased to be a pubescent. Of legal age, or simply major: one who has completed eighteen years. Minor: one who has not reached that age.

**Article 27:** The degrees of consanguinity between two persons are counted by the number of generations. Thus, the grandchild is in the second degree of consanguinity with the grandparent, and two first cousins are in the fourth degree of consanguinity with each other.

When one of the two persons is an ascendant of the other, consanguinity is in the direct line; and when the two persons descend from a common ascendant, and one of them is not an ascendant of the other, consanguinity is in the collateral or transverse line.

**Article 28:** Consanguinity is the relationship that exists between two persons who descend one from the other or from a common parent, in any of their degrees.

**Article 29:** Repealed.

**Article 30:** Repealed.

**Article 31:** Relationship by affinity is that which exists between a person who is or has been married and the consanguineous relatives of their spouse.

The line and degree of affinity of a person with a consanguineous relative of their spouse are determined by the line and degree of consanguinity of said spouse with the referred relative. Thus, one spouse is in the first degree of affinity, in the direct line, with the children born to their spouse in a previous marriage, and in the second degree of affinity, in the transverse line, with the siblings of their spouse.

Laws or other provisions that refer to the expressions husband and wife, husband or wife, shall be understood to apply to all spouses, without distinction of sex, sexual orientation, or gender identity.

**Article 32:** Repealed.

**Article 33:** Those whose filiation has been determined, in accordance with the rules provided in Title VII of Book I of this Code, have the civil status of children with respect to a person. The law considers all children equal.

**Article 34:** The fathers and mothers of a person are their progenitors, with respect to whom a filiation relationship has been determined. They shall be understood as their mother and/or father, their two mothers, or their two fathers.

Laws or other provisions that refer to the expressions father and mother, or father or mother, or other similar expressions, shall be understood to apply to all progenitors, without distinction of sex, gender identity, or sexual orientation, unless the context or express provision requires otherwise.

**Article 35:** Repealed.

**Article 36:** Repealed.

**Article 37:** The filiation of children may not be determined with respect to one of their progenitors, or both.

**Article 38:** Repealed.

**Article 39:** Repealed.

**Article 40:** Repealed.

**Article 41:** Siblings may be of simple or double connection. Those who are by both progenitors are called siblings of double connection, and those who are only by one of them are called siblings of simple connection.

**Article 42:** In cases where the law provides that relatives of a person shall be heard, the spouse of that person and their consanguineous relatives of both sexes, of legal age, shall be understood to be included in that denomination. In the absence of a sufficient number of consanguineous relatives, relatives by affinity shall be heard. Descendants and ascendants shall be preferred to collaterals, and among these, those of closer relationship. Relatives shall be summoned and shall appear to be heard verbally, in the form prescribed by the Code of Procedure.

**Article 43:** The legal representatives of a person are one or both progenitors, the adoptive parent, and their guardian or curator.

**Article 44:** The law distinguishes three kinds of fault or negligence.

Grave fault, gross negligence, lata culpa, is that which consists in not managing another's affairs with the care that even negligent and imprudent persons usually employ in their own affairs. This fault in civil matters is equivalent to fraud.

Slight fault, slight negligence, slight carelessness, is the lack of that diligence and care that men ordinarily employ in their own affairs. Fault or carelessness, without further qualification, means slight fault or carelessness. This kind of fault is opposed to ordinary or medium diligence or care. One who must administer a business as a good father of a family is responsible for this kind of fault.

Slightest fault or negligence is the lack of that exquisite diligence that a judicious person employs in the administration of their important affairs. This kind of fault is opposed to the highest diligence or care.

Fraud consists of the positive intention to cause injury to the person or property of another.

**Article 45:** Force majeure or fortuitous event is the unforeseeable that cannot be resisted, such as a shipwreck, an earthquake, capture by enemies, acts of authority exercised by a public official, etc.

**Article 46:** Security generally means any obligation contracted for the security of another's obligation, whether one's own or another's. Species of security are surety, mortgage, and pledge.

**Article 47:** A fact is said to be presumed when it is deduced from certain known antecedents or circumstances.

If these antecedents or circumstances that give rise to the presumption are determined by law, the presumption is called legal.

Proof of the non-existence of the fact legally presumed shall be permitted, even if the antecedents or circumstances from which the law infers it are certain; unless the law itself expressly rejects this evidence, given the antecedents or circumstances.

If a thing, according to the expression of the law, is presumed of right, it is understood that contrary proof is inadmissible, given the antecedents or circumstances.

**Article 48:** All periods of days, months, or years mentioned in laws or decrees of the President of the Republic, of the courts or tribunals, shall be understood to be complete; and they shall run until midnight of the last day of the period.

The first and last day of a period of months or years shall have the same number in the respective months. The period of one month may therefore be 28, 29, 30, or 31 days, and the period of one year 365 or 366 days, as the case may be.

If the month in which a period of months or years is to begin has more days than the month in which the period is to end, and if the period runs from one of the days in which the first of said months exceeds the second, the last day of the period shall be the last day of this second month.

These rules shall apply to prescriptions, age qualifications, and generally to any periods or terms prescribed in laws or in acts of Chilean authorities; unless the same laws or acts expressly provide otherwise.

**Article 49:** When it is said that an act must be executed on or within a certain period, it shall be understood to be valid if executed before midnight on the last day of the period; and when it is required that a space of time elapse for certain rights to arise or expire, it shall be understood that these rights do not arise or expire until after midnight on the last day of said space of time.

**Article 50:** In the periods fixed in laws, or in decrees of the President of the Republic, or of the courts or tribunals, holidays shall be included; unless the period fixed is of business days, expressed as such, in which case holidays shall not be counted.

**Article 51:** The measures of length, weight, duration, and any others mentioned in laws, or in decrees of the President of the Republic, or of the courts or tribunals, shall always be understood according to legal definitions; and in the absence of these, in the general and popular sense, unless otherwise stated.

### § 6\. Repeal of Laws

**Article 52:** The repeal of laws may be express or tacit.

It is express when the new law expressly states that it repeals the old one.

It is tacit when the new law contains provisions that cannot be reconciled with those of the previous law.

The repeal of a law may be total or partial.

**Article 53:** Tacit repeal leaves in force in previous laws, even if they deal with the same matter, everything that does not conflict with the provisions of the new law.

---

# BOOK ONE: OF PERSONS

## TITLE I: OF PERSONS, AS TO THEIR NAME, NATIONALITY, AND DOMICILE

### § 1\. Division of Persons

**Article 54:** Persons are natural or juridical. The legal personality and special rules relating to it are dealt with in the final title of this Book.

**Article 55:** All individuals of the human species are persons, whatever their age, sex, lineage, or condition. They are divided into Chileans and foreigners.

**Article 56:** Chileans are those whom the Constitution of the State declares as such. All others are foreigners.

**Article 57:** The law does not recognize differences between Chilean and foreigner regarding the acquisition and enjoyment of civil rights governed by this Code.

**Article 58:** Persons are also divided into domiciled and transient.

### § 2\. Name of Persons

**Article 58 bis:** Name is the set of words that legally serves to identify a person. It is formed by the given name(s) and the surname(s) with which the person is individualized in their respective birth registration.

**Article 58 ter:** The first surname of the progenitor(s) shall be transmitted to their children, in the order determined, according to the cases, by application of the following rules:

1. In the birth registration of the first common child, the progenitors shall determine, by mutual agreement, the order of transmission of their respective first surnames, which shall apply to all their common children. In the absence of an agreement at the time of registering the first common child, their will shall be understood to be that the order of surnames be determined by lottery before the Civil Registry Officer.  
     
2. In every birth registration in which the filiation of the newborn is determined with respect to both progenitors at the time of registration, the Civil Registry Officer shall proceed according to the order of surnames established in the birth registration of the first common child of said persons; and if they have no more common children, according to the order determined when making the registration, in accordance with the provisions of the preceding rule.  
     
3. In the birth registration of a child whose filiation at the time of registration is determined only with respect to one of the progenitors, the newborn shall be registered with the respective first surname of said progenitor. In this case, when the filiation not determined at the time of birth registration is subsequently established, if there is another or other common children of said progenitors, the order of surnames established in the birth registration of their first common child shall apply. If, on the contrary, there are no more common children of said progenitors, the first surname of the progenitor that was determined at the time of birth registration shall precede the other surname, unless, the child not having reached the age of majority, the progenitors declare, by mutual agreement, their will to proceed with the reverse order.

However, to apply the rules indicated in the preceding paragraph, the Civil Registry Officer must previously verify if there are children registered in the name of each of the progenitors in the registries.

Once the order of the surnames of the first common child is established in the birth registration, the other children that two progenitors have in common shall always be registered with the same order of surnames, in accordance with the provisions of this article.

Birth registrations and declarations of the agreement of the progenitors, regarding the order of surnames, shall be made in accordance with the provisions of this article, and in the manner determined by the regulations.

### § 3\. Of Domicile as Depending on Residence and Intent to Remain

**Article 59:** Domicile consists of residence, accompanied, really or presumably, by the intent to remain there.

It is divided into political and civil.

**Article 60:** Political domicile relates to the territory of the State in general. One who has or acquires it is or becomes a member of Chilean society, even if they retain the status of foreigner.

The establishment and effects of political domicile belong to International Law.

**Article 61:** Civil domicile relates to a determined part of the territory of the State.

**Article 62:** The place where an individual is settled, or where they habitually exercise their profession or occupation, determines their civil domicile or neighborhood.

**Article 63:** The intent to remain is not presumed, nor is civil domicile consequently acquired in a place, by the mere fact that an individual has lived for some time in their own or another's house there, if they have their domestic home elsewhere or if other circumstances show that the residence is accidental, such as that of a traveler, or one who exercises a temporary commission, or one who engages in some itinerant trade.

**Article 64:** On the contrary, the intent to remain and settle in a place is presumed from the fact of opening there a shop, pharmacy, factory, workshop, inn, school, or other durable establishment, to administer it in person; from the fact of accepting in said place a council position, or a fixed position of those regularly granted for a long time; and from other analogous circumstances.

**Article 65:** Civil domicile is not changed by the fact that the individual resides for a long time elsewhere, voluntarily or forcibly, keeping their family and the principal seat of their business in the previous domicile.

Thus, confined by judicial decree to a determined place, or banished in the same manner outside the Republic, they shall retain their previous domicile, as long as they maintain there their family and the principal seat of their business.

**Article 66:** Bishops, priests, and other ecclesiastics obliged to a determined residence have their domicile there.

**Article 67:** When constitutive circumstances of civil domicile concur in various territorial sections with respect to the same individual, they shall be understood to have it in all of them; but if it concerns things that have special relation to one of said sections exclusively, that alone shall be for such cases the civil domicile of the individual.

**Article 68:** Mere residence shall serve as civil domicile with respect to persons who have no civil domicile elsewhere.

**Article 69:** In a contract, a special civil domicile may be established by common agreement for the judicial or extrajudicial acts arising from the same contract.

**Article 70:** Parochial, municipal, provincial domicile, or relative to any other section of the territory, is determined mainly by the laws and ordinances that constitute special rights and obligations for particular purposes of government, police, and administration in the respective parishes, communities, provinces, etc., and is acquired or lost according to said laws or ordinances. In the absence of special provisions in said laws or ordinances, it is acquired or lost according to the rules of this title.

### § 4\. Of Domicile as Depending on the Person's Status

**Article 71:** Repealed.

**Article 72:** One who lives under parental authority follows the domicile of the one who exercises it, and one who is under guardianship or curatorship follows the domicile of their guardian or curator.

**Article 73:** The domicile of a person shall also be the domicile of their servants and dependents who reside in the same house as them; without prejudice to the provisions of the two preceding articles.

---

## TITLE II: OF THE BEGINNING AND END OF THE EXISTENCE OF PERSONS

**Article 74:** The legal existence of every person begins at birth, that is, upon complete separation from their mother.

The creature that dies in the mother's womb, or that perishes before being completely separated from their mother, or that has not survived the separation for even a moment, shall be deemed never to have existed.

**Article 75:** The law protects the life of the unborn. The judge, consequently, shall take, at the request of any person or ex officio, all measures deemed convenient to protect the existence of the unborn, whenever they believe it to be in any way endangered.

Any punishment of the mother, which could endanger the life or health of the creature in her womb, shall be postponed until after birth.

**Article 76:** From the time of birth, the time of conception is inferred according to the following rule:

It is presumed as a matter of law that conception preceded birth by no less than one hundred and eighty full days and no more than three hundred days, counted backward from midnight on the day of birth.

**Article 77:** The rights that would be conferred to the creature in the mother's womb, if they had been born and lived, shall be suspended until birth occurs. And if birth constitutes a beginning of existence, the newborn shall enter into the enjoyment of said rights, as if they had existed at the time they were conferred. In the case of article 74, paragraph 2, these rights shall pass to other persons, as if the creature had never existed.

### § 2\. Of the End of the Existence of Persons

**Article 78:** A person ends in natural death.

**Article 79:** If, because two or more persons have perished in the same event, such as a shipwreck, fire, ruin, or battle, or for any other cause, the order of their deaths cannot be known, in all cases proceedings shall be as if said persons had perished at the same moment, and none of them had survived the others.

### § 3\. Of Presumption of Death by Disappearance

**Article 80:** An individual who has disappeared, their whereabouts being unknown, is presumed dead, upon fulfillment of the conditions to be stated.

**Article 81:**

1. The declaration of presumed death must be made by the judge of the last domicile the disappeared person had in Chile, having previously proven that the whereabouts of the disappeared person are unknown, that possible efforts have been made to ascertain them, and that at least five years have elapsed since the date of the last news of their existence.  
     
2. Among these proofs, the citation of the disappeared person shall be mandatory; which must have been repeated up to three times in the official newspaper, with more than two months between each two citations.  
     
3. The declaration may be requested by any person having an interest in it, provided at least three months have elapsed since the last citation.  
     
4. The defender of absentees shall be heard before proceeding to the declaration, and in all subsequent judicial proceedings; and the judge, at the request of the defender, or of any person having an interest in it, or ex officio, may require, in addition to the proofs presented of the disappearance, if they do not deem them satisfactory, such others as circumstances may warrant.  
     
5. All sentences, both final and interlocutory, shall be inserted in the official newspaper.  
     
6. The judge shall fix as the presumptive day of death the last day of the first biennium counted from the date of the last news; and after five years have elapsed from the same date, they shall grant provisional possession of the disappeared person's property.  
     
7. However, if after a person received a serious wound in war, or was overtaken by other similar danger, no news of them has been received, and after five years have elapsed and the justification and citations provided in the preceding numbers have been carried out, the judge shall fix as the presumptive day of death the day of the action of war or danger, or, if that day is not entirely determined, shall adopt a mean between the beginning and end of the period in which the event could have occurred, and shall immediately grant definitive possession of the disappeared person's property.  
     
8. Any vessel or aircraft that does not appear within three months of the date of the last news of it shall be deemed lost. After this period, anyone having an interest in it may request the declaration of presumed death of those who were on the vessel or aircraft. The judge shall fix the presumptive day of death in accordance with the preceding number, and shall immediately grant definitive possession of the disappeared persons' property.

If the vessel or aircraft is found wrecked or lost, or its remains, the same rules of the preceding paragraph shall apply, provided that the bodies of all or some of its occupants cannot be located, or the remains of those found cannot be identified.

If during navigation or flight, a crew member or passenger falls into the sea or on land and disappears without their remains being found, the judge shall proceed in the manner indicated in the preceding paragraphs; but there must be record in the proceedings that in the summary conducted by the maritime or aeronautical authorities, the disappearance of these persons and the impossibility of their being alive has been fully demonstrated.

In this case, the provisions of number 2 shall not apply, nor the period established in number 3; but it shall be mandatory to hear the General Directorate of the Navy or the General Directorate of Aeronautics, as appropriate.

9. After six months of an earthquake or catastrophe that has caused or could have caused the death of numerous persons in determined populations or regions, anyone having an interest in it may request the declaration of presumed death of the disappeared who inhabited those populations or regions.

In this case, the citation of the disappeared shall be made by notice published once in the Official Gazette corresponding to the first or fifteenth day, or the following business day, if not published on the indicated dates, and twice in a newspaper of the commune or of the capital of the province or of the capital of the region, if there is none in the commune, with no less than fifteen days between these two publications. The judge may order that by the same notice two or more disappeared persons be cited.

The judge shall fix, as the presumptive day of death, the day of the earthquake, catastrophe, or natural phenomenon and shall immediately grant definitive possession of the disappeared persons' property, but it shall be mandatory to hear the Defender of Absentees.

**Article 82:** The judge shall grant definitive possession, instead of provisional possession, if, upon completion of said five years, it is proven that seventy years have elapsed since the birth of the disappeared person. They may also grant it, after ten years have elapsed from the date of the last news, whatever the age of the disappeared person might be, if alive.

**Article 83:** During the five years or six months prescribed in numbers 6, 7, and 8 of article 81, the disappearance shall be regarded as mere absence, and the disappeared person's interests shall be cared for by their attorneys or legal representatives.

**Article 84:** By the decree of provisional possession, the conjugal partnership shall be dissolved or the participation in the partnership gains shall terminate, according to which existed with the disappeared person; the opening and publication of the will shall proceed, if the disappeared person left one, and provisional possession shall be given to the presumptive heirs.

In the absence of heirs, proceedings shall be in accordance with the provisions for the same case in Book III, title On the opening of the succession.

**Article 85:** Presumptive heirs of the disappeared person are the testamentary or legitimate heirs they were on the date of presumed death.

The estate in which they are presumed to succeed shall include the property, rights, and actions of the disappeared person, as they were on the date of presumed death.

**Article 86:** The provisional possessors shall first make a solemn inventory of the property, or shall review and rectify with the same solemnity the existing inventory.

**Article 87:** The provisional possessors shall represent the succession in actions and defenses against third parties.

**Article 88:** The provisional possessors may immediately sell a part of the movable property or all of it, if the judge deems it convenient, after hearing the defender of absentees.

The real property of the disappeared person may not be alienated or mortgaged before definitive possession, except for necessary or evident useful cause, declared by the judge with knowledge of the case, and with hearing of the defender.

The sale of any part of the disappeared person's property shall be made at public auction.

**Article 89:** Each of the provisional possessors shall give security for conservation and restitution, and shall make the respective fruits and interests their own.

**Article 90:** If during the provisional possession the disappeared person does not reappear, or news is not received that warrants the distribution of their property according to the general rules, definitive possession shall be decreed and the securities shall be cancelled.

By virtue of definitive possession, the restrictions imposed by article 88 cease.

If there has been no previous provisional possession, by the decree of definitive possession, the succession of the disappeared person shall be opened according to the general rules.

**Article 91:** Once definitive possession is decreed, the owners and the fiduciaries of property usufructed or held in trust by the disappeared person, the legatees, and in general all those who have rights subordinate to the condition of the death of the disappeared person, may enforce them as in the case of actual death.

**Article 92:** One who claims a right for whose existence it is assumed that the disappeared person died on the date of presumed death, shall not be obliged to prove that the disappeared person actually died on that date; and as long as no contrary evidence is presented, they may use their right in the terms of the preceding articles.

And on the contrary, anyone who claims a right for whose existence it is required that the disappeared person died before or after that date, shall be obliged to prove it; and without this proof, they cannot prevent the claimed right from passing to others, nor demand any responsibility from them.

**Article 93:** The decree of definitive possession may be rescinded in favor of the disappeared person if they reappear, or of their legitimate heirs born during the disappearance, or of their spouse by marriage contracted in the same period.

**Article 94:** In the rescission of the decree of definitive possession, the following rules shall be observed:

1. The disappeared person may request rescission at any time they appear or prove their existence.  
     
2. Other persons may only request it within the respective periods of limitation counted from the date of the actual death.  
     
3. This benefit shall only avail the persons who obtain it by judicial sentence.  
     
4. By virtue of this benefit, the property shall be recovered in the state in which it is found, with alienations, mortgages, and other real rights legally constituted on them subsisting.  
     
5. For all restitution, the defendants shall be considered possessors in good faith, unless proved otherwise.  
     
6. Having known and concealed the actual death of the disappeared person, or their existence, constitutes bad faith.

### § 4\. Of Judicial Confirmation of Death

**Article 95:** Whenever a person's disappearance has occurred under circumstances such that death can be considered certain, even if their corpse is not found, the judge of the last domicile the deceased had in Chile, at the request of anyone having an interest in it, may deem their death confirmed for civil purposes and order the registration of the corresponding resolution in the Civil Registry and Identification Service. The same rule shall apply in cases where identification of the corpse is not possible.

**Article 96:** An extract of the resolution confirming the disappearance's death shall be published in the Official Gazette within sixty days from when it is final and enforceable. Said extract shall contain, at least, the essential background information for their identification and the date of death established by the judge.

**Article 97:** The resolution referred to in article 95 may be set aside in accordance with the provisions of the preceding paragraph.

---

## TITLE III: OF ESPOUSALS

**Article 98:** Espousals or betrothal, that is, the promise of marriage mutually accepted, is a private matter, which the laws entirely subject to the honor and conscience of the individual, and which produces no obligation before the civil law.

This promise may not be alleged either to demand that the marriage be carried out, or to claim damages.

**Article 99:** Neither may the fine stipulated by one of the spouses in favor of the other for the case of non-fulfillment of the promise be demanded.

But if the fine has been paid, its return may not be demanded.

**Article 100:** The foregoing does not prevent the return of things donated and delivered under the condition of a marriage that has not taken place from being demanded.

**Article 101:** Nor does the foregoing prevent proof of the contract of espousals from being admitted as an aggravating circumstance in the crime of seduction.

---

## TITLE IV: OF MARRIAGE

**Article 102:** Marriage is a solemn contract by which two persons are united actually and indissolubly, and for life, for the purpose of living together, procreating, and assisting each other mutually.

**Article 103:** Marriage may be celebrated by an attorney especially authorized for this purpose. The power of attorney shall be granted by public deed, and shall indicate the name, surname, profession, and domicile of the contracting parties and the attorney.

**Articles 104-123:** Repealed.

---

## TITLE V: OF SECOND MARRIAGES

**Article 124:** One who has children from a previous marriage under their parental authority, or under their guardianship or curatorship, and wishes to marry again, must proceed to the solemn inventory of the property they are administering and which belongs to them as heirs of their deceased spouse or by any other title.

For the preparation of this inventory, a special curator shall be given to said children.

**Article 125:** The appointment of a curator shall be warranted even if the children have no property of any kind in the possession of the spouse who wishes to marry again. When this is the case, the special curator shall testify to it.

**Article 126:** The corresponding Civil Registry Officer shall not permit the marriage of one who wishes to marry again, without the presentation of an authentic certificate of the appointment of a special curator for the aforementioned purposes, or without a summary information preceding that they have no children from a previous marriage who are under their parental authority or under their guardianship or curatorship.

**Article 127:** The widower or divorced person or one who has had their marriage annulled, who through their negligence has failed to make the inventory provided for in article 124 in a timely manner, shall lose the right to succeed as a legitimate heir or as an intestate heir to the child whose property they have administered.

**Articles 128-129:** Repealed.

**Article 130:** When, because the mother has passed to other nuptials, there is doubt as to which of the two marriages a child belongs, and a judicial decision is invoked in accordance with the rules of Title VIII, the judge shall decide, taking into consideration the circumstances. Expert biological tests and the opinion of specialists shall be ordered if requested.

---

## TITLE VI: OBLIGATIONS AND RIGHTS BETWEEN SPOUSES

### § 1\. General Rules

**Article 131:** Spouses are obliged to keep faith with each other, to support and help each other in all circumstances of life. Likewise, they owe each other mutual respect and protection.

**Article 132:** Adultery constitutes a serious infraction of the duty of fidelity imposed by marriage and gives rise to the sanctions provided by law.

A married person who lies with another person who is not their spouse commits adultery.

**Article 133:** Both spouses have the right and duty to live in the common home, unless one of them has serious reasons not to do so.

**Article 134:** Both spouses must provide for the needs of the common family, considering their economic capacities and the property regime between them.

The judge, if necessary, shall regulate the contribution.

**Article 135:** By the fact of marriage, a partnership of property is contracted between spouses, and the husband takes the administration of the wife's property, according to the rules that will be set forth in the title On the conjugal partnership.

The provisions of the preceding paragraph shall not apply to marriages celebrated between persons of the same sex, who, by the fact of marriage, shall be understood to be totally separated as to property, without prejudice to the faculty of opting for the regime of participation in the partnership gains in the matrimonial capitulations, or of substituting it for this regime during the marriage, in accordance with the provisions of article 1723\.

Those who have married in a foreign country shall be regarded in Chile as separated as to property, unless they register their marriage in the Registry of the First Section of the Commune of Santiago, and agree in that act to a conjugal partnership or participation in the partnership gains, leaving record thereof in said registration. In the case of marriages between persons of the same sex married in a foreign country, they may only agree to the regime of participation in the partnership gains.

**Article 136:** Spouses shall be obliged to provide each other with the necessary assistance for their judicial actions or defenses. The husband shall also, if married in a conjugal partnership, provide the wife with the expenses for the litigation she pursues against him, if she does not have the property referred to in articles 150, 166, and 167, or they are insufficient.

**Article 137:** The acts and contracts of a married woman in a conjugal partnership only bind her in the property she administers in accordance with articles 150, 166, and 167\.

However, purchases made on credit of movable objects naturally destined for the ordinary consumption of the family bind the husband in his property and in that of the conjugal partnership; and they also bind the wife's own property, up to the amount of the particular benefit she derives from the act, including in this benefit that of the common family to the extent she was legally obliged to provide for its needs.

**Article 138 (145):** If due to an impediment of long or indefinite duration, such as interdiction, prolonged absence, or disappearance, the husband's administration is suspended, the provisions of paragraph 4 of the title On the conjugal partnership shall be observed.

If the impediment is not of long or indefinite duration, the wife may act with respect to the husband's property, that of the conjugal partnership, and her own property administered by the husband, with authorization from the judge, with knowledge of the case, when delay would cause harm.

In the case referred to in the preceding paragraph, the wife binds the husband in his property and in the partnership property in the same way as if the act were performed by the husband; and she also binds her own property, up to the amount of the particular benefit she derives from the act.

**Article 138 bis:** If the husband unjustifiably refuses to perform an act or enter into a contract with respect to the wife's own property, the judge may authorize her to act on her own, prior hearing to which the husband shall be summoned.

In such case, the wife shall only bind her own property and the assets of her reserved or special patrimonies of articles 150, 166, and 167, but shall not bind the social assets nor the husband's own property, except to the extent of the benefit that the partnership or the husband derived from the act.

The same shall apply to appoint a partitioner, to provoke the partition, and to participate in it in cases where the wife has a share in the inheritance.

**Article 139 (148):** Repealed.

**Article 140 (149):** The rules of the preceding articles are subject to exceptions or modifications for the following reasons:

1. The existence of family property.  
2. The wife exercising a profession, industry, employment, or trade.  
3. Separation of property.  
4. Judicial separation of spouses.  
5. The regime of participation in the partnership gains.

The first four are dealt with in the following paragraphs; the last in Title XXII-A, of Book IV.

### § 2\. Of Family Property

**Article 141:** The immovable property owned by either spouse that serves as the principal residence of the family, and the movable property that furnishes it, may be declared family property and shall be governed by the rules of this paragraph, whatever the property regime of the marriage.

The judge shall summon the interested parties to the preparatory hearing. If no opposition is raised, the judge shall decide in the same hearing. Otherwise, or if the judge considers that there are insufficient grounds to decide, they shall summon to the trial hearing.

However, the mere filing of the claim shall provisionally transform the property in question into family property. In its first resolution, the judge shall order that the preceding circumstance be noted in the margin of the respective registration. The Registrar shall make the annotation with the sole merit of the decree, which the court shall notify ex officio.

For the purposes provided in this article, spouses shall enjoy the privilege of poverty.

The spouse who fraudulently acts to obtain the declaration referred to in this article shall indemnify the damages caused, without prejudice to the criminal sanction that may apply.

**Article 142:** Family property may not be voluntarily alienated or encumbered, nor may it be promised to encumber or alienate, without the authorization of the non-owner spouse. The same limitation shall apply to the celebration of lease, loan for use, or any other contracts that grant personal rights of use or enjoyment over any family property.

The authorization referred to in this article shall be specific and granted in writing, or by public deed if the act requires this solemnity, or by the spouse intervening expressly and directly in any way in the act. In all cases, it may be granted by means of a special mandate in writing or by public deed, as the case may be.

**Article 143:** The non-owner spouse whose will has not been expressed in accordance with the provisions of the preceding article may request the rescission of the act.

Acquirers of rights over an immovable property that is family property shall be in bad faith for the purposes of the restitution obligations arising from the declaration of nullity.

**Article 144:** In the cases of article 142, the will of the non-owner spouse of a family property may be supplanted by the judge in case of impossibility or refusal not based on the interest of the family. The judge shall decide after hearing the spouse, in case of their refusal.

**Article 145:** Spouses, by mutual agreement, may disaffect a family property. If the declaration refers to an immovable property, it must be by public deed annotated in the margin of the respective registration.

The owner spouse may request the judge to disaffect a family property, based on the fact that it is not currently destined for the purposes indicated in article 141, which they must prove. In this case, the judge shall proceed in the manner established in the second paragraph of article 141\.

The same rule shall apply if the marriage has been declared null, or has ended by death of one of the spouses or by divorce. In such cases, the owner of the family property or any of their heirs shall make the corresponding request to the judge.

**Article 146:** The provisions of this paragraph apply to the rights or shares that spouses have in companies that own an immovable property that is the principal residence of the family.

Once the affections of rights or shares occurs, the consent of both spouses shall also be required to perform any act as a partner or shareholder of the respective company that relates to the family property.

The affection of rights shall be made by declaration of either spouse contained in a public deed. In the case of a partnership, it shall be annotated in the margin of the respective social registration, if any. In the case of corporations, it shall be registered in the shareholders' register.

**Article 147:** During the marriage, the judge may prudentially constitute, in favor of the non-owner spouse, rights of usufruct, use, or habitation over family property. In the constitution of these rights and in fixing the term that puts an end to them, the judge shall especially consider the interest of the children, if any, and the patrimonial resources of the spouses.

The court may, in these cases, fix other obligations or conditions if deemed equitable.

The judicial declaration referred to in the preceding paragraph shall serve as title for all legal purposes.

The constitution of the aforementioned rights over family property shall not prejudice creditors the owner spouse had on the date of their constitution, nor benefit creditors the non-owner spouse may have at any time.

**Article 148:** Spouses who are sued benefit from the right of excussion. Consequently, either of them may require that before proceeding against family property, the credit be pursued against other property of the debtor. The provisions of Title XXXVI of Book IV on suretyship shall apply to the exercise of the excussion referred to in this article, as applicable.

Whenever by virtue of an executive action brought by a third-party creditor, attachment of any family property owned by the debtor spouse is ordered, the judge shall order the corresponding writ to be personally notified to the non-owner spouse. This notification shall not affect the rights and actions of the non-owner spouse over said property.

**Article 149:** Any stipulation that contravenes the provisions of this paragraph is null.

### § 3\. Exceptions Relating to the Wife's Profession or Trade

**Article 150:** A married woman of any age may freely engage in the exercise of an employment, office, profession, or industry.

A married woman who performs any employment or who exercises a profession, trade, or industry, separate from that of her husband, shall be considered separated as to property with respect to the exercise of that employment, office, profession, or industry and what she obtains from them, notwithstanding any stipulation to the contrary.

It is incumbent upon the wife to prove, both with respect to the husband and third parties, the origin and ownership of the property acquired in accordance with this article. For this purpose, she may use all means of proof established by law.

Third parties contracting with the wife shall be protected from any claim that she or the husband, their heirs or assignees, may interpose, based on the fact that the wife acted outside the terms of this article, provided that, not dealing with property covered by articles 1754 and 1755, the wife has proven, by public or private instruments, referred to in the instrument executed for that purpose, that she exercises or has exercised an employment, office, profession, or industry separate from that of her husband.

The acts or contracts entered into by the wife in this separate administration shall bind the property included in it and that she administers in accordance with the provisions of articles 166 and 167, and shall not bind the husband's property except in accordance with article 161\.

The husband's creditors shall have no action over the property the wife administers by virtue of this article, unless they prove that the contract entered into by him benefited the wife or the common family.

Upon dissolution of the conjugal partnership, the property referred to in this article shall enter into the partition of the partnership gains; unless the wife or her heirs renounce the latter, in which case the husband shall not be liable for the obligations contracted by the wife in her separate administration.

If the wife or her heirs accept the partnership gains, the husband shall be liable for those obligations up to the value of half of those property existing at the dissolution of the partnership. But to enjoy this benefit, he must prove the excess of the contribution required of him in accordance with article 1777\.

**Article 151:** Repealed.

### § 4\. Exceptions Relating to Separation of Property

**Article 152:** Separation of property is that which is effected without judicial separation, by decree of the competent court, by provision of law, or by agreement of the parties.

**Article 153:** The wife may not renounce in the matrimonial capitulations the faculty of requesting the separation of property to which the laws entitle her.

**Article 154:** Repealed.

**Article 155:** The judge shall decree the separation of property in the case of insolvency or fraudulent administration by the husband.

The judge shall also decree it if the husband, through his fault, fails to comply with the obligations imposed by articles 131 and 134, or incurs in any cause for judicial separation, according to the terms of the Civil Marriage Law.

In the case of unjustified absence of the husband for more than one year, the wife may request separation of property. The same shall apply if, without absence, there is de facto separation of the spouses.

If the husband's affairs are in bad condition, as a consequence of hazardous speculation, or of erroneous or careless administration, or there is imminent risk thereof, he may oppose the separation by providing surety or mortgages that sufficiently secure the wife's interests.

**Article 156:** When separation of property is requested, the judge, at the wife's request, may take such measures as deemed conducive to the security of her interests while the lawsuit lasts.

In the case of paragraph 3 of the preceding article, the judge may, at any time, at the wife's request, proceeding with knowledge of the case, take similar measures before separation of property is requested, requiring the wife to provide security for the results if deemed convenient.

**Article 157:** In the lawsuit for separation of property due to the husband's bad business condition, his confession does not constitute proof.

**Article 158:** What in the preceding articles of this paragraph is said of the husband or wife applies indistinctly to spouses in the regime of participation in the partnership gains.

Once separation is decreed, the division of the partnership gains and the payment of reimbursements or the calculation of the participation credit in the partnership gains shall proceed, according to the regime being terminated.

**Article 159:** Spouses separated as to property administer, with full independence from each other, the property they had before the marriage and those they acquire during it, by any title.

If spouses separate as to property during the marriage, the separate administration shall include the property obtained as a product of the liquidation of the conjugal partnership or of the regime of participation in the partnership gains that existed between them.

The foregoing is without prejudice to the provisions of paragraph 2 of Title VI of Book I of this Code.

**Article 160:** In a state of separation, both spouses must provide for the needs of the common family in proportion to their means. The judge, if necessary, shall regulate the contribution.

**Article 161:** Creditors of the separated wife, for acts or contracts legitimately entered into by her, shall have action over the wife's property.

The husband shall not be liable with his property, except when he has acceded as a surety, or in another way, to the obligations contracted by the wife.

He shall also be liable, proportionally to the benefit he may have derived from the obligations contracted by the wife; including in this benefit that of the common family, to the extent he was legally obliged to provide for its needs.

The same provisions apply to the separated wife with respect to the obligations contracted by the husband.

**Article 162:** If the separated wife entrusts to the husband the administration of any part of her property, the husband shall be obliged to the wife as a simple agent.

**Article 163:** Spouses separated as to property shall be given a curator for the administration of their property in all cases in which, if single, they would need a curator to administer it.

**Article 164:** Repealed.

**Article 165:** Separation effected by judicial decree or by provision of law is irrevocable and may not be rendered without effect by agreement of the spouses or by judicial resolution.

In the case of conventional separation, and also in the case of article 40 of the Civil Marriage Law, spouses may agree, once, to the regime of participation in the partnership gains, in accordance with the provisions of article 1723\.

**Article 166:** If a gift is made to a married woman, or an inheritance or legacy is left to her, with the precise condition that in the gifted, inherited, or bequeathed property the husband shall not have administration, and if said gift, inheritance, or legacy is accepted by the wife, the following rules shall be observed:

1. With respect to the gifted, inherited, or bequeathed property, the provisions of articles 159, 160, 161, 162, and 163 shall apply, but upon dissolution of the conjugal partnership, the obligations contracted by the wife in her separate administration may be pursued against all her property.  
     
2. The husband's creditors shall have no action over the property the wife administers by virtue of this article, unless they prove that the contract entered into by him benefited the wife or the common family.  
     
3. The fruits of the property she administers and everything she acquires with them shall belong to the wife, but upon dissolution of the conjugal partnership, the rules of article 150 shall apply to said fruits and acquisitions.

**Article 167:** If in the matrimonial capitulations it has been stipulated that the wife shall separately administer any part of her property, the rules of the preceding article shall apply to this partial separation.

### § 5\. Exceptions Relating to Judicial Separation

**Articles 168-171:** Repealed.

**Article 172:** The innocent spouse may revoke gifts made to the guilty spouse, provided the latter has given cause for divorce or judicial separation by adultery, atrocious cruelty, attempt on the life of the other spouse, or other crime of equal gravity.

**Article 173:** Spouses judicially separated administer their property with full independence from each other, in the terms of article 159\.

The foregoing is understood without prejudice to the provisions of Paragraph 2 of Title VI of Book I of this Code.

**Article 174:** The spouse who has not given cause for judicial separation shall have the right to be provided with maintenance by the other spouse according to the general rules.

**Article 175:** The spouse who has given cause for judicial separation through their fault shall have the right to be provided by the other spouse with what is necessary for their modest support; but in this case, the judge shall regulate the contribution especially considering the conduct observed by the alimony recipient before the respective lawsuit, during its development, or after it.

**Article 176:** Repealed.

**Article 177:** If the culpability of the spouse against whom judicial separation has been obtained is attenuated by serious circumstances in the conduct of the spouse who requested it, the judge may moderate the rigor of the preceding provisions.

**Article 178:** To judicial separation, the provisions of articles 160 and 165 shall apply.

---

## TITLE VII: OF FILIATION

### § 1\. General Rules

**Article 179:** Filiation by nature may be matrimonial or non-matrimonial.

Adoption, the rights between adopter and adopted, and the filiation that may be established between them, are governed by the respective law.

**Article 180:** Filiation is matrimonial when there is marriage between the progenitors at the time of conception or birth of the child.

Filiation is also matrimonial when the child's progenitors marry after their birth, provided that the filiation had been previously determined by the means this Code establishes with respect to those who marry, or is determined by recognition made by both progenitors in the act of marriage or during its validity, in the form prescribed by article 187\. This matrimonial filiation shall benefit, as appropriate, the posterity of the deceased child.

In all other cases, filiation is non-matrimonial.

**Article 181:** Filiation produces civil effects when it is legally determined, but these effects retroact to the time of the child's conception.

Notwithstanding, rights acquired and obligations contracted before its determination shall subsist, but the child shall participate in successions opened prior to the determination of their filiation when called in their capacity as such.

All the foregoing is understood without prejudice to the limitation of rights and actions, which shall occur according to the general rules.

The accreditation of the determined filiation shall be carried out in accordance with the rules established in Title XVII.

**Article 182:** The filiation of the child born through the application of human assisted reproduction techniques shall be determined with respect to the two persons who have undergone them.

The filiation determined according to the preceding rule may not be challenged, nor may a different one be claimed.

### § 2\. Of the Determination of Maternity

**Article 183:** Maternity is legally determined by childbirth, when the birth and the identities of the child and the woman who gave birth appear in the Civil Registry records.

In other cases, maternity is determined by recognition or final judgment in a filiation lawsuit, according to the provisions of the following articles.

### § 3\. Of the Determination of Matrimonial Filiation

**Article 184:** In the case of spouses of different sexes, children born after the celebration of the marriage and within three hundred days following its dissolution or the judicial separation of the spouses are presumed to be children of the husband.

This presumption shall not apply to those born before one hundred and eighty days have elapsed following the marriage, if the husband had no knowledge of the pregnancy at the time of marriage and judicially disclaims paternity. The action shall be exercised within the period and manner stated in articles 212 and following. However, the husband may not exercise it if, by positive acts, he has recognized the child after birth.

The presumption of paternity shall apply, however, with respect to the child born three hundred days after judicial separation is decreed, by the fact of the husband's name being recorded as the father, at the request of both spouses, in the child's birth registration.

If the woman successively contracts two marriages and gives birth to a child after the second is celebrated, the child shall be presumed to be the child of the current husband, whatever period may have elapsed since the dissolution of the first marriage, without prejudice to the right of the current husband to disclaim this paternity if the assumptions provided in the second paragraph apply. Once paternity is thus disclaimed, the husband of the previous marriage shall be presumed the father, provided the child was born within three hundred days following its dissolution.

Paternity thus determined or disclaimed may be challenged or claimed, respectively, according to the rules established in Title VIII.

**Article 185:** Matrimonial filiation is determined by the birth of the child during the marriage of its progenitors, provided that the maternity or paternity of both are legally established in accordance with articles 183 and 184, respectively.

In the case of the child born before its progenitors marry, matrimonial filiation is determined by the celebration of that marriage, provided that the maternity or paternity of both are already determined in accordance with article 186 or, otherwise, by the last recognition in accordance with the provisions of the following paragraph.

Matrimonial filiation may also be determined by a judgment rendered in a filiation lawsuit, which shall be sub-registered in the margin of the child's birth registration.

### § 4\. Of the Determination of Non-Matrimonial Filiation

**Article 186:** Non-matrimonial filiation is legally determined by the recognition of one of the progenitors, or of both, or by final judgment in a filiation lawsuit.

**Article 187:** The recognition of the child shall take place through a declaration made with that determined purpose by one of its progenitors or both, as the case may be:

1. Before the Civil Registry Officer, at the time of registering the child's birth or in the act of the marriage of the progenitors;  
2. In a record made at any time, before any Civil Registry Officer;  
3. In a public deed; or  
4. In a testamentary act.

If only one of the progenitors recognizes the child, they shall not be obliged to state the person with or from whom they had the child.

Recognition not recorded in the child's birth registration shall be sub-registered in its margin.

**Article 188:** The fact of recording the name of one of the progenitors, at the request of either of them, at the time of making the birth registration, is sufficient recognition of filiation.

**Article 189:** Recognition of a child who has a legally determined different filiation shall not produce effects, without prejudice to the right to exercise the actions referred to in article 208\.

Recognition is irrevocable, even if contained in a will revoked by a later testamentary act, and not susceptible to conditions.

Recognition shall not prejudice the rights of third parties in good faith acquired prior to the sub-registration of the recognition in the margin of the child's birth registration.

**Article 190:** Recognition by act between living persons indicated in article 187 may be made through an attorney constituted by public deed and specially empowered for this purpose.

**Article 191:** The child who, at the time of recognition, is of legal age, may repudiate it within one year from when they learned of it. If they are a minor, no one may repudiate it except the child and within one year from when, upon reaching legal age, they learned of the recognition.

The curator of the adult who is under interdiction for dementia or deaf-mutism shall need judicial authorization to repudiate.

The spendthrift under interdiction shall not need authorization from their legal representative or from the court to repudiate.

The repudiation shall be made by public deed, within the period indicated in this article. This deed shall be sub-registered in the margin of the child's birth registration.

Repudiation shall retroactively deprive the recognition of all effects that exclusively benefit the child or their descendants, but shall not alter rights already acquired by the parents or third parties, nor affect acts or contracts validly executed or entered into prior to the corresponding sub-registration.

Every repudiation is irrevocable.

**Article 192:** The child who, during their legal age, has accepted the recognition expressly or tacitly, may not repudiate it.

Acceptance is express when they assume the title of child in a public or private instrument, or in a judicial proceeding act.

It is tacit when they perform an act that necessarily implies the status of child and that they could not have performed except in that capacity.

**Article 193:** If the child being recognized is dead, or if the recognized minor dies before reaching legal age, their heirs may effect the repudiation within one year following the recognition, in the first case, or the death, in the second, subject to the provisions of the preceding articles.

If the recognized adult dies before the term for repudiation has expired, their heirs may effect the repudiation during the time that was lacking for them to complete said term.

**Article 194:** The repudiation of any of the recognitions that give rise to the matrimonial filiation of children born before their parents' marriage, granted in accordance with the preceding rules, shall prevent said filiation from being legally determined.

---

## TITLE VIII: OF FILIATION ACTIONS

### § 1\. General Rules

**Article 195:** The law enables the investigation of paternity or maternity, in the manner and with the means provided in the following articles.

The right to claim filiation is imprescriptible and inalienable. However, its patrimonial effects are subject to the general rules of limitation and waiver.

**Article 196:** Repealed.

**Article 197:** The proceeding shall be secret until a final judgment is rendered, and only the parties and their judicial attorneys shall have access to it.

The person who exercises a filiation action in bad faith or with the purpose of harming the honor of the defendant is obliged to indemnify the damages caused to the affected party.

**Article 198:** In lawsuits on the determination of filiation, maternity and paternity may be established through all kinds of evidence, decreed ex officio or at the request of a party.

Nevertheless, for these purposes, testimonial evidence alone shall be insufficient, and the requirements of article 1712 shall apply to presumptive evidence.

**Article 199:** Expert biological tests shall be conducted by the Legal Medical Service or by laboratories competent for this purpose, designated by the judge. The parties shall always, and only once, have the right to request a new biological expert report.

The judge may give these expert tests, alone, sufficient value to establish paternity or maternity, or to exclude it.

In any case, the judge shall obtain by the most expeditious possible means, before rendering judgment, the results of the expert tests performed that have not been reported to the court.

The unjustified refusal of one of the parties to undergo the examination shall create a legal presumption of paternity or maternity, or the absence thereof, as appropriate.

There shall be unjustified refusal if, after the party is cited twice, they do not attend the examination. For this purpose, citations shall be made under warning of the application of the presumption indicated in the preceding paragraph.

**Article 199 bis:** Once a filiation claim action is filed, if the defendant does not appear at the preparatory hearing or denies or expresses doubts about their paternity or maternity, the judge shall immediately order the performance of the biological expert test, which shall be notified personally or by any means that guarantees the defendant's proper information.

Judicial recognition of paternity or maternity shall be reduced to a record that shall be sub-registered in the margin of the child's birth registration, for which purpose the court shall send an authentic copy to the Civil Registry.

**Article 200:** Notorious possession of the status of child with respect to a determined person shall also serve for the judge to consider filiation sufficiently proven, provided it has lasted at least five continuous years and is proven by a set of witnesses and background information or faithful circumstances that establish it irrefutably.

Notorious possession consists of their father, mother, or both having treated them as a child, providing for their education and establishment in a competent manner, and presenting them in that character to their relatives and friends; and that these and the neighborhood of their domicile, in general, have reputed and recognized them as such.

**Article 201:** Notorious possession of the civil status of child, duly proven, shall prevail over biological expert evidence in case of contradiction between them.

However, if there are serious reasons that demonstrate the inconvenience for the child of applying the preceding rule, biological evidence shall prevail.

**Article 202:** The action to seek nullity of the act of recognition due to defects of consent shall expire within one year from the date of its granting or, in the case of force, from the day such force ceased.

**Article 203:** When filiation has been judicially determined against the opposition of the father or mother, the latter shall be deprived of parental authority and, in general, of all rights conferred by law with respect to the person and property of the child or their descendants. The judge shall so declare in the judgment and record thereof shall be made in the corresponding sub-registration.

The father or mother shall retain, however, all their legal obligations whose fulfillment benefits the child or their descendants.

However, the father or mother shall be restored to all the rights of which they are deprived, if the child, upon reaching full capacity, declares by public deed or by will their will to restore them. Restoration by public deed shall produce effects from its sub-registration in the margin of the child's birth registration and shall be irrevocable. Restoration by testamentary act shall produce effects from the death of the testator.

### § 2\. Of Claims Actions

**Article 204:** The action to claim matrimonial filiation belongs exclusively to the child, or to either of their progenitors.

In the case of children, the action must be filed jointly against both progenitors.

If the action is exercised by one of the progenitors, the other must necessarily intervene in the lawsuit, under penalty of nullity.

**Article 205:** The action to claim non-matrimonial filiation belongs only to the child against one of their progenitors, or to either of the progenitors when the child has a determined different filiation, for which they shall be subject to the provisions of article 208\.

The legal representative of the incapable child may also claim filiation, in the child's interest.

**Article 206:** If the child is posthumous, or if one of the progenitors dies within one hundred and eighty days following birth, the action may be brought against the heirs of the deceased progenitor, within three years from their death or, if the child is incapable, from when they reach full capacity.

**Article 207:** If the child dies while incapable, the action may be exercised by their heirs, within three years from the death.

If the child dies before three years have elapsed from reaching full capacity, the action shall belong to their heirs for the entire time lacking to complete said term.

The term or its residue shall begin to run for incapable heirs from when they reach full capacity.

**Article 208:** If a person's filiation is determined and they wish to claim a different one, the actions to challenge the existing filiation and to claim the new filiation must be exercised simultaneously.

In this case, the periods indicated in paragraph 3 of this Title shall not apply to the challenge action.

**Article 209:** Once filiation is judicially claimed, the judge may decree provisional maintenance in the terms of article 327\.

**Article 210:** The concubinage of the mother with the alleged father, during the period in which conception could have legally occurred, shall serve as a basis for a judicial presumption of paternity.

If the alleged father proves that the mother cohabited with another during the legal period of conception, this circumstance alone shall not suffice to dismiss the claim, but judgment cannot be rendered in the lawsuit without the other party being summoned.

### § 3\. Of Challenge Actions

**Article 211:** Filiation is rendered without effect by challenge of paternity or maternity in accordance with the following precepts.

**Article 212:** The paternity of a child conceived or born during the marriage may be challenged by the husband within one hundred and eighty days from the day he learned of the birth, or within one year from that same date, if he proves that at the time of birth he was de facto separated from the wife.

The husband's residence in the place of the child's birth shall create a presumption that he learned of it immediately; unless it is proven that the wife concealed the birth.

If at the time of birth the husband was absent, he shall be presumed to have learned of it immediately after his return to the wife's residence; except in the case of concealment mentioned in the preceding paragraph.

**Article 213:** If the husband dies without knowing of the birth, or before the term for challenge indicated in the preceding article has expired, the action shall belong to his heirs, and in general, to any person to whom the alleged paternity causes actual harm, for that same period, or the time lacking to complete it.

This right shall cease if the father recognized the child as his own in his will or in another public instrument.

**Article 214:** The paternity referred to in article 212 may also be challenged by the legal representative of the incapable child, in the child's interest, during the year following birth.

The child, personally, may file the challenge action within one year from reaching full capacity.

**Article 215:** In the challenge lawsuit of the paternity of the child of matrimonial filiation, the mother shall be summoned, but not obliged to appear.

**Article 216:** Paternity determined by recognition may be challenged by the child itself, within two years from when they learned of such recognition.

If the child is incapable, this action shall be exercised in accordance with the rules provided in article 214\.

If the child dies without knowing that act, or before the period for challenging paternity has expired, the action shall belong to their heirs for the same period or the time lacking to complete it, counted from the child's death.

All the foregoing shall also apply to challenge the paternity of children born before their parents' marriage, but the two-year period shall be counted from when the child learned of the marriage or the recognition that produces it.

Any person who proves a current interest in the matter may also challenge paternity determined by recognition, within one year from when they had that interest and could exercise their right.

**Article 217:** Maternity may be challenged by proving a false birth, or substitution of the alleged child for the real one.

The right to challenge it belongs, within the year following birth, to the husband of the alleged mother and the alleged mother herself.

The true father or mother of the child, the true child, or the one passing as such may also challenge it at any time, if the determination of the authentic filiation of the true or alleged child is claimed jointly.

If the action to challenge the maternity of the alleged child is not filed jointly with the claim action, it must be exercised within one year from when the child reaches full capacity.

Even if the periods established in this article have expired, in the case of unexpectedly coming to light some fact incompatible with putative maternity, the respective action may subsist or revive for one year from the justified revelation of the fact.

**Article 218:** The challenge action shall also be granted to any other person to whom apparent maternity currently causes harm to their rights over the testamentary or intestate succession of the alleged father or mother, provided there is no notorious possession of civil status.

This action shall expire within one year from the death of said father or mother.
