**Article 219:** None of those who have participated in the fraud of false birth or substitution shall benefit in any way from the discovery of the fraud, not even to exercise over the child the rights of parental authority, or to demand maintenance, or to succeed in their property by reason of death.

The judgment that sanctions the fraud or substitution shall expressly declare this deprivation of rights and shall be sub-registered in the margin of the child's birth registration.

**Article 220:** Challenge of a filiation determined by final judgment shall not proceed, without prejudice to the provisions of article 320\.

**Article 221:** The judgment that grants the claim or challenge action shall be sub-registered in the margin of the child's birth registration, and shall not prejudice the rights of third parties in good faith acquired prior to the sub-registration.

---

## TITLE IX: OF THE RIGHTS AND OBLIGATIONS BETWEEN PARENTS AND CHILDREN

**Article 222:** The fundamental concern of parents is the best interest of the child, for which they shall seek their greatest spiritual and material fulfillment possible, and shall guide them in the exercise of the essential rights emanating from human nature in a manner consistent with the evolution of their faculties.

Children owe respect and obedience to their parents.

**Article 223:** Although emancipation gives the child the right to act independently, they always remain obliged to care for their parents in their old age, in a state of dementia, and in all circumstances of life when they need their assistance.

All other ascendants have the right to the same assistance in case of absence or insufficiency of the immediate descendants.

**Article 224:** The personal care of the children belongs jointly to the parents, or to the surviving father or mother. This shall be based on the principle of co-responsibility, by virtue of which both parents, whether living together or separated, shall participate actively, equitably, and permanently in the upbringing and education of their children.

The personal care of the child not conceived nor born during the marriage, recognized by one of the parents, belongs to the father or mother who recognized them. If not recognized by either parent, the person who will have their care shall be determined by the judge.

**Article 225:** If parents live separately, they may determine by mutual agreement that the personal care of the children correspond to the father, the mother, or to both in a shared manner. The agreement shall be granted by public deed or record made before any Civil Registry Officer and shall be sub-registered in the margin of the child's birth registration within thirty days following its granting. This agreement shall establish the frequency and freedom with which the parent not having personal care shall maintain a direct and regular relationship with the children and may be revoked or modified by fulfilling the same formalities.

Shared personal care is a lifestyle regime that seeks to stimulate the co-responsibility of both separated parents in the upbringing and education of their common children, through a residence system that ensures their adequate stability and continuity.

In the absence of the agreement of the first paragraph, the children shall continue under the personal care of the parent with whom they are living.

In any of the cases established in this article, when circumstances require it and the best interest of the child makes it convenient, the judge may attribute the personal care of the child to the other parent, or vest it in only one of them, if by agreement there is some form of shared exercise. The foregoing shall be understood without prejudice to the provisions of article 226\.

In no case may the judge base their decision exclusively on the economic capacity of the parents.

Whenever the judge attributes the personal care of the child to one of the parents, they shall establish, ex officio or at the request of a party, in the same resolution, the frequency and freedom with which the other parent who does not have personal care shall maintain a direct and regular relationship with the children, considering their best interest, provided the criteria set forth in article 229 are met.

As long as a new sub-registration regarding personal care is not cancelled by another later one, any new agreement or resolution shall be unenforceable against third parties.

**Article 225-2:** In establishing the regime and exercise of personal care, the following criteria and circumstances shall be considered and weighed together:

a) The affective connection between the child and their parents, and other persons in their family environment. b) The aptitude of the parents to guarantee the child's well-being and the possibility of providing them with an adequate environment, according to their age. c) The contribution to the child's maintenance while under the personal care of the other parent, when able to do so. d) The attitude of each parent to cooperate with the other, to ensure maximum stability for the child and guarantee the direct and regular relationship, for which they shall especially consider the provisions of paragraph five of article 229\. e) The effective dedication each parent gave to the child before separation and, especially, that which they can continue to develop according to their possibilities. f) The opinion expressed by the child. g) The result of expert reports ordered to be conducted. h) The agreements of the parents before and during the respective lawsuit. i) The domicile of the parents. j) Any other relevant background information considering the best interest of the child.

In no case may the establishment of the personal care regime be based on race or ethnicity, nationality, sexual orientation, gender identity or expression, personal appearance, or any other category that results in discrimination.

**Article 226:** The judge may, in the case of physical or moral inability of both parents, entrust the personal care of the children to another competent person or persons, primarily ensuring the best interest of the child according to the criteria established in article 225-2.

In choosing these persons, the closest relatives shall be preferred and, especially, the ascendants, the spouse, or the civil partner of the parent, as appropriate.

**Article 227:** In the matters referred to in the preceding articles, the judge shall hear the children and the relatives.

The resolutions issued, once final, shall be sub-registered in the manner and within the period established in article 225\.

The judge may enforce in the manner established in article 543 of the Code of Civil Procedure, anyone condemned by a judicial resolution that becomes final, to surrender the child and does not do so or refuses to do so within the period determined for these purposes. The same enforcement shall apply to the one who retains the child's belongings and refuses to surrender them upon the judge's request.

**Article 228:** Repealed.

**Article 229:** The father or mother who does not have the personal care of the child shall have the right and duty to maintain a direct and regular relationship with the child, which shall be exercised with the frequency and freedom agreed directly with the person who has the child under their care according to the conventions referred to in paragraph one of article 225 or, in default thereof, with those the judge deems convenient for the child.

A direct and regular relationship is understood to be one that tends to maintain the family bond between the parent who does not exercise personal care and their child through periodic and stable contact.

To determine this regime, parents, or the judge as applicable, shall encourage a healthy and close relationship between the parent who does not exercise personal care and their child, ensuring the best interest of the latter, their right to be heard and the evolution of their faculties, and especially considering:

a) The age of the child. b) The affective connection between the child and their father or mother, as appropriate, and the relationship with their close relatives. c) The personal care regime for the child that has been agreed or determined. d) Any other relevant element considering the best interest of the child.

Whether the regime of direct and regular relationship is judicially decreed or in the approval of parents' agreements in these matters, the judge shall ensure the greatest participation and co-responsibility of the parents in the child's life, establishing conditions that foster a healthy and close relationship.

The father or mother who exercises the personal care of the child shall not obstruct the direct and regular relationship regime established in favor of the other parent, in accordance with the provisions of this article.

The exercise of this right shall be suspended or restricted when it manifestly harms the child's well-being, which the court shall declare in a reasoned decision.

**Article 229-2:** The child has the right to maintain a direct and regular relationship with their grandparents. In the absence of agreement, the judge shall establish the modality of this relationship considering the interest of the child, in accordance with the criteria of article 229\.

**Article 230:** The expenses of education, upbringing, and establishment of the children are the responsibility of the conjugal partnership, according to the rules that will be stated regarding it. If there is none, the parents shall contribute in proportion to their respective economic means.

In case of death of the father or mother, said expenses correspond to the survivor.

**Article 231:** If the child has their own property, the expenses of their establishment, and if necessary, those of their upbringing and education, may be taken from them, keeping the capital intact as far as possible.

**Article 232:** The obligation to feed the child who lacks property passes, in the absence or insufficiency of both parents, to their grandparents, from both lines jointly.

In case of insufficiency of one of the parents, the obligation indicated above shall pass first to the grandparents of the line of the parent who does not provide; and in subsidium of these to the grandparents of the other line.

**Article 233:** In case of disagreement among those obliged to contribute to the expenses of upbringing, education, and establishment of the child, this shall be determined according to their economic means by the judge, who may modify it from time to time according to circumstances that arise.

**Article 234:** Parents shall have the faculty to correct their children, ensuring that this does not harm their health or personal development. This faculty excludes all forms of physical and psychological abuse and shall, in any case, be exercised in accordance with the law and the Convention on the Rights of the Child.

If such harm is produced or is reasonably feared to occur, the judge, at the request of any person or ex officio, may decree one or more of the special precautionary measures of article 71 of Law No. 19,968, subject to the procedure provided in Paragraph One of Title IV of the same law, without prejudice to the sanctions that may apply for the infraction.

When necessary for the child's well-being, parents may request the court to determine on the child's future life for the time they deem most convenient, which shall not exceed the time remaining for the child to reach eighteen years of age.

The judge's resolutions may not be modified by the sole will of the parents.

**Article 235:** The provisions contained in the preceding article extend, in the absence, inability, or death of both parents, to any other person to whom the personal care of the child corresponds.

**Article 236:** Parents shall have the right and duty to educate their children, guiding them towards their full development in the different stages of their life.

**Article 237:** The right granted to parents by the preceding article shall cease with respect to children whose care has been entrusted to another person, who shall exercise it with the consent of the guardian or curator, if they themselves are not such.

**Article 238:** The rights granted to parents in the preceding articles may not be claimed over the child they have abandoned.

**Article 239:** The same deprivation of rights shall apply to parents who, through their moral inability, have given cause for the provision to separate the children from their side; unless this has been revoked later.

**Article 240:** If the abandoned child by their parents has been fed and raised by another person, and their parents wish to remove them from that person's power, they must be authorized by the judge to do so, and must previously pay the costs of their upbringing and education, as assessed by the judge.

The judge shall only grant authorization if they consider, for serious reasons, that it is convenient for the child.

**Article 241:** If the minor child away from home is in urgent need, in which they cannot be assisted by the father or mother who has their personal care, the latter's authorization shall be presumed for the provisions made to them, by any person, for maintenance, considering their social position.

The person who makes the provisions shall give notice thereof to the father or mother as soon as possible. Any voluntary omission in this regard shall cause the responsibility to cease.

What is said of the father or mother in the preceding paragraphs extends, as applicable, to the person who, by death or inability of the parents, is responsible for the child's maintenance.

**Article 242:** The judge's resolutions under the respects indicated in the preceding rules shall be revoked by the cessation of the cause that gave rise to them, and may also be modified or revoked, in all cases and at any time, if a just cause arises and the legal requirements are met.

In all cases, to adopt their resolutions, the judge shall consider, as a primary consideration, the best interest of the child, and shall duly consider their opinions, according to their age and maturity.

---

## TITLE X: OF PARENTAL AUTHORITY

### § 1\. General Rules

**Article 243:** Parental authority is the set of rights and duties that correspond to the father or mother over the property of their non-emancipated children.

Parental authority shall also be exercised over the eventual rights of the unborn child.

**Article 244:** Parental authority shall be exercised by the father or mother or both jointly, as agreed in an agreement signed by public deed or record made before any Civil Registry Officer, which shall be sub-registered in the margin of the child's birth registration within thirty days following its granting.

In the absence of agreement, the exercise of parental authority belongs jointly to the father and mother.

However, parents may act indistinctly in acts of mere conservation. For the rest of the acts, joint action shall be required. In case of disagreement of the parents, or when one of them is absent or impeded or unjustifiably refuses, judicial authorization shall be required.

In any case, when the child's interest makes it indispensable, at the request of one of the parents, the judge may entrust the exercise of parental authority to the father or mother who lacked it, or vest it in only one of the parents, if they exercised it jointly. Once the resolution is final, it shall be sub-registered within the same period indicated in the first paragraph.

In default of the father or mother who has parental authority, the rights and duties shall correspond to the other parent.

**Article 245:** If parents live separately, parental authority shall be exercised by the one who has the personal care of the child, or by both, in accordance with article 225\.

However, by agreement of the parents or judicial resolution based on the interest of the child, parental authority may be attributed to the other parent or vested in one of them if they exercised it jointly. Furthermore, based on the same interest, parents may exercise it jointly. The rules on sub-registration provided in the preceding article shall apply to the agreement or judicial resolution.

In the exercise of joint parental authority, the provisions of paragraph three of the preceding article shall apply.

**Article 246:** As long as a sub-registration regarding the exercise of parental authority is not cancelled by another later one, any new agreement or resolution shall be unenforceable against third parties.

**Article 247:** The property regime that may exist between parents shall not impede the rules provided in articles 244 and 245\.

**Article 248:** A guardian or curator shall be appointed for the child whenever paternity and maternity have been judicially determined against the opposition of both father and mother. The same shall apply to the child whose parents do not have the right to exercise parental authority or whose filiation is not legally determined with respect to either the father or the mother.

**Article 249:** The legal determination of paternity or maternity puts an end to the guardianship in which the minor child may be and gives the father or mother, as appropriate, parental authority over their property.

### § 2\. Of the Legal Right of Enjoyment over the Children's Property and Its Administration

**Article 250:** Parental authority confers the legal right of enjoyment over all the child's property, except for the following:

1. Property acquired by the child in the exercise of any employment, office, profession, or industry. The property covered by this number forms their professional or industrial special property.  
2. Property acquired by the child by way of gift, inheritance, or legacy, when the donor or testator has stipulated that the person exercising parental authority shall not have the enjoyment or administration; has imposed the condition of obtaining emancipation, or has expressly provided that the child shall have the enjoyment of this property, and  
3. Inheritances or legacies that have passed to the child due to incapacity, indignity, or disinheritance of the father or mother who has parental authority.

In these cases, the enjoyment shall correspond to the child or the other parent, in accordance with articles 251 and 253\.

The enjoyment over the child's mines shall be limited to half of the products and the parent exercising parental authority shall be liable to the child for the other half.

**Article 251:** The child shall be regarded as of legal age for the administration and enjoyment of their professional or industrial special property, without prejudice to the provisions of article 254\.

**Article 252:** The legal right of enjoyment is a personal right consisting of the faculty to use the child's property and receive its fruits, with the obligation to preserve the form and substance of said property and to restore them, if they are not fungible; or with the obligation to return an equal quantity and quality of the same kind, or to pay their value, if they are fungible.

The father or mother is not obliged, by reason of their legal right of enjoyment, to provide security for preservation or restitution, nor to make a solemn inventory, without prejudice to the provisions of article 124\. But if they do not make a solemn inventory, they shall keep a detailed description of the property from when they begin to enjoy it.

When this right corresponds to the married mother in a conjugal partnership, this shall be considered partially separated as to property with respect to its exercise and what is obtained from it. This separation shall be governed by the rules of article 150\.

If parental authority is exercised jointly by both parents and no other distribution has been agreed, the legal right of enjoyment shall be divided between them in equal parts.

The legal right of enjoyment is also called the legal usufruct of the father or mother over the child's property. To the extent appropriate to its nature, it shall be supplementarily governed by the rules of Title IX of Book II.

**Article 253:** One who exercises the legal right of enjoyment over the child's property shall have their administration, and one who is deprived of the latter shall also be deprived of the former.

If the father or mother who has parental authority cannot exercise the legal right of enjoyment over one or more of the child's property, this shall pass to the other; and if both are impeded, full ownership shall belong to the child and a curator shall be appointed for their administration.

**Article 254:** In no case may the child's real property, even if belonging to their professional or industrial special property, nor their hereditary rights, be alienated or encumbered without judicial authorization with knowledge of the case.

**Article 255:** No donation of any part of the child's property may be made, nor may it be leased for a long time, nor may an inheritance devolved to the child be accepted or repudiated, except in the manner and with the limitations imposed on guardians and curators.

**Article 256:** The father or mother is responsible, in the administration of the child's property, even for slight fault.

The responsibility towards the child extends to the property and the fruits, in those child's property in which they have administration but not enjoyment, and is limited to the property when they exercise both faculties over the property.

**Article 257:** There shall be a right to remove the administration of the child's property from the father or mother, or both, when they have been guilty of fraud, or of habitual gross negligence, and so established by judicial sentence, which shall be sub-registered in the margin of the child's birth registration.

They shall also lose the administration whenever parental authority is suspended, in accordance with article 267\.

**Article 258:** If one of the parents is deprived of the administration of the property, the other shall have it; if neither of them has it, full ownership shall belong to the child, and a curator shall be appointed for their administration.

**Article 259:** At the end of parental authority, parents shall inform their children of the administration they have exercised over their property.

### § 3\. Of the Legal Representation of Children

**Article 260:** The acts and contracts of the child not authorized by the father or mother who has them under parental authority, or by the joint curator, as applicable, shall bind the child only in their professional or industrial special property.

But they may not take money on interest, nor buy on credit (except in the ordinary course of said special property) without written authorization from the mentioned persons. And if they do so, they shall not be bound by these contracts, except to the extent of the benefit they derived from them.

**Article 261:** If there is a conjugal partnership between the parents, the acts and contracts the child enters into outside their professional or industrial special property and which the father or mother exercising parental authority authorizes or ratifies in writing, or those they perform in representation of the child, directly bind the father or mother in accordance with the provisions of that property regime and, subsidiarily, the child, to the extent of the benefit the child derived from said acts or contracts.

If there is no conjugal partnership, these acts and contracts only bind, in the manner indicated in the preceding paragraph, the father or mother who has intervened. The foregoing does not prevent the father or mother from seeking reimbursement from the other parent, to the extent they were legally required to provide for the child's needs.

**Article 262:** The adult minor does not need authorization from their parents to dispose of their property by testamentary act to take effect after their death, nor to recognize children.

**Article 263:** Whenever the child has to litigate as plaintiff against the father or mother who exercises parental authority, it shall be necessary to obtain the court's leave and the judge, upon granting it, shall appoint a curator for the lawsuit.

The father or mother who, having parental authority, litigates with the child, whether as plaintiff or defendant, shall provide them with expenses for the lawsuit, which the court shall regulate incidentally, considering the amount and importance of what is at issue and the economic capacity of the parties.

**Article 264:** The child may not appear in court, as plaintiff, against a third party, unless authorized or represented by the father or mother who exercises parental authority, or by both, if they exercise it jointly.

If the father, mother, or both refuse their consent to the child for the civil action they wish to bring against a third party, or if they are unable to give it, the judge may supply it, and in doing so shall appoint a curator for the lawsuit for the child.

**Article 265:** In civil actions against the child, the plaintiff must address the father or mother who has parental authority, to authorize or represent the child in the lawsuit. If both exercise parental authority jointly, it shall suffice to address one of them.

If the father or mother cannot or will not give their authorization or representation, the judge may supply it, and shall appoint a curator for the lawsuit for the child.

**Article 266:** Parental intervention shall not be necessary to proceed criminally against the child; but the father or mother who has parental authority shall be obliged to provide them with the necessary assistance for their defense.

### § 4\. Of the Suspension of Parental Authority

**Article 267:** Parental authority is suspended by the dementia of the father or mother who exercises it, by their minority, by being under interdiction to administer their property, and by their prolonged absence or other physical impediment, from which serious harm to the child's interests results, which the absent or impeded parent does not remedy.

In these cases, parental authority shall be exercised by the other parent, with respect to whom it shall be suspended for the same reasons. If suspended with respect to both, the child shall be subject to guardianship.

**Article 268:** The suspension of parental authority shall be decreed by the judge with knowledge of the case, and after hearing the child's relatives and the defender of minors; except when it concerns the minority of the father or mother, in which case suspension shall occur by operation of law.

The judge, in the child's interest, may decree that the father or mother regain parental authority when the cause that motivated the suspension has ceased.

The resolution that decrees or sets aside the suspension shall be sub-registered in the margin of the child's birth registration.

### § 5\. Of Emancipation

**Article 269:** Emancipation is an event that puts an end to the parental authority of the father, mother, or both, as the case may be. It may be legal or judicial.

**Article 270:** Legal emancipation occurs:

1. By the death of the father or mother, unless the other parent is to exercise parental authority;  
2. By the decree granting provisional possession, or definitive possession as applicable, of the property of the disappeared father or mother, unless the other parent is to exercise parental authority;  
3. Eliminated.  
4. By the child reaching the age of eighteen.

**Article 271:** Judicial emancipation is effected by judge's decree:

1. When the father or mother habitually mistreats the child, unless the other parent is to exercise parental authority;  
2. When the father or mother has abandoned the child, except in the exception case of the preceding number;  
3. When by final judgment the father or mother has been convicted of a crime deserving afflictive punishment, even if a pardon is granted for the sentence, unless, considering the nature of the crime, the judge considers there is no risk to the child's interest, or the other parent assumes parental authority, and  
4. In case of physical or moral inability of the father or mother, if the other parent is not to exercise parental authority.

The judicial resolution decreeing emancipation shall be sub-registered in the margin of the child's birth registration.

**Article 272:** Every emancipation, once effected, is irrevocable.

Excepted from this rule is emancipation by presumed death or by judicial sentence based on the parent's moral inability, which may be set aside by the judge, at the request of the respective parent, when their existence is reliably proven or that the inability has ceased, as applicable, and moreover it is shown that recovery of parental authority is in the child's interests. The judicial resolution that grants revocation shall only produce effects from its sub-registration in the margin of the child's birth registration. Revocation of emancipation may proceed only once.

**Article 273:** The emancipated minor child shall be subject to guardianship.

**Articles 274-296:** Repealed.

---

## TITLE XVI: OF THE ENABLING OF AGE

**Note:** Numbers 23 of Article 1 of Law 19585, published on 26.10.1998, repealed Titles VII to XV of Book One of this Code and in its No. 24 introduced Titles VII to X, as they appear in the text. As a consequence of the cited modifications, Titles XI to XV are repealed.

**Articles 297-303:** Repealed.

---

## TITLE XVII: OF THE EVIDENCE OF CIVIL STATUS

**Article 304:** Civil status is the quality of an individual, as it enables them to exercise certain rights or contract certain civil obligations.

**Article 305:** The civil status of married, judicially separated, divorced, or widowed, and of father, mother, or child, shall be accredited before third parties and proved by the respective marriage, death, and birth or baptism records.

The civil status of father, mother, or child shall also be accredited or proved by the corresponding registration or sub-registration of the act of recognition or the judicial ruling that determines filiation.

Age and death may be accredited or proved by the respective birth or baptism, and death records.

**Article 306:** The authenticity and purity of the aforementioned documents shall be presumed, provided they are in due form.

**Article 307:** The aforementioned documents may be rejected, even if their authenticity and purity are established, by proving non-identity of person, that is, the fact that the person to whom the document refers and the person to whom it is intended to apply are not the same.

**Article 308:** The aforementioned documents attest to the declaration made by the contracting parties of marriage, by the parents, godparents, or other persons in the respective cases, but do not guarantee the veracity of this declaration in any of its parts.

They may therefore be challenged by proving that the declaration on the point in question was false.

**Article 309:** The lack of a marriage record may be supplied by other authentic documents, by declarations of witnesses who witnessed the celebration of the marriage, and in default of these proofs, by notorious possession of that civil status.

Filiation, in the absence of a record or sub-registration, may only be accredited or proved by the authentic instruments by which it has been legally determined. In the absence of these, the status of father, mother, or child must be proved in the corresponding filiation lawsuit in the manner and with the means provided in Title VIII.

**Article 310:** Notorious possession of the civil status of marriage consists mainly in the alleged spouses having treated each other as such in their domestic and social relations; and in one of the spouses having been received in that character by the relatives and friends of the other, and by the neighborhood of their domicile in general.

**Article 311:** Repealed.

**Article 312:** For notorious possession of the civil status of marriage to be received as proof of civil status, it must have lasted at least ten continuous years.

**Article 313:** Notorious possession of the civil status of marriage shall be proved by a set of trustworthy witnesses, establishing it irrefutably; particularly in case the lack of the respective record, or the loss or misplacement of the book or register in which it should be found, is not explained and satisfactorily proved.

**Article 314:** When it is necessary to qualify the age of an individual, for the execution of acts or exercise of offices requiring a certain age, and it is not possible to do so by documents or declarations that establish the time of their birth, they shall be assigned a medium age between the oldest and youngest that appear compatible with the individual's development and physical appearance.

The judge, to establish age, shall hear the opinion of specialists, or of other competent persons.

**Article 315:** The judicial ruling pronounced in accordance with the provisions of Title VIII that declares the paternity or maternity of the child true or false, applies not only to the persons who have intervened in the lawsuit, but to all, relative to the effects that said paternity or maternity entails.

**Article 316:** For the rulings referred to in the preceding article to produce the effects therein designated, it is necessary:

1. That they have passed into the authority of res judicata;  
2. That they have been pronounced against a legitimate adversary;  
3. That there has been no collusion in the lawsuit.

**Article 317:** A legitimate adversary in the question of paternity is the father against the child, or the child against the father, and in the question of maternity the child against the mother, or the mother against the child.

The heirs of the deceased father or mother against whom the child may direct or continue the action are also legitimate adversaries, and also the heirs of the deceased child when they take charge of the action initiated by the child or decide to file it.

**Article 318:** The ruling pronounced in favor of or against any of the heirs benefits or harms the co-heirs who, summoned, did not appear.

**Article 319:** Proof of collusion in the lawsuit is only admissible within five years following the judgment.

**Article 320:** Neither limitation nor any ruling, pronounced between any other persons, may be opposed to one who appears as the true father or mother of the one who passes as the child of others, or as the true child of the father or mother who denies them.

The actions that correspond shall be exercised in accordance with the rules established in Title VIII and, as applicable, shall be notified to the persons who were parties in the previous proceeding for the determination of filiation.

---

## TITLE XVIII: OF MAINTENANCE OWED BY LAW TO CERTAIN PERSONS

**Article 321:** Maintenance is owed:

1. To the spouse;  
2. To descendants;  
3. To ascendants;  
4. To siblings; and  
5. To the one who made a substantial gift, if it has not been rescinded or revoked.

The donor's action shall be directed against the donee.

Maintenance is not owed to the persons designated here, in cases where an express law denies it to them.

**Article 322:** The general rules to which the provision of maintenance is subject are the following; without prejudice to the special provisions this Code contains regarding certain persons.

**Article 323:** Maintenance must enable the recipient to subsist adequately, safeguarding the best interest, progressive autonomy, and integral development of the child and adolescent.

They include the obligation to provide the recipient under twenty-one years of age with basic and secondary education, and education in some profession or trade. The maintenance granted according to article 332 to the descendant or sibling over twenty-one years of age shall also include the obligation to provide education in some profession or trade.

**Article 324:** In case of atrocious injury, the obligation to provide maintenance shall cease. But if the conduct of the maintenance recipient is mitigated by serious circumstances in the conduct of the provider, the judge may moderate the rigor of this provision.

Only the conduct described in article 968 constitutes atrocious injury.

The father or mother who has not paid judicially decreed maintenance, or who abandoned the child in childhood when filiation had to be established by judicial sentence against their opposition, shall be deprived of the right to demand maintenance from the child.

**Article 325:** Repealed.

**Article 326:** One who to request maintenance has several of the titles enumerated in article 321, may only use one of them, in the following order:

1. The one they have according to number 5\.  
2. The one they have according to number 1\.  
3. The one they have according to number 2\.  
4. The one they have according to number 3\.  
5. Number 4 shall only apply in the absence of all others.

Among several ascendants or descendants, recourse must be had to those of the closest degree. Among those of the same degree, as also among several obliged by the same title, the judge shall distribute the obligation in proportion to their means.

Having several maintenance recipients with respect to the same debtor, the judge shall distribute the maintenance in proportion to the needs of the recipients.

Only in the case of insufficiency of all obliged by the preferred title may recourse be had to another.

**Article 327:** While the obligation to provide maintenance is being litigated, the judge shall order it provisionally, with the sole merit of the documents and background information presented; without prejudice to restitution, if the person from whom it is demanded obtains an acquittal judgment.

This right to restitution ceases against one who, in good faith and with some plausible basis, has filed the claim.

**Article 328:** In case of fraud to obtain maintenance, all those who have participated in the fraud shall be jointly and severally obliged to restitution and damages.

**Article 329:** In assessing maintenance, the debtor's means and domestic circumstances shall always be taken into consideration.

**Article 330:** Maintenance is only owed to the extent that the recipient's means of subsistence are not sufficient for them to live in a manner corresponding to their social position.

**Article 331:** Maintenance is owed from the first demand, and shall be paid in advance monthly payments.

Restitution may not be claimed for that part of the advance payments that the recipient had not earned because they died.

**Article 332:** Maintenance owed by law is understood to be granted for the entire life of the recipient, continuing the circumstances that legitimized the claim.

However, maintenance granted to descendants and siblings shall be earned until they reach twenty-one years of age, except if they are studying a profession or trade, in which case it shall cease at twenty-eight years of age; if they are affected by a physical or mental disability that prevents them from supporting themselves, or if, for qualified circumstances, the judge considers it indispensable for their subsistence.

**Article 333:** The judge shall regulate the form and amount in which maintenance is to be provided, and may order that it be converted into the interest of a capital to be deposited for this purpose in a savings bank or other analogous establishment, and returned to the provider or their heirs when the obligation ceases.

**Article 334:** The right to demand maintenance may not be transmitted by reason of death, nor sold or assigned in any way, nor renounced.

**Article 335:** One who owes maintenance may not oppose to the claimant in compensation what the claimant owes them.

**Article 336:** Notwithstanding the provisions of the two preceding articles, overdue maintenance installments may be renounced or compensated; and the right to demand them may be transmitted by reason of death, sold, and assigned; without prejudice to the limitation that may apply to the debtor.

**Article 337:** The provisions of this title do not govern voluntary maintenance assignments made by will or by gift between living persons; with respect to which the will of the testator or donor shall be followed, to the extent they could freely dispose of their property.

---

## TITLE XIX: OF GUARDIANSHIPS AND CURATORSHIPS IN GENERAL

### § 1\. Definitions and General Rules

**Article 338:** Guardianships and curatorships are charges imposed on certain persons in favor of those who cannot direct themselves or competently administer their affairs, and who are not under the authority of a father or mother who can provide them with due protection.

The persons who exercise these charges are called guardians or curators and generally caregivers.

**Article 339:** The provisions of this title and the following two are subject to the modifications and exceptions that will be expressed in the special titles of guardianship and each kind of curatorship.

**Article 340:** Guardianship and general curatorships extend not only to the property but to the person of the individuals subject to them.

**Article 341:** Pubescents are subject to guardianship.

**Article 342:** The following are subject to general curatorship: adult minors; those who, for prodigality or dementia, have been placed under interdiction to administer their property; and the deaf or deaf-mutes who cannot make themselves clearly understood.

**Article 343:** Those appointed for the property of the absentee, the vacant estate, and the eventual rights of the unborn are called curators of property.

**Article 344:** Those appointed in certain cases to persons who are under the authority of father or mother, or under guardianship or general curatorship, to exercise a separate administration, are called joint curators.

**Article 345:** A special curator is one appointed for a particular matter.

**Article 346:** Individuals subject to guardianship or curatorship are called wards.

**Article 347:** Two or more individuals may be placed under the same guardianship or curatorship, provided there is indivision of patrimonies. Once the patrimonies are divided, there shall be considered as many guardianships or curatorships as there are distinct patrimonies, even if exercised by the same person.

The same guardianship or curatorship may be exercised jointly by two or more guardians or curators.

**Article 348:** No guardian or general curator may be appointed for one who is under parental authority, except when it is suspended in one of the cases enumerated in article 267\.

A joint curator shall be appointed for the child when the father or mother are deprived of the administration of the child's property or part of it, according to article 251\.

**Article 349:** A curator shall be appointed for spouses in the same cases in which, if single, they would need a curator for the administration of their property.

**Article 350:** Generally, no guardian or curator may be appointed for one who already has one: only a joint curator may be appointed, in the cases designated by law.

**Article 351:** If the guardian or curator, alleging the excessive complexity of the ward's affairs and their insufficiency to fully administer them, requests that a curator be added, the judge may grant it, having heard the ward's relatives and the respective defender.

The judge shall then divide the administration in the manner deemed most convenient.

**Article 352:** If a gift, inheritance, or legacy is made to one who is under guardianship or curatorship, with the precise condition that the property included in the gift, inheritance, or legacy be administered by a person designated by the donor or testator, the wishes of the latter shall be followed; unless, after hearing the relatives and the respective defender, it appears more convenient for the ward to repudiate the gift, inheritance, or legacy than to accept it on those terms.

If the gift, inheritance, or legacy is accepted, and the donor or testator has not designated the person, or the designated person is not competent, the magistrate shall make the designation.

**Article 353:** Guardianships or curatorships may be testamentary, legitimate, or dativa.

Testamentary are those constituted by testamentary act.

Legitimate are those conferred by law on the relatives or spouse of the ward.

Dativa are those conferred by the magistrate.

The rules of testamentary guardianship are followed by that conferred by act between living persons, according to article 360\.

### § 2\. Of Testamentary Guardianship or Curatorship

**Article 354:** The father or mother may appoint a guardian, by will, not only for born children, but for the one still in the mother's womb, in case they are born alive.

**Article 355:** They may also appoint a curator, by will, for adult minors; and for adults of any age who are in a state of dementia, or are deaf or deaf-mutes who do not understand or make themselves clearly understood.

**Article 356:** They may also appoint a curator, by will, for the defense of the eventual rights of the unborn child.

**Article 357:** The father or mother who has been deprived of parental authority by judge's decree, according to article 271, or who by bad administration has been judicially removed from the child's guardianship, shall lack the rights conferred by the preceding articles.

The father or mother shall also lack these rights when filiation has been judicially determined against their opposition.

**Article 358:** If both the father and mother have appointed a guardian by will, the appointment made by the parent who exercised the child's parental authority shall be considered first.

**Article 359:** If it is not possible to apply the rule of the preceding article, the rules of articles 361 and 363 shall apply to the guardians appointed by the will of the father and mother.

**Article 360:** Notwithstanding the provisions of article 357, the father, mother, and any other person, may appoint a curator, by will or by act between living persons, when they give or leave to the ward any part of their property, which is not owed to them by way of legitimate share.

This curatorship shall be limited to the property given or left to the ward.

**Article 361:** Two or more guardians or curators may be appointed by will to exercise the guardianship simultaneously; and the testator shall have the faculty to divide the administration among them.

**Article 362:** If there are several wards, and the testator divides them among the appointed guardians or curators, all of them shall exercise the guardianship or curatorship jointly, as long as the patrimony remains undivided; and once the patrimony is divided, the guardianship shall be divided among them by the same fact, and they shall be independent from each other.

But the care of the person of each ward shall belong exclusively to their respective guardian or curator, even during the indivision of the patrimony.

**Article 363:** If the testator appoints several guardians or curators to exercise guardianship or curatorship jointly, and does not divide the functions among them, the judge, after hearing the ward's relatives, may entrust them to one of the appointed persons or to the number they deem sufficient, and in this second case, divide it as best suits the security of the ward's interests.

**Article 364:** Several guardians or curators may also be appointed by will to substitute or succeed one another; and once the substitution or succession is established for a particular case, it shall apply to the others in which the guardian or curator fails; unless it manifestly appears that the testator intended to limit the substitution or succession to the designated case or cases.

**Article 365:** Testamentary guardianships and curatorships admit suspensive and resolutory conditions, and designation of a certain day on which they begin or expire.

### § 3\. Of Legitimate Guardianship or Curatorship

**Article 366:** Legitimate guardianship takes place when testamentary guardianship is lacking or expires.

It takes place especially when the minor is emancipated, and when parental authority is suspended by judge's decree.

**Article 367:** Those called to legitimate guardianship or curatorship are, in general:

First, the father of the ward; Second, the mother; Third, other ascendants of both sexes; Fourth, the siblings of both sexes of the ward, and the siblings of both sexes of the ward's ascendants.

If there is no place for the guardianship or curatorship of the father or mother, the judge, after hearing the ward's relatives, shall choose among the other ascendants, and in the absence of ascendants, among the collaterals here designated, the person deemed most suitable, and who presents the best guarantees; and they may also, if deemed convenient, choose more than one and divide the functions among them.

**Article 368:** The father or mother who first recognized the child not conceived nor born during marriage is called to the legitimate guardianship of the child, and if both recognized at the same time, the father.

This call shall put an end to the guardianship in which the recognized child was, except in case of inability or legitimate excuse of the one who, according to the preceding paragraph, is called to exercise it.

If filiation has not been determined or if filiation has been established judicially against the opposition of the father or mother, the child's guardianship shall be dativa.

**Article 369:** If during the pupil's status the legitimate caregiver ceases in their charge, they shall be replaced by another of the same kind.

### § 4\. Of Dativa Guardianship or Curatorship

**Article 370:** In the absence of another guardianship or curatorship, dativa guardianship applies.

**Article 371:** When for any cause the discernment of a guardianship or curatorship is delayed, or during it an impediment arises that for some time prevents the guardian or curator from continuing to exercise it, an interim guardian or curator shall be appointed by the magistrate, for as long as the delay or impediment lasts.

But if there is another guardian or curator who can supply the lack, or if it concerns appointing a guardian or curator to succeed the one currently performing the guardianship or curatorship, and they can continue in it for some time, the appointment of the interim shall not take place.

**Article 372:** The magistrate, for the election of the dativa guardian or curator, shall hear the ward's relatives, and may, if necessary, appoint two or more, and divide the functions among them, as in the case of article 363\.

If there is a joint curator, the judge may prefer them for the dativa guardianship or curatorship.

---

## TITLE XX: OF THE DILIGENCES AND FORMALITIES THAT MUST PRECEDE THE EXERCISE OF GUARDIANSHIP OR CURATORSHIP

**Article 373:** Every guardianship or curatorship must be discerned. Discernment is the judicial decree that authorizes the guardian or curator to exercise their office.

**Article 374:** To discern the guardianship or curatorship, it shall be necessary that the granting of the surety or security to which the guardian or curator is obliged precedes. Nor shall they be given the administration of the property, without a solemn inventory preceding.

**Article 375:** All guardians or curators are obliged to give surety, except only:

1. The spouse and the ascendants and descendants;  
2. The interim, called for a short time to serve the office;  
3. Those appointed for a particular matter, without administration of property.

The guardian or curator who is a person of known probity and of sufficient means to be liable for them may also be relieved of the surety, when the ward has few property.

**Article 376:** In place of the surety provided in the preceding article, sufficient pledge or mortgage may be given.

**Article 377:** The acts of the guardian or curator prior to discernment are null; but discernment, once granted, shall validate the previous acts, whose delay could have caused harm to the ward.

**Article 378:** The guardian or curator is obliged to inventory the ward's property within ninety days following discernment, and before taking any part in the administration, except insofar as absolutely necessary.

The judge, according to circumstances, may restrict or extend this period.

For the guardian's negligence in proceeding to the inventory and for any serious fault that may be imputed to them in it, they may be removed from the guardianship or curatorship as suspect, and shall be condemned to reparation of any loss or damage resulting to the ward, in the manner provided in article 423\.

**Article 379:** The testator may not exempt the guardian or curator from the obligation to make an inventory.

**Article 380:** If the guardian or curator proves that the property is too small to bear the cost of preparing the inventory, the judge, after hearing the ward's relatives and the defender of minors, may waive the obligation to solemnly inventory said property, and require only a private note, under the signatures of the guardian or curator, and of three of the closest relatives, of legal age, or other respectable persons in their absence.

**Article 381:** The inventory shall be made before a notary and witnesses in the form prescribed in the Code of Procedure.

**Article 382:** The inventory shall include all the real and movable property of the person whose estate is being inventoried, particularizing them one by one, or indicating collectively those consisting of number, weight, or measure, with expression of quantity and quality; without prejudice to making the necessary explanations to protect the guardian's responsibility.

It shall also include property titles, public and private deeds, credits and debts of the ward of which there is proof or even notice, business or account books, and in general all present objects, except those known to be of no value or utility, or that it is necessary to destroy for some moral purpose.

**Article 383:** If after the inventory is made, property is found of which there was no notice when making it, or by any title new property increases the inventoried estate, a solemn inventory of them shall be made and added to the previous one.

**Article 384:** The inventory must include even things not belonging to the person whose estate is being inventoried, if found among those that do; and the guardian's responsibility shall extend to both.

**Article 385:** The mere assertion made in the inventory that the objects enumerated belong to determined persons does not constitute proof as to their true ownership.

**Article 386:** If the guardian or curator alleges that by error things that did not exist have been listed in the inventory, or the number, weight, or measure of existing ones has been exaggerated, or they have been attributed a material or quality they lacked, this exception shall not avail them; except that they prove the error could not have been avoided with due care on their part, or without scientific knowledge or experiments.

**Article 387:** The guardian or curator who alleges having knowingly placed in the inventory things that were not really delivered to them shall not be heard, even if they offer to prove they had some beneficial purpose for the ward.

**Article 388:** Obscure or doubtful passages of the inventory shall be interpreted in favor of the ward, unless proved otherwise.

**Article 389:** The guardian or curator who succeeds another shall receive the property by the previous inventory and shall note the differences in it. This operation shall be done with the same formalities as the previous inventory, which shall thus become the successor's inventory.

---

## TITLE XXI: OF THE ADMINISTRATION OF GUARDIANS AND CURATORS RELATIVE TO PROPERTY

**Article 390:** It is the guardian's or curator's role to represent or authorize the ward in all judicial or extrajudicial acts that concern them and may impair their rights or impose obligations on them.

**Article 391:** The guardian or curator administers the ward's property, and is obliged to preserve this property and to repair and cultivate it. Their responsibility extends to slight fault inclusive.

**Article 392:** If in the will a person is appointed whom the guardian must consult in the exercise of their office, the guardian shall not be obliged to submit to the consultant's opinion; nor by doing so shall their responsibility cease.

If in the will it is expressly ordered that the guardian proceed in agreement with the consultant, the guardian's responsibility shall not cease for acceding to the consultant's opinion; but if there is disagreement between them, the guardian shall not proceed except with authorization from the judge, which must be granted with knowledge of the case.

**Article 393:** It shall not be lawful for the guardian or curator, without prior judicial decree, to alienate the ward's real property, nor to encumber it with mortgage, ground rent, or servitude, nor to alienate or pledge precious movable property or those with sentimental value; nor may the judge authorize these acts, except for necessary or manifestly useful cause.

**Article 394:** The sale of any part of the ward's property enumerated in the preceding articles shall be made at public auction.

**Article 395:** Notwithstanding the provision of article 393, if there has been a prior decree of execution and attachment on the ward's real property, no new decree shall be necessary for its alienation.

Nor shall judicial decree be necessary for the constitution of a mortgage, ground rent, or servitude on real property transferred to the ward with the burden of constituting such mortgage, ground rent, or servitude.

**Article 396:** Without prior judicial decree, the guardian or curator may not proceed to the division of real or hereditary property the ward possesses with others in common.

If the judge, at the request of a co-owner or co-heir, has decreed the division, no new decree shall be necessary.

**Article 397:** The guardian or curator may not repudiate any inheritance devolved to the ward, without a judge's decree with knowledge of the case, nor accept it without benefit of inventory.

**Article 398:** Gifts or legacies may not be repudiated except in accordance with the provisions of article 1236; and if they impose obligations or burdens on the ward, they may not be accepted without prior appraisal of the gifted or bequeathed property.

**Article 399:** Once a division of an inheritance or of real property the ward possesses with others in common is made, a new judge's decree, which with hearing of the respective defender approves and confirms it, shall be necessary for it to have effect.

**Article 400:** Prior decree is also necessary to proceed to transactions or compromises on the ward's rights valued at more than one centavo, and on their real property; and in each case the transaction or the arbitrator's award shall be subject to judicial approval, under penalty of nullity.

**Article 401:** Money left or given to the ward for the acquisition of real property may not be destined for any other object that prevents or hampers it; except by judicial authorization with knowledge of the case.

**Article 402:** Donation of the ward's real property is prohibited, even with prior judicial decree.

Only with prior judicial decree may donations be made in money or other movable property of the ward; and the judge shall not authorize them except for serious cause, such as assisting a needy relative, contributing to an object of public charity, or other similar, and provided they are proportionate to the ward's means, and that productive capital does not suffer notable impairment.

Small expenses for charitable objects, or lawful recreation, are not subject to the preceding prohibition.

**Article 403:** The gratuitous remission of a right is subject to the rules of donation.

**Article 404:** The ward is incapable of being bound as a surety without prior judicial decree, which shall only authorize this surety in favor of a spouse, an ascendant or descendant, and for urgent and serious cause.

**Article 405:** The ward's debtors who pay the guardian or curator are freed from any new payment.

**Article 406:** The guardian or curator shall lend the ward's idle money with the best securities, at the current interest obtainable in the market. They may, if deemed preferable, invest it in the acquisition of real property.

For omission in this matter, they shall be responsible for lost profits, insofar as it appears that the ward's idle money could have been employed with manifest usefulness and without danger.

**Article 407:** The guardian or curator may not lease any part of the ward's rural estates for more than eight years, nor urban ones for more than five, nor for more years than the ward lacks to reach eighteen.

If they do so, the lease shall not be binding on the ward or on the one who succeeds in the ownership of the estate, for the time exceeding the limits here established.

**Article 408:** The guardian or curator shall take care to collect what is owed to the ward, immediately when payment is demandable, and to pursue the debtors by legal means.

**Article 409:** The guardian or curator shall take special care to interrupt the limitations that may run against the ward.

**Article 410:** The guardian or curator may cover with the ward's monies the advances made for the ward's benefit, charging current market interest, but for this they must be authorized by the other general guardians or curators of the same ward, if any, or by the judge in subsidium.

If the ward owes them any kind of property, real or movable, by way of legacy, trust, or any other, it shall be necessary that possession of it be given to the guardian or curator by the other general guardians or curators, or by the judge in subsidium.

**Article 411:** In all acts and contracts the guardian or curator executes or enters into in representation of the ward, they shall express this circumstance in the deed of the same act or contract; under penalty that if this expression is omitted, the act shall be deemed executed or the contract entered into in representation of the ward, if it is useful to the ward, and not otherwise.

**Article 412:** As a general rule, no act or contract in which the guardian or curator, or their spouse, or any of their ascendants or descendants, or their siblings, or their consanguineous or affine relatives up to the fourth degree inclusive, or any of their business partners, has a direct or indirect interest, may be executed or entered into without authorization from the other general guardians or curators, who are not implicated in the same way, or by the judge in subsidium.

But not even in this way may the guardian or curator buy the ward's real property, or take it in lease; and this prohibition extends to their spouse, and their ascendants or descendants.

**Article 413:** When there are many general guardians or curators, all of them shall jointly authorize the ward's acts and contracts; but in matters that, due to the administration having been divided, are especially in charge of one of said guardians or curators, the intervention or authorization of that one alone shall suffice.

It shall be understood that guardians or curators act jointly, when one of them does so in the name of the others, by virtue of a mandate in form; but the joint liability of the mandators shall subsist in this case.

In case of discord among them, the judge shall decide.

**Article 414:** The guardian or curator has the right to be reimbursed for expenses incurred in the exercise of their office: in case of legitimate claim, the judge shall have them assessed.

**Article 415:** The guardian or curator is obliged to keep a faithful, accurate, and as far as possible documented account of all their administrative acts, day by day; to exhibit it when their administration ends; to restore the property to whom it belongs; and to pay the balance that results against them.

This obligation includes every guardian or curator, even the testamentary one, notwithstanding that the testator has exempted them from rendering any account, or has anticipatorily remitted the balance; and even if the ward has no other property than that of the testator's succession, and even if it is left under the precise condition of not demanding the account or the balance. Such a condition shall be considered as not written.

**Article 416:** The judge may order ex officio, when deemed convenient, that the guardian or curator, even during their office, exhibit the accounts of their administration or show the existing property to another of the guardians or curators of the same ward, or to a special curator, whom the judge shall designate for the purpose.

This provision may be requested, with serious cause, qualified by the judge verbally, by any other guardian or curator of the same ward, or by any of the closest relatives of the ward, or their spouse, or the respective defender.

**Article 417:** Once their office expires, the guardian shall proceed to deliver the property as soon as possible; without prejudice to executing in the intervening time those acts that would otherwise be delayed to the ward's detriment.

**Article 418:** If there are many guardians who administer jointly, at the expiration of their office they shall present a single account; but if the administration has been divided among them, one account shall be presented for each separate administration.

**Article 419:** The responsibility of guardians and curators who administer jointly is joint and several; but if the administration is divided among them, whether by the testator, or by disposition or with approval of the judge, each shall be responsible directly for their own acts, and subsidiarily for the acts of the other guardians or curators, insofar as exercising the right granted by article 416, paragraph 2, they could have prevented the faulty administration of the other guardians or curators.

This subsidiary responsibility extends even to general guardians or curators who do not administer.

General guardians or curators are subject to the same subsidiary responsibility for the faulty administration of joint curators.

**Article 420:** The subsidiary responsibility prescribed in the preceding article does not extend to guardians or curators who, when the administration is divided by the testator's disposition, or with the judge's authority, administer in different communes.

**Article 421:** The responsibility of guardians or curators is joint and several when they divide the administration among themselves only by private agreement.

**Article 422:** Once the account is presented by the guardian or curator, it shall be discussed by the person to whom the administration of the property passes.

If the administration is transferred to another guardian or curator, the account shall not be closed except with judicial approval, after hearing the respective defender.

**Article 423:** Against the guardian or curator who does not give a true account of their administration, exhibiting at the same time the inventory and the existing property, or who in their administration is convicted of fraud or gross fault, the ward shall have the right to appraise and swear to the amount of the damage received, including lost profits; and the guardian or curator shall be condemned in the amount appraised and sworn; unless the judge has seen fit to moderate it.

**Article 424:** The guardian or curator shall pay current interest on the balance resulting against them, from the day their account is closed or there has been default in exhibiting it; and shall collect in turn interest on the balance in their favor, from the day they request it once their account is closed.

**Article 425:** Every action of the ward against the guardian or curator by reason of the guardianship or curatorship shall prescribe in four years, counted from the day the ward left the pupil status.

If the ward dies before the four-year period is completed, said action shall prescribe in the time lacking to complete it.

**Article 426:** One who exercises the office of guardian or curator, not truly being one, but believing themselves to be, has all the obligations and responsibilities of the true guardian or curator, and their acts shall not bind the ward, except insofar as they have brought positive advantage.

If guardianship or curatorship has been discerned to them, and they have administered rightly, they shall have the right to ordinary remuneration, and the office may be conferred on them, if no person with a better right to exercise it presents themselves.

But if they have proceeded in bad faith, feigning to be guardian or curator, they shall be precisely removed from the administration, and deprived of all emoluments of guardianship or curatorship, without prejudice to the penalty for the imposture.

**Article 427:** One who, in case of need, and to protect the ward, takes the administration of the ward's property, shall immediately apply to the judge to provide for the guardianship or curatorship, and in the meantime shall proceed as an officious agent and shall only have the obligations and rights of such. Any voluntary delay in applying to the judge shall make them responsible even for the slightest fault.

---

## TITLE XXII: SPECIAL RULES RELATING TO GUARDIANSHIP

**Article 428:** Regarding the upbringing and education of the ward, the guardian is obliged to conform to the will of the person or persons in charge of them, according to the provisions in Title IX, without prejudice to applying to the judge when deemed convenient.

But the father or mother who exercise guardianship shall not be obliged to consult any person on this matter.

**Article 429:** The guardian, in case of negligence of the person or persons in charge of the upbringing and education of the ward, shall strive by all prudent means to make them fulfill their duty, and if necessary shall apply to the judge.

**Article 430:** The ward shall not reside in the habitation or under the personal care of any of those who, if they died, would succeed to their property. Ascendants are not subject to this exclusion.

**Article 431:** When the parents have not provided by will for the upbringing and education of the ward, the guardian shall provide what is necessary for these purposes, according to the social rank of the family; taking it from the ward's property, and as far as possible, from the fruits.

The guardian shall be responsible for any immoderate expense in the upbringing and education of the ward, even if taken from the fruits. To cover their responsibility, they may ask the judge to fix, in view of the ward's means, the maximum amount to be invested in their upbringing and education.

**Article 432:** If the fruits of the ward's property are not sufficient for their moderate support and necessary education, the guardian may alienate or encumber part of the property, not contracting loans nor touching real property or productive capital except by extreme necessity and with due authorization.

**Article 433:** In case of indigence of the ward, the guardian shall resort to the persons who, by their relationship with the ward, are obliged to provide maintenance, summoning them judicially if necessary, to do so.

**Article 434:** The guardian's continued negligence in providing for the ward's support and education is sufficient cause for their removal from guardianship.

---

## TITLE XXIII: SPECIAL RULES RELATING TO THE CURATORSHIP OF THE MINOR

**Article 435:** The curatorship of the minor dealt with in this title is that to which, by reason of age alone, the emancipated adult is subject.

**Article 436:** Upon the minor reaching puberty, their guardian shall enter upon the curatorship by the sole ministry of law.

Consequently, it shall not be necessary to discern the office to them, nor for them to give new securities, nor to make an inventory. The accounts of guardianship and curatorship shall be rendered jointly.

**Article 437:** The adult minor who lacks a curator must request one from the judge, designating the person who shall be. If the minor does not request it, the relatives may do so; but the designation of the person shall always belong to the minor, or to the judge in subsidium.

The judge, hearing the defender of minors, shall accept the person designated by the minor, if competent.

**Article 438:** The curator may exercise, regarding the upbringing and education of the minor, the faculties conferred on the guardian concerning the pubescent in the preceding title.

**Article 439:** The minor who is under curatorship shall have the same administrative faculties as the child subject to parental authority, with respect to property acquired by them in the exercise of an employment, office, profession, or industry.

The provisions of article 260 apply to the minor and the curator.

**Article 440:** The curator represents the minor, in the same way the guardian represents the pubescent.

The curator may, nevertheless, if deemed convenient, entrust to the ward the administration of any part of the pupil property; but shall authorize under their responsibility the ward's acts in this administration.

Authorization shall be presumed for all ordinary acts incidental to it.

The curator shall also exercise, by operation of law, the guardianship or curatorship of the children under the ward's parental authority.

**Article 441:** The ward shall have the right to request the intervention of the defender of minors when any of the curator's acts causes manifest harm; and the defender, finding the complaint well-founded, shall apply to the judge.

---

## TITLE XXIV: SPECIAL RULES RELATING TO THE CURATORSHIP OF THE SPENDTHRIFT

**Article 442:** Those who, as prodigals or spendthrifts, have been placed under interdiction to administer their property, shall be given a legitimate curator, and in default of such, a dativa curator.

This curatorship may be testamentary in the case of article 451\.

**Article 443:** The interdiction proceeding may be provoked by the spouse not judicially separated from the alleged spendthrift, by any of their consanguineous relatives up to the fourth degree, and by the public defender.

The public defender shall be heard even in cases where the interdiction proceeding has not been provoked by them.

**Article 444:** If the alleged spendthrift is a foreigner, the proceeding may also be provoked by the competent diplomatic or consular official.

**Article 445:** Prodigality shall be proved by repeated acts of waste that manifest a total lack of prudence.

Habitual gambling in which considerable portions of the patrimony are risked, substantial donations without adequate cause, ruinous expenses, authorize interdiction.

**Article 446:** While the cause is being decided, the judge may, by virtue of verbal reports of the relatives or other persons, and after hearing the explanations of the alleged spendthrift, decree provisional interdiction.

**Article 447:** Decrees of provisional or definitive interdiction shall be registered in the Registrar's Registry and notified to the public by means of three notices published in a newspaper of the commune, or of the capital of the province or the capital of the region, if there is none in the commune.

The registration and notification shall be limited to stating that such individual, designated by name, surname, and domicile, does not have the free administration of their property.

**Article 448:** The curatorship shall be granted:

1. To ascendants, but the father or mother whose paternity or maternity has been judicially determined against their opposition or who is married to a third party may not exercise this office;  
2. To siblings, and  
3. To other collaterals up to the fourth degree.

The judge shall have freedom to choose in each class designated in the preceding numbers the person or persons deemed most suitable.

In the absence of the aforementioned persons, dativa curatorship shall apply.

**Article 449:** The curator of the spendthrift husband shall administer the conjugal partnership insofar as it subsists and shall exercise by operation of law the guardianship of the children in case the mother, for any reason, does not exercise parental authority.

The curator of the spendthrift wife shall also exercise, and in the same manner, the guardianship or curatorship of the children who are under her parental authority, when this does not correspond to the father.

**Article 450:** No spouse may be curator of the other declared spendthrift. The married wife in a conjugal partnership whose spendthrift husband is subject to curatorship, if she is over eighteen years of age or reaches that age after interdiction, shall have the right to request separation of property.

**Article 451:** The father or mother who exercises the curatorship of the spendthrift child may appoint by will the person who, at their death, shall succeed them in the guardianship.

**Article 452:** The spendthrift shall have the right to request the intervention of the public ministry when the curator's acts are vexatious or harmful to them; and the curator shall then conform to what is agreed by the public ministry.

**Article 453:** The spendthrift shall always retain their freedom, and shall have for their personal expenses the free disposal of a sum of money, proportionate to their means, and fixed by the judge.

Only in extreme cases may the curator be authorized to provide personally for the spendthrift's subsistence, procuring the necessary objects for them.

**Article 454:** The spendthrift shall be rehabilitated for the administration of their property, if it is judged that they can exercise it without inconvenience; and once rehabilitated, interdiction may be renewed if cause arises.

**Article 455:** The provisions indicated in the preceding article shall be decreed by the judge with the same formalities as for primitive interdiction; and shall be followed by the registration and notification provided in article 447; which in the case of rehabilitation shall be limited to stating that such individual (designated by name, surname, and domicile) has the free administration of their property.

---

## TITLE XXV: SPECIAL RULES RELATING TO THE CURATORSHIP OF THE DEMENTED PERSON

**Article 456:** The adult who is in a habitual state of dementia shall be deprived of the administration of their property, even if they have lucid intervals.

The curatorship of the demented person may be testamentary, legitimate, or dativa.

**Article 457:** When the demented child has reached puberty, the head of the family may continue to care for their person and property until the age of majority; upon reaching which they must precisely provoke the interdiction proceeding.

**Article 458:** The guardian of the demented ward may not subsequently exercise the curatorship without previous judicial interdiction, except for the time necessary to provoke the interdiction.

The same shall be necessary when dementia befalls the minor who is under curatorship.

**Article 459:** The interdiction of the demented person may be provoked by the same persons who may provoke that of the spendthrift.

The curator of the minor to whom dementia occurs during the curatorship must provoke it.
