**Article 906:** The possessor in bad faith is responsible for the deteriorations the thing has suffered by their act or fault.

The possessor in good faith, as long as they remain in it, is not responsible for these deteriorations, except insofar as they have taken advantage of them; for example, by destroying a forest or grove, and selling the wood or firewood, or using it for their own benefit.

**Article 907:** The possessor in bad faith is obliged to restore the natural and civil fruits of the thing, and not only those perceived but those the owner could have perceived with medium intelligence and activity, having the thing in their power.

If the fruits do not exist, they shall owe the value they had or would have had at the time of perception: those that have deteriorated in their power shall be considered non-existent.

The possessor in good faith is not obliged to restore fruits perceived before the contestation of the claim: as for those perceived after, they shall be subject to the rules of the two preceding paragraphs.

In any restitution of fruits, ordinary expenses invested in producing them shall be reimbursed to the one making it.

**Article 908:** The defeated possessor has the right to be reimbursed for necessary expenses invested in the conservation of the thing, according to the following rules:

If these expenses were invested in permanent works, such as a fence to prevent depredations, or a dike to check floods, or repairs of a building ruined by an earthquake, said expenses shall be reimbursed to the possessor insofar as truly necessary; but reduced to what the works are worth at the time of restitution.

And if the expenses were invested in things that by their nature do not leave a permanent material result, such as judicial defense of the estate, they shall be reimbursed to the possessor insofar as they benefit the reclaimer, and have been executed with medium intelligence and economy.

**Article 909:** The possessor in good faith, defeated, also has the right to be reimbursed for useful improvements made before contestation of the claim.

Only improvements that have increased the commercial value of the thing are understood as useful improvements.

The reclaimer shall choose between payment of what the works constituting the improvements are worth at the time of restitution, or payment of what the thing is worth more by virtue of said improvements at said time.

As for works made after contestation of the claim, the possessor in good faith shall only have the rights granted to the possessor in bad faith by the following article.

**Article 910:** The possessor in bad faith shall not have the right to be reimbursed for the useful improvements mentioned in the preceding article.

But they may take away the materials of said improvements, provided they can separate them without damage to the reclaimed thing, and the owner refuses to pay them the price said materials would have after separation.

**Article 911:** Regarding voluptuary improvements, the owner shall not be obliged to pay them to the possessor in bad or good faith, who shall only have with respect to them the right granted by the preceding article to the possessor in bad faith with respect to useful improvements.

Voluptuary improvements are those consisting only of objects of luxury and recreation, such as gardens, belvederes, fountains, artificial waterfalls, and generally those that do not increase the commercial value of the thing in the general market, or only increase it in an insignificant proportion.

**Article 912:** The separation of materials, permitted by the preceding articles, shall be understood to be to the detriment of the reclaimed thing, when it would leave it in worse condition than before the improvements were made; except insofar as the defeated possessor can immediately restore it to its former state, and undertakes to do so.

**Article 913:** The good or bad faith of the possessor refers, relatively to fruits, to the time of perception, and relatively to expenses and improvements, to the time they were made.

**Article 914:** When the defeated possessor has a balance to claim in reason of expenses and improvements, they may retain the thing until payment is made, or secured to their satisfaction.

**Article 915:** The rules of this title shall apply against one who, possessing in another's name, unduly retains a real or movable thing, even if without intention of lord.

---

## TITLE XIII: OF POSSESSORY ACTIONS

**Article 916:** Possessory actions have for their object to preserve or recover possession of real property or of real rights constituted on them.

**Article 917:** Over things that cannot be gained by prescription, such as non-apparent or discontinuous servitudes, there can be no possessory action.

**Article 918:** Only the one who has been in peaceful and uninterrupted possession for a full year may bring a possessory action.

**Article 919:** The heir has and is subject to the same possessory actions that their author would have had and would be subject to, if living.

**Article 920:** Actions intended to preserve possession prescribe after one full year counted from the act of molestation or hindrance caused to it.

Those intended to recover possession expire after one full year counted from when the previous possessor lost it.

If the new possession has been violent or clandestine, this year shall be counted from the last act of violence, or from when the clandestinity ceased.

The rules on continuation of possession given in articles 717, 718, and 719 apply to possessory actions.

**Article 921:** The possessor has the right to request that they not be disturbed or hindered in their possession or dispossessed of it, that they be indemnified for the damage received, and that security be given against the one they reasonably fear.

**Article 922:** The usufructuary, the user, and the one who has the right of habitation are competent to exercise by themselves the possessory actions and exceptions directed to preserve or recover the enjoyment of their respective rights, even against the owner himself. The owner is obliged to assist them against any external disturber or usurper, when requested.

Judgments obtained against the usufructuary, the user, or the one who has the right of habitation, bind the owner; except if it concerns possession of the dominion of the estate or of rights annexed to it: in this case the judgment shall not be valid against the owner who has not intervened in the lawsuit.

**Article 923:** In possessory lawsuits, the dominion alleged by either party shall not be taken into account.

However, titles of dominion may be exhibited to prove possession, but only those whose existence can be summarily proved, nor shall it be valid to object against them other defects or faults than those that can be proved in the same manner.

**Article 924:** Possession of registered rights is proved by the registration, and as long as it subsists, and provided it has lasted a full year, no proof of possession intended to challenge it is admissible.

**Article 925:** Possession of the soil shall be proved by positive acts, such as those only dominion gives the right to, such as cutting timber, constructing buildings, enclosing, planting or sowing, and others of equal significance, executed without the consent of the one disputing possession.

**Article 926:** One who has been unjustly deprived of possession shall have the right to request restitution, with indemnification for damages.

**Article 927:** The action for restitution may be directed not only against the usurper, but against any person whose possession derives from the usurper's by any title.

But only the usurper himself, or the third party in bad faith, shall be obliged to indemnify for damages; and if several persons are obliged, all shall be jointly liable.

**Article 928:** Anyone who has been violently dispossessed, whether of possession or mere holding, and who because they possess in another's name, or because they have not possessed long enough, or for any other cause, cannot bring a possessory action, shall nevertheless have the right for things to be restored to the state they were in before, without needing to prove more than the violent dispossession, nor can clandestinity or previous dispossession be objected to them. This right prescribes in six months.

Once things are restored, and reparation of damages is assured, the corresponding possessory actions may be brought by either party.

**Article 929:** Acts of violence committed with or without weapons shall additionally be punished with the penalties corresponding under the Criminal Code.

---

## TITLE XIV: OF SOME SPECIAL POSSESSORY ACTIONS

**Article 930:** The possessor has the right to request that any new work intended to be constructed on the land of which they are in possession be prohibited.

But they shall not have the right to denounce for this purpose works necessary to prevent the ruin of a building, aqueduct, canal, bridge, ditch, etc., provided that insofar as they may inconvenience them, they are reduced to what is strictly necessary, and that once finished, things are restored to their former state at the owner's expense.

Nor shall they have the right to hinder work conducive to maintaining due cleanliness in roads, ditches, pipes, etc.

**Article 931:** New works are denounceable if constructed on the servient estate and hinder the enjoyment of a servitude constituted on it.

Constructions intended to be supported on another's building that is not subject to such servitude are likewise denounceable.

Any overhanging work that crosses the vertical plane of the dividing line of two estates, even if it does not rest on the neighboring estate, nor give a view, nor shed rainwater on it, is especially declared denounceable.

**Article 932:** One who fears that the ruin of a neighboring building may cause them harm has the right to complain to the judge to order the owner of such building to demolish it, if so deteriorated that it admits no repair; or if it does, to order it repaired immediately; and if the complained party does not proceed to comply with the judicial order, the building shall be demolished or repaired at their expense.

If the damage feared from the building is not serious, it shall suffice for the complained party to give security to repair any harm arising from the building's bad condition.

**Article 933:** In the case of the repair mentioned in the preceding article being made by someone other than the complained party, the one undertaking it shall preserve the form and dimensions of the old building in all its parts, unless necessary to alter them to prevent danger.

The alterations shall be adjusted to the will of the building's owner, insofar as compatible with the purpose of the complaint.

**Article 934:** If, after notification of the complaint, the building falls due to its bad condition, neighbors shall be indemnified for all damage; but if it falls by fortuitous event, such as flood, lightning, or earthquake, there shall be no indemnity; unless it is proved that the fortuitous event, without the building's bad condition, would not have brought it down.

There shall be no indemnity if there was no prior notification of the complaint.

**Article 935:** The preceding provisions shall extend to the danger feared from any constructions; or from poorly rooted trees, or exposed to being knocked down by cases of ordinary occurrence.

**Article 936:** Repealed.

**Article 937:** No prescription shall be admitted against works that corrupt the air and make it notoriously harmful.

**Articles 938-940:** Repealed.

**Article 941:** The owner of a house has the right to prevent near their walls there being deposits or currents of water, or moist materials that may damage it.

They also have the right to prevent trees from being planted at a distance of less than fifteen decimeters, nor vegetables or flowers at a distance of less than five decimeters.

If the trees are of those that extend their roots a great distance, the judge may order them planted at the distance convenient to prevent damage to neighboring buildings: the maximum distance fixed by the judge shall be five meters.

The rights granted in this article shall subsist against trees, flowers, or vegetables planted, unless the planting preceded the construction of the walls.

**Article 942:** If a tree extends its branches over another's land, or penetrates with its roots, the owner of the land may require the excess part of the branches to be cut, and cut the roots themselves.

This is understood even if the tree is planted at the proper distance.

**Article 943:** Fruits given by branches stretched over another's land belong to the owner of the tree; who, however, may not enter to gather them except with permission of the landowner, if the land is enclosed.

The landowner shall be obliged to grant this permission; but only on days and at times opportune, from which no harm results to them.

**Articles 944-945:** Repealed.

**Article 946:** Whenever a work belonging to many is to be prohibited, destroyed, or amended, the denunciation or complaint may be brought against all jointly or against any of them; but the indemnity for damage received shall be distributed among all equally, without prejudice to those charged with this indemnity dividing it among themselves in proportion to each one's share in the work.

And if the damage suffered or feared belongs to many, each shall have the right to bring the denunciation or complaint by themselves, insofar as it is directed to the prohibition, destruction, or amendment of the work; but none may claim indemnity except for the damage they themselves have suffered, unless they legitimize their representation with respect to the others.

**Article 947:** The actions granted in this title shall not apply against the exercise of a legitimately constituted servitude.

**Article 948:** The municipality and any member of the community shall have, in favor of roads, squares, or other places of public use, and for the safety of those traveling through them, the rights granted to owners of private estates or buildings.

And whenever by virtue of a popular action a construction is to be demolished or amended, or damage repaired, the actor shall be compensated at the cost of the complained party with a sum not less than one-tenth nor more than one-third of the cost of the demolition or amendment, or of the reparation of the damage; without prejudice to, if the crime or negligence is punished with a pecuniary penalty, half being awarded to the actor.

**Article 949:** Municipal or popular actions are understood without prejudice to those belonging to immediate interested parties.

**Article 950:** The actions granted in this title for indemnification of damage suffered prescribe forever after one full year.

Actions directed to prevent damage do not prescribe as long as there is just cause to fear it.

If actions directed against a new work are not brought within the year, the denounced or complained parties shall be protected in the possessory hearing, and the denouncer or complainant may only pursue their right by the ordinary route.

But not even this action shall apply when, according to the rules given for servitudes, the right has prescribed.

---

# BOOK THREE: OF SUCCESSION BY REASON OF DEATH, AND OF DONATIONS BETWEEN LIVING PERSONS

## TITLE I: DEFINITIONS AND GENERAL RULES

**Article 951:** One succeeds to a deceased person by universal title or by singular title.

The title is universal when one succeeds to the deceased in all their property, rights, and transmissible obligations, or in a share thereof, such as half, third, or fifth.

The title is singular when one succeeds in one or more species or certain bodies, such as a particular horse, a particular house; or in one or more indeterminate species of a certain kind, such as a horse, three cows, six hundred pesos, forty bushels of wheat.

**Article 952:** If succession is by virtue of a will, it is called testamentary succession, and if by virtue of law, intestate or ab intestato succession.

Succession to a deceased person's property may be partly testamentary and partly intestate.

**Article 953:** Assignments by reason of death are those made by law, or by the will of a deceased person, to succeed to their property.

By the word assignments, this Book signifies assignments by reason of death, whether made by man or by law.

The assignee is the person to whom the assignment is made.

**Article 954:** Universal assignments are called inheritances, and singular assignments are called legacies. The assignee of an inheritance is called an heir, and the assignee of a legacy, a legatee.

**Article 955:** The succession to a person's property opens at the moment of their death in their last domicile; excepting expressly excepted cases.

Succession is governed by the law of the domicile in which it opens; excepting legal exceptions.

**Article 956:** The grant of an assignment is the actual calling of the law to accept or repudiate it.

The inheritance or legacy is granted to the heir or legatee at the moment of the death of the person whose succession is concerned, if the heir or legatee is not called conditionally; or at the moment the condition is fulfilled, if the call is conditional.

Except if the condition is of not doing something that depends on the sole will of the assignee, in which case the assignment is granted at the moment of the testator's death, with the assignee giving sufficient security to restore the assigned thing with its accessions and fruits, in case the condition is contravened.

This, however, shall not apply when the testator has provided that while the condition of not doing something is pending, the assigned thing belongs to another assignee.

**Article 957:** If the heir or legatee whose rights to the succession have not prescribed dies before having accepted or repudiated the inheritance or legacy granted to them, they transmit to their heirs the right to accept or repudiate said inheritance or legacy, even if they die without knowing it has been granted to them.

This right cannot be exercised without accepting the inheritance of the person who transmits it.

**Article 958:** If two or more persons called to succeed one another are in the case of article 79, none of them shall succeed to the property of the others.

**Article 959:** In every succession by reason of death, to carry out the dispositions of the deceased or of the law, the following shall be deducted from the estate or mass of property the deceased has left, including hereditary credits:

1. The costs of publication of the will, if any, and others incidental to the opening of the succession;  
2. Hereditary debts;  
3. Fiscal taxes that burden the entire hereditary mass;  
4. Compulsory alimentary assignments.

The remainder is the liquid estate of which the testator or the law may dispose.

**Article 960:** Fiscal taxes that burden the entire mass extend to revocable donations confirmed by death.

Fiscal taxes on certain shares or legacies shall be charged to the respective assignees.

**Article 961:** Every person not declared incapable or unworthy by law shall be capable and worthy of succeeding.

**Article 962:** To be capable of succeeding, it is necessary to exist at the time the succession opens; except if one succeeds by right of transmission, according to article 957, in which case it suffices to exist when the succession of the person through whom the inheritance or legacy is transmitted opens.

If the inheritance or legacy is left under suspensive condition, it shall also be necessary to exist at the moment the condition is fulfilled.

However, assignments to persons who do not exist at the time the succession opens but are expected to exist shall not be invalidated for this reason if said persons exist before ten years from the opening of the succession expire.

Assignments offered as a prize to those who render an important service shall be valid with the same limitation, even if the one who performs the service did not exist at the time of the testator's death.

**Article 963:** Confraternities, guilds, or any establishments that are not juridical persons are incapable of any inheritance or legacy.

But if the assignment has for its object the foundation of a new corporation or establishment, legal approval may be requested, and once obtained, the assignment shall be valid.

**Article 964:** One who, before the inheritance or legacy is granted to them, has been judicially convicted of the crime of unlawful intercourse with said person and has not contracted with them a marriage producing civil effects, is incapable of succeeding to another person as heir or legatee.

The same extends to the person who, before the inheritance or legacy is granted to them, has been accused of said crime, if judicial conviction follows.

**Article 965:** By will granted during the last illness, no inheritance or legacy may be received, not even as fiduciary executor, by the ecclesiastic who confessed the deceased during the same illness, or habitually in the last two years prior to the will; nor the order, convent, or confraternity of which the ecclesiastic is a member; nor their relatives by consanguinity or affinity up to the third degree inclusive.

But this incapacity shall not include the testator's parish church, nor shall it affect the portion of property that said ecclesiastic or their relatives would have inherited ab intestato if there had been no will.

**Article 966:** A disposition in favor of an incapable person shall be null, even if disguised under the form of an onerous contract or by interposition of person.

**Article 967:** The incapable person does not acquire the inheritance or legacy until actions that may be brought against them by those having an interest in it have prescribed.

**Article 968:** The following are unworthy to succeed to the deceased as heirs or legatees:

1. The one who has committed the crime of homicide, femicide, parricide, or infanticide against the person of the deceased, or has intervened in this crime by act or counsel, or let them perish when able to save them;  
2. The one who committed a serious attack against the life, honor, or property of the person whose succession is concerned, or of their spouse, or of any of their ascendants or descendants, provided said attack is proved by final judgment;  
3. The relative within the sixth degree inclusive, who in a state of dementia or destitution of the person whose succession is concerned, did not assist them when able;  
4. The one who by force or fraud obtained some testamentary disposition from the deceased, or prevented them from making a will;  
5. The one who has fraudulently detained or concealed a will of the deceased, fraud being presumed by the mere fact of detention or concealment.

**Article 969:** 6\. The one who, being of legal age, has not denounced to the courts the homicide, femicide, parricide, infanticide, or any other crime against the life of the deceased person, as soon as possible, is unworthy to succeed.

This unworthiness shall cease if the courts have begun to proceed on the case.

But this cause of unworthiness may not be alleged unless it is established that the heir or legatee is not the spouse of the person by whose act or counsel the homicide, femicide, parricide, or infanticide was committed, nor is among their ascendants and descendants, nor is there between them a relative by consanguinity or affinity up to the third degree inclusive.

**Article 970:** 7\. The ascendant or descendant who, being called to succeed ab intestato to the pubescent, demented person, or deaf or deaf-mute who cannot make themselves clearly understood, did not request that a guardian or curator be appointed for them, and remained in this omission for a full year, is unworthy to succeed: unless it appears it was impossible for them to do so personally or through an attorney.

If many are called to the succession, the diligence of one of them shall benefit the others.

After the year has elapsed, the aforementioned obligation shall fall on those called in the second degree to the intestate succession.

The obligation does not extend to minors, nor in general to those living under guardianship or curatorship.

This cause of unworthiness disappears when the pubescent reaches puberty, or the demented, deaf, or deaf-mute takes the administration of their property.

**Article 971:** 8\. The guardian or curator who, appointed by the testator, excuses themselves without legitimate cause, is unworthy to succeed.

The executor who, appointed by the testator, excuses themselves without proving serious inconvenience, likewise becomes unworthy to succeed them.

This cause of unworthiness shall not extend to compulsory assignees in the amount they are, nor to those who, after the excuse is rejected by the judge, enter to serve the office.

**Article 972:** 9\. Finally, the one who, knowing of the incapacity, has promised the deceased to have their property or part of it pass, under any form, to an incapable person, is unworthy to succeed.

This cause of unworthiness may not be alleged against any of those who may have been induced to make the promise to the deceased by reverential fear; unless they have proceeded to execute the promise.

**Article 973:** The causes of unworthiness mentioned in the preceding articles may not be alleged against testamentary dispositions made after the facts that produce it, even if it is offered to prove that the deceased had no knowledge of those facts at the time of making the will or afterwards.

**Article 974:** Unworthiness produces no effect unless declared in a lawsuit, at the instance of any of the interested parties in the exclusion of the unworthy heir or legatee.

Once judicially declared, the unworthy person is obliged to restore the inheritance or legacy with its accessions and fruits.

**Article 975:** Unworthiness is purged in five years of possession of the inheritance or legacy.

**Article 976:** The action of unworthiness does not pass against third parties in good faith.

**Article 977:** The inheritance or legacy from which their author was made unworthy is transmitted to heirs, but with the same defect of unworthiness of their author, for all the time lacking to complete the five years.

**Article 978:** Hereditary or testamentary debtors may not oppose to the claimant the exception of incapacity or unworthiness.

**Article 979:** Incapacity or unworthiness does not deprive the excluded heir or legatee of maintenance the law indicates for them; but in the cases of article 968 they shall have no right to maintenance.

---

## TITLE II: RULES RELATING TO INTESTATE SUCCESSION

**Article 980:** Laws regulate succession to property the deceased has not disposed of, or if they did dispose, did not do so in accordance with law, or their dispositions have not taken effect.

**Article 981:** The law does not consider the origin of property to regulate intestate succession or burden it with restitutions or reserves.

**Article 982:** In intestate succession, sex nor primogeniture is considered.

**Article 983:** Called to intestate succession are the descendants of the deceased, their ascendants, the surviving spouse, their collaterals, the adopted child, as applicable, and the State.

The hereditary rights of the adopted child are governed by the respective law.

**Article 984:** One succeeds ab intestato either by personal right or by right of representation.

Representation is a legal fiction by which it is supposed that a person has the place and consequently the degree of relationship and hereditary rights that their father or mother would have, if the latter did not wish or could not succeed.

One may represent a father or mother who, if they had wished or been able to succeed, would have succeeded by right of representation.

**Article 985:** Those who succeed by representation inherit in all cases by roots, that is, whatever the number of children who represent the father or mother, they take among all and in equal parts the portion that would have fallen to the represented father or mother.

Those who do not succeed by representation succeed by heads, that is, they take among all and in equal parts the portion to which the law calls them; unless the same law establishes another division.

**Article 986:** There is always a place for representation in the descendants of the deceased and in the descendants of their siblings. Outside these descents, there is no place for representation.

**Article 987:** One may represent the ascendant whose inheritance has been repudiated.

One may also represent the incapable, the unworthy, the disinherited, and the one who repudiated the inheritance of the deceased.

**Article 988:** Children exclude all other heirs, unless there is also a surviving spouse, in which case the latter shall concur with them.

The surviving spouse shall receive a portion that, as a general rule, shall be equivalent to twice what by rigorous or effective legitimate share corresponds to each child. If there is only one child, the spouse's share shall be equal to the rigorous or effective legitimate share of that child. But in no case shall the portion corresponding to the spouse be less than one-fourth of the inheritance, or one-fourth of the legitimizing half as applicable.

If the surviving spouse corresponds to one-fourth of the inheritance or of the legitimizing half, the remainder shall be divided among the children in equal parts.

The said one-fourth shall be calculated considering the provisions of article 996\.

**Article 989:** If the deceased has left no posterity, they shall be succeeded by the surviving spouse and their ascendants of the closest degree.

In this case, the inheritance shall be divided into three parts, two for the spouse and one for the ascendants.

In the absence of the latter, the spouse shall take all the property, and in the absence of spouse, the ascendants.

If there is a single ascendant in the closest degree, they shall succeed in all the property, or in the entire hereditary portion of the ascendants.

**Article 990:** If the deceased has left no descendants, nor ascendants, nor spouse, they shall be succeeded by their siblings.

Among the siblings mentioned in this article, those of simple and double connection shall be included, but the portion of the former shall be half that corresponding to the latter.

**Article 991:** Repealed.

**Article 992:** In the absence of descendants, ascendants, spouse, and siblings, the other collaterals of the closest degree, whether of simple or double connection, up to the sixth degree inclusive, shall succeed to the deceased.

Collaterals of simple connection, that is, those who are only relatives of the deceased by one of the parents, shall have the right to half the portion of collaterals of double connection, that is, those who are at once relatives of the deceased by both parents. The collateral or collaterals of the closest degree shall always exclude the others.

**Article 993:** Repealed.

**Article 994:** The spouse judicially separated, who has given cause for separation by their fault, shall not have any share in the ab intestato inheritance of their spouse.

Nor shall the parents of the deceased succeed ab intestato if paternity or maternity has been judicially determined against their opposition, unless there is a restoration under article 203\.

**Article 995:** In the absence of all the ab intestato heirs designated in the preceding articles, the State shall succeed.

**Article 996:** When in the same estate one is to succeed by will and ab intestato, the testamentary dispositions shall be fulfilled, and the remainder shall be adjudicated to the ab intestato heirs according to the general rules.

But those who succeed at once by will and ab intestato shall charge to the portion corresponding to them ab intestato what they receive by will, without prejudice to retaining the entire testamentary portion if it exceeds the other.

The express will of the testator shall prevail over all this, to the extent provided by law.

In any case, the rule of paragraph one shall apply once the legitimacies and improvements of the inheritance are fully paid to those entitled to them.

**Article 997:** Foreigners are called to ab intestato successions opened in Chile in the same manner and according to the same rules as Chileans.

**Article 998:** In the ab intestato succession of a foreigner dying within or outside the territory of the Republic, Chileans shall have, by way of inheritance or maintenance, the same rights that according to Chilean laws would correspond to them on the intestate succession of a Chilean.

The interested Chileans may request that all that corresponds to them in the foreigner's succession be adjudicated to them in the foreigner's property existing in Chile.

The same shall apply if necessary to the succession of a Chilean who leaves property in a foreign country.

---

## TITLE III: OF THE ORDERING OF THE WILL

### § 1\. Of the Will in General

**Article 999:** A will is a more or less solemn act, by which a person disposes of all or part of their property to take full effect after their days, retaining the faculty to revoke the dispositions contained in it while living.

**Article 1000:** Any donation or promise that is not made perfect and irrevocable except by the death of the donor or promisor is a will, and must be subject to the same formalities as a will.

Excepted are donations or promises between spouses, which, although revocable, may be made under the form of contracts between living persons.

**Article 1001:** All testamentary dispositions are essentially revocable, notwithstanding the testator expresses in the will the determination not to revoke them.

Clauses derogating from future dispositions shall be deemed not written, even if confirmed by oath.

If in a previous will it has been ordered that its revocation shall not be valid unless made with certain words or signs, this disposition shall be regarded as not written.

**Article 1002:** Notes or papers referred to by the testator in the will shall not be regarded as parts of the will, even if the testator so orders; nor shall they be worth more than they would without this circumstance.

**Article 1003:** A will is an act of a single person.

All dispositions contained in a will executed by two or more persons at the same time shall be null, whether for the reciprocal benefit of the makers or of a third person.

**Article 1004:** The faculty to make a will is non-delegable.

**Article 1005:** The following are not competent to make a will:

1. Repealed;  
2. The pubescent;  
3. One who is under interdiction for dementia;  
4. One who is not currently in their sound mind due to drunkenness or other cause;  
5. Anyone who cannot clearly express their will.

Persons not included in this enumeration are competent to make a will.

**Article 1006:** A will made during the existence of any of the causes of incompetence expressed in the preceding article is null, even if the cause subsequently ceases to exist.

And on the contrary, a valid will does not cease to be so by the fact of any of these causes of incompetence subsequently arising.

**Article 1007:** A will in which force has in any way intervened is null in all its parts.

**Article 1008:** A will is solemn or less solemn.

A solemn will is one in which all the formalities that the law ordinarily requires have been observed.

A less solemn or privileged will is one in which some of these formalities may be omitted, due to special circumstances expressly determined by law.

A solemn will is open or closed.

An open, nuncupative, or public will is one in which the testator makes their dispositions known to the witnesses; and a closed or secret will is one in which the witnesses do not need to have knowledge of them.

**Article 1009:** The opening and publication of the will shall be made before the judge of the testator's last domicile; without prejudice to exceptions established in this regard by the laws.

**Article 1010:** Whenever the judge is to proceed to the opening and publication of a will, they shall first ascertain the death of the testator.

Excepted are cases where death must be presumed by law.

### § 2\. Of the Solemn Will and Firstly of That Executed in Chile

**Article 1011:** The solemn will is always written.

**Article 1012:** The following may not be witnesses in a solemn will executed in Chile:

1. Repealed;  
2. Those under eighteen years of age;  
3. Those under interdiction for dementia;  
4. All those currently deprived of reason;  
5. The blind;  
6. The deaf;  
7. The mute;  
8. Those convicted of any of the penalties designated in article 267, number 7, and in general, those who by final judgment are disqualified from being witnesses;  
9. The amanuenses of the notary authorizing the will;  
10. Foreigners not domiciled in Chile;  
11. Persons who do not understand the testator's language; without prejudice to the provisions of article 1024\.

At least two of the witnesses shall be domiciled in the commune or grouping of communes in which the will is executed, and at least one must know how to read and write when only three witnesses attend, and two when five attend.

**Article 1013:** If any of the causes of incapacity expressed in the preceding article does not manifest itself in the appearance or behavior of a witness, and is generally unknown in the place where the will is executed, the contrary opinion being based on positive and public facts, the will shall not be invalidated by the real incapacity of the witness.

But putative competence may only serve for one of the witnesses.

**Article 1014:** In Chile, the solemn and open will must be executed before a competent notary and three witnesses, or before five witnesses.

The judge of letters of the jurisdictional territory of the place of execution may act as notary: all said in this title concerning the notary shall be understood with respect to the judge of letters, as applicable.

**Article 1015:** What essentially constitutes the open will is the act in which the testator makes their dispositions known to the notary, if any, and to the witnesses.

The will shall be witnessed in all its parts by the testator, by the same notary, if any, and by the same witnesses.

**Article 1016:** In the will, the name and surname of the testator; the place of their birth; the nation to which they belong; whether or not they are domiciled in Chile, and if so, the commune in which they have their domicile; their age; the circumstance of being in their sound mind; the names of the persons with whom they have contracted marriage, of the children born in each marriage, of any other children of the testator, with distinction of living and dead; and the name, surname, and domicile of each of the witnesses shall be expressed.

These designations shall be adjusted to what the testator and witnesses respectively declare.

The place, day, month, and year of execution; and the name, surname, and office of the notary, if any, shall also be expressed.

**Article 1017:** The open will may have been previously written.

But whether the testator has it written, or it is written in one or more acts, all of it shall be read aloud by the notary, if any, or in the absence of a notary by one of the witnesses designated by the testator for this purpose.

While the will is being read, the testator shall be in view, and the persons whose presence is necessary shall hear the full tenor of their dispositions.

**Article 1018:** The act ends with the signatures of the testator and witnesses, and by that of the notary, if any.

If the testator does not know how or cannot sign, this circumstance shall be mentioned in the will, stating the cause.

If any of the witnesses is in the same situation, another shall sign for them and at their request, stating it.

**Article 1019:** The blind, the deaf, or the deaf-mute who can make themselves clearly understood, even if not in writing, may only make a will nuncupatively and before a notary or official acting as such.

In the case of the blind, the will shall be read aloud twice: the first by the notary or official, and the second by one of the witnesses chosen for the purpose by the testator.

In the case of the deaf or deaf-mute, the first and second reading shall also be made before an expert or specialist in sign language, who shall simultaneously make known to the maker the content thereof.

Special mention shall be made of these formalities in the will.

**Article 1020:** If the will has not been executed before a notary, or before a judge of letters, but before five witnesses, it shall be necessary to proceed to its publication in the following manner:

The competent judge shall have the witnesses appear to recognize their signatures and that of the testator.

If one or more of them does not appear due to absence or other impediment, it shall suffice that the present instrumental witnesses recognize the testator's signature, their own signatures, and those of the absent witnesses.

If necessary, and whenever the judge deems it convenient, the signatures of the testator and of the absent witnesses may be supported by sworn declarations of other trustworthy persons.

The judge shall then put their rubric at the beginning and end of each page of the will, and shall order it delivered with the proceedings to the acting notary to be incorporated into their protocols.

**Article 1021:** The solemn closed will must be executed before a notary and three witnesses.

The respective judge of letters may act as notary.

**Article 1022:** One who cannot read and write may not execute a closed will.

**Article 1023:** What essentially constitutes the closed will is the act in which the testator presents to the notary and witnesses a closed writing, declaring in a loud voice and in such a way that the notary and witnesses see, hear, and understand them (except in the case of the following article), that in that writing is contained their will. The mute may make this declaration by writing it in the presence of the notary and witnesses.

The will must be written or at least signed by the testator.

The superscription or cover of the will shall be closed or shall be closed externally, so that the will cannot be removed without breaking the cover.

It is at the testator's discretion to stamp a seal or mark, or use any other means for the security of the cover.

The notary shall express on the superscription or cover, under the heading "will", the circumstance that the testator is in their sound mind; the name, surname, and domicile of the testator and of each of the witnesses; and the place, day, month, and year of execution.

The execution ends with the signatures of the testator and of the witnesses, and by the signature and seal of the notary, on the cover.

During the execution, in addition to the testator, the same notary and the same witnesses shall be present, and there shall be no interruption except in the brief intervals that some accident requires.

**Article 1024:** When the testator cannot understand or be understood by word of mouth, they may only execute a closed will.

The testator shall write in their own hand, on the cover, the word "will", or the equivalent in the language they prefer, and shall likewise make the designation of their person, expressing at least their name, surname, and domicile, and the nation to which they belong; and in other respects the provisions of the preceding article shall be observed.

**Article 1025:** The closed will, before receiving execution, shall be presented to the judge.

The will shall not be opened until after the notary and witnesses recognize before the judge their signature and that of the testator, declaring also whether in their opinion it is closed, sealed, or marked as at the time of delivery.

If all the witnesses cannot appear, it shall suffice that the notary and the present instrumental witnesses recognize their signatures and that of the testator, and support those of the absent.

If the notary or official who authorized the will cannot appear, they shall be replaced for the opening proceedings by the notary chosen by the judge.

If necessary, and whenever the judge deems it convenient, the signatures of the notary and absent witnesses may be supported, as in the case of paragraph 4 of article 1020\.

**Article 1026:** The solemn will, open or closed, in which any of the formalities to which it must respectively be subject according to the preceding articles is omitted, shall have no value.

However, when one or more of the designations prescribed in article 1016, in paragraph 5 of 1023, and in paragraph 2 of 1024 are omitted, the will shall not be null for this reason, as long as there is no doubt about the personal identity of the testator, notary, or witness.

### § 3\. Of the Solemn Will Executed in a Foreign Country

**Article 1027:** A written will executed in a foreign country shall be valid in Chile if, regarding formalities, its conformity to the laws of the country where it was executed is proven, and if the authenticity of the respective instrument is also proven in the ordinary manner.

**Article 1028:** A will executed in a foreign country shall also be valid in Chile, provided the following requirements concur:

1. Only a Chilean, or a foreigner having domicile in Chile, may make a will in this manner.  
2. Only a Plenipotentiary Minister, a Chargé d'Affaires, a Secretary of Legation holding such title issued by the President of the Republic, or a Consul holding a patent from the same, but not a Vice-Consul, may authorize this will. Express mention shall be made of the office, and of the said title and patent.  
3. Witnesses shall be Chileans, or foreigners domiciled in the city where the will is executed.  
4. In other respects, the rules of the solemn will executed in Chile shall be observed.  
5. The instrument shall bear the seal of the Legation or Consulate.

**Article 1029:** The will executed in the form prescribed in the preceding article and not before a head of Legation shall bear the "Seen" of this head; if the will is open, at the foot, and if closed, on the cover: the open will shall always be rubriced by the same head at the beginning and end of each page.

The head of Legation shall then send a copy of the open will, or of the cover of the closed one, to the Minister of Foreign Relations of Chile; who in turn, attesting to the signature of the head of Legation, shall send said copy to the judge of the deceased's last domicile in Chile, to have it incorporated into the protocols of a notary of the same domicile.

If the testator is known to have no domicile in Chile, the will shall be sent by the Minister of Foreign Relations to a judge of letters of Santiago, for incorporation into the protocols of the notary's office designated by the same judge.

### § 4\. Of Privileged Wills

**Article 1030:** Privileged wills are:

1. The verbal will;  
2. The military will;  
3. The maritime will.

**Article 1031:** In privileged wills, any person of sound mind, man or woman, over eighteen years of age, who sees, hears, and understands the testator, and who does not have the incapacity designated in number 8 of article 1012, may serve as witness.

For written privileged wills, it shall also be required that the witnesses know how to read and write.

Putative competence, in accordance with the provisions of article 1013, shall suffice.

**Article 1032:** In privileged wills, the testator shall expressly declare that their intention is to make a will: the persons whose presence is necessary shall be the same from beginning to end; and the act shall be continuous, or only interrupted in the brief intervals that some accident requires.

No other formalities shall be necessary than these, and those expressed in the following articles.

**Article 1033:** The verbal will shall be witnessed by at least three witnesses.

**Article 1034:** In the verbal will, the testator makes their declarations and dispositions aloud, so that all see, hear, and understand them.

**Article 1035:** The verbal will shall only apply in cases of such imminent danger to the testator's life that there appears to be no way or time to make a solemn will.

**Article 1036:** The verbal will shall have no value if the testator dies after thirty days following the execution; or if, having died before, the will has not been put in writing, with the formalities to be expressed, within thirty days following the day of death.

**Article 1037:** To put the verbal will in writing, the judge of letters of the jurisdictional territory in which it was executed, at the request of any person who may have an interest in the succession, and with summons of the other interested parties residing in the same jurisdiction, shall take sworn statements from the individuals who witnessed it as instrumental witnesses and all other persons whose testimony seems conducive to clarifying the following points:

1. The name, surname, and domicile of the testator, the place of their birth, the nation to which they belonged, their age, and the circumstances that led them to believe that their life was in imminent danger;  
2. The name and surname of the instrumental witnesses and the commune in which they reside;  
3. The place, day, month, and year of execution.

**Article 1038:** The instrumental witnesses shall testify on the following points:

1. Whether the testator appeared to be of sound mind;  
2. Whether they expressed the intention to make a will before them;  
3. Their testamentary declarations and dispositions.

**Article 1039:** The information mentioned in the preceding articles shall be sent to the judge of letters of the last domicile, if not the one who received the information; and the judge, if they find that the prescribed formalities have been observed, and that the testator's last will clearly appears in the information, shall rule that according to said information, the testator has made the following declarations and dispositions (stating them); and shall order that said declarations and dispositions be valid as the will of the deceased, and that their decree be recorded in the protocols as such.

Only those declarations or dispositions in which the witnesses who attended by way of formality agree shall be regarded as declarations or testamentary dispositions.

**Article 1040:** The will recorded in the recorded judicial decree may be challenged in the same manner as any other authentic will.

**Article 1041:** In time of war, the will of military personnel and other individuals employed in a body of troops of the Republic, and also of volunteers, hostages, and prisoners belonging to said body, and of persons accompanying and serving any of the aforementioned, may be received by a captain or by an officer of rank superior to captain or by an army intendant, commissary, or judge advocate.

If the person wishing to make a will is sick or wounded, their will may be received by the chaplain, doctor, or surgeon attending them; and if they are in a detachment, by the officer commanding it, even if of rank inferior to captain.

**Article 1042:** The will shall be signed by the testator, if they know how and are able to write, by the official who received it, and by the witnesses. If the testator does not know how or cannot sign, this shall be expressed in the will.

**Article 1043:** To make a military will, it shall be necessary to be in a war expedition currently on march or campaign against the enemy, or in the garrison of a currently besieged city.

**Article 1044:** If the testator dies before the ninety days following the time the circumstances enabling military will-making ceased expire, their will shall be valid as if executed in the ordinary form. If the testator survives this period, the will shall lapse.

**Article 1045:** The will shall bear at the foot the "Seen" of the superior head of the expedition or of the commander of the city, if not executed before the same head or commander, and shall always be rubriced at the beginning and end of each page by said head or commander; who shall then send it with the possible brevity and security to the Minister of War, who shall proceed as the Minister of Foreign Relations in the case of article 1029\.

**Article 1046:** When a person who can make a military will is in imminent danger, they may make a verbal will in the form prescribed above; but this will shall lapse by the fact of the testator surviving the danger.

The information mentioned in articles 1037 and 1038 shall be taken as soon as possible before the judge advocate or the person acting as such.

To send the information to the judge of the last domicile, the provisions of the preceding article shall be complied with.

**Article 1047:** If the person who can make a military will prefers to make a closed will, the formalities prescribed in article 1023 shall be observed, with any of the persons designated at the end of paragraph 1 of article 1041 acting as minister of faith.

The cover shall be vised like the will in the case of article 1045; and for its sending, proceedings shall be according to the same article.

**Article 1048:** A maritime will may be executed on board a Chilean warship on the high seas.

It shall be received by the commander or by their second officer in the presence of three witnesses.

If the testator does not know how or cannot sign, this circumstance shall be expressed in the will.

A duplicate of the will shall be made with the same signatures as the original.

**Article 1049:** The will shall be kept among the most important papers of the ship, and notice of its execution shall be given in the logbook.

**Article 1050:** If the ship before returning to Chile arrives at a foreign port where there is a Chilean diplomatic or consular agent, the commander shall deliver to this agent a copy of the will, demanding receipt, and noting it in the logbook, and the said agent shall send it to the Ministry of the Navy for the purposes expressed in article 1029\.

If the ship arrives in Chile first, said copy shall be delivered with the same formalities to the respective maritime governor, who shall transmit it for the same purposes to the Ministry of the Navy.

**Article 1051:** Not only officers and crew members may make a will in the form prescribed by article 1048, but any others who are on board a Chilean warship on the high seas.

**Article 1052:** The maritime will shall not be valid except when the testator has died before disembarking, or before ninety days from disembarkation expire.

Disembarkation shall not be understood to mean going ashore for a short time to re-embark on the same ship.

**Article 1053:** In case of imminent danger, a verbal will may be executed on board a warship on the high seas, observing the provisions of article 1046; and the will shall lapse if the testator survives the danger.

The information mentioned in articles 1037 and 1038 shall be received by the commander or their second officer, and for its sending to the judge of letters through the Ministry of the Navy, the provisions of article 1046 shall apply.

**Article 1054:** If the person who can make a maritime will prefers to make a closed one, the formalities prescribed in article 1023 shall be observed, with the commander of the ship or their second officer acting as minister of faith.

The provisions of article 1049 shall also be observed, and a copy of the cover shall be sent to the Ministry of the Navy to be recorded like the will according to article 1050\.

**Article 1055:** On merchant ships under the Chilean flag, a will may only be made in the form prescribed by article 1048, the will being received by the captain or their second officer or the pilot, and the provisions of article 1050 also being observed.

---

## TITLE IV: OF TESTAMENTARY ASSIGNMENTS

### § 1\. General Rules

**Article 1056:** Every testamentary assignee must be a certain and determined person, natural or juridical, whether determined by name or by clear indications in the will. Otherwise, the assignment shall be deemed not written.

Assignments destined for charitable purposes shall be valid, even if not for determined persons.

Assignments made to a charitable establishment without designating it shall be given to the charitable establishment designated by the President of the Republic, preferring one from the testator's commune or province.

What is left to the testator's soul, without specifying its investment otherwise, shall be understood to be left to a charitable establishment, and shall be subject to the provision of the preceding paragraph.

What in general is left to the poor shall be applied to the poor of the testator's parish.

**Article 1057:** Error in the name or quality of the assignee does not vitiate the disposition, if there is no doubt about the person.

**Article 1058:** An assignment that appears motivated by an error of fact, such that it is clear that without this error it would not have taken place, shall be deemed not written.

**Article 1059:** Captatory dispositions shall not be valid. Such are those in which the testator assigns part of their property on the condition that the assignee leaves them by will some part of theirs.

**Article 1060:** No testamentary disposition is valid that the testator has not made known otherwise than by yes or no, or by a sign of affirmation or negation, answering a question.

**Article 1061:** No testamentary disposition in favor of the notary authorizing the will, or of the official acting as such, or of the spouse of said notary or official, or of any of their ascendants, descendants, siblings, brothers-in-law, employees, or employees of the same, is valid.

No testamentary disposition in favor of any of the witnesses, or of their spouse, ascendants, descendants, siblings, or brothers-in-law, is valid either.

**Article 1062:** The creditor whose credit appears only by the will shall be considered as a legatee for the purposes of the preceding article.

**Article 1063:** The choice of an assignee, whether absolutely or from among a certain number of persons, shall not depend on another's pure discretion.

**Article 1064:** What is left indeterminately to relatives shall be understood to be left to the consanguineous relatives of the closest degree, according to the order of intestate succession, with the right of representation applying in accordance with the legal rules; except that on the date of the will there has been only one in that degree, in which case those of the immediate degree shall be understood to be called at the same time.

**Article 1065:** If the assignment is conceived or written in such terms that it is not known to which of two or more persons the testator intended to designate, none of said persons shall have the right to it.

**Article 1066:** Every assignment must be either by universal title, or of determined species or that by the testator's indications can be clearly determined, or of kinds and quantities that are likewise or can be determined. Otherwise, it shall be deemed not written.

However, if the assignment is destined for a charitable object expressed in the will, without determining the share, quantity, or species to be invested in it, the assignment shall be valid and the share, quantity, or species shall be determined, considering the nature of the object, the other dispositions of the testator, and the resources of the estate, in the part of which the testator could freely dispose.

The judge shall make the determination, hearing the defender of pious works and the heirs; and conforming as much as possible to the testator's intention.

**Article 1067:** If the fulfillment of an assignment is left to the discretion of an heir or legatee who would benefit by refusing it, the heir or legatee shall be obliged to carry it out, unless they prove just cause not to do so. If refusing the assignment results in no benefit to the heir or legatee, they shall not be obliged to justify their decision, whatever it may be.

The benefit of an ascendant or descendant, of a spouse, or of a sibling or brother-in-law, shall be considered, for the purpose of this provision, as benefit of said heir or legatee.

**Article 1068:** An assignment that, due to the assignee failing, is transferred to a different person, by accrual, substitution, or other cause, shall carry with it all transmissible obligations and burdens, and the right to accept or repudiate it separately.

An assignment that has been repudiated by all persons successively called to it by the testator or by law because it is too burdensome, shall be granted finally to the persons in whose favor the encumbrances were constituted.

**Article 1069:** Over the rules given in this title regarding the understanding and effect of testamentary dispositions, the clearly manifested will of the testator shall prevail, provided it does not oppose legal requirements or prohibitions.

To know the testator's will, more weight shall be given to the substance of the dispositions than to the words used.

### § 2\. Of Conditional Testamentary Assignments

**Article 1070:** Testamentary assignments may be conditional.

A conditional assignment is, in the will, one that depends on a condition, that is, on a future and uncertain event, so that according to the testator's intention, the assignment is not valid if the positive event does not occur, or if the negative event occurs.

Conditional testamentary assignments are subject to the rules given in the title On conditional obligations, with the exceptions and modifications to be expressed.

**Article 1071:** A condition consisting of a present or past fact does not suspend fulfillment of the disposition. If it exists or has existed, it is regarded as not written; if it does not exist or has not existed, the disposition is not valid.

Past, present, and future shall be understood in relation to the time of making the will, unless otherwise expressed.

**Article 1072:** If the condition imposed as for future time consists of a fact that occurred during the testator's lifetime, and the testator knew it at the time of making the will, and the fact is one that can be repeated, the testator shall be presumed to require its repetition; if the testator knew it at the time of making the will, and the fact is one whose repetition is impossible, the condition shall be regarded as fulfilled; and if the testator did not know it, the condition shall be regarded as fulfilled, whatever the nature of the fact.

**Article 1073:** The condition of not challenging the will, imposed on an assignee, does not extend to claims of nullity for any defect in its form.

**Article 1074:** The condition imposed on the heir or legatee not to marry shall be deemed not written, except if limited to not marrying before the age of eighteen or less.

**Article 1075:** The condition of remaining in a state of widowhood shall likewise be deemed not written; unless the assignee has one or more children from a previous marriage at the time the assignment is granted.

**Article 1076:** The preceding articles do not prevent provision being made for a person's subsistence while they remain unmarried or widowed, leaving them for that time a right of usufruct, use, or habitation, or a periodic pension.

**Article 1077:** The condition of marrying or not marrying a determined person, and of embracing any state or profession permitted by the laws, even if incompatible with the state of marriage, shall be valid.

**Article 1078:** Conditional testamentary assignments under suspensive condition do not confer on the assignee any right while the condition is pending, except to request necessary conservatory measures.

If the assignee dies before the condition is fulfilled, they transmit no right.

Once the condition is fulfilled, they shall not have the right to fruits perceived in the intervening time, unless the testator has expressly granted them to them.

**Article 1079:** Conditional dispositions establishing trusts and granting fiduciary property are governed by the title On fiduciary property.

### § 3\. Of Testamentary Assignments to a Day

**Article 1080:** Testamentary assignments may be limited to terms or days on which current enjoyment or extinction of a right depends; and shall then be subject to the rules given in the title On obligations at term, with the following explanations.

**Article 1081:** A day is certain and determined if it necessarily must arrive and it is known when, such as the day of such month and year, or so many days, months, or years after the date of the will or the death of the testator.

It is certain but indeterminate if it necessarily must arrive, but it is not known when, such as the day of a person's death.

It is uncertain but determined if it may or may not arrive, but supposing it is to arrive, it is known when, such as the day a person turns twenty-five.

Finally, it is uncertain and indeterminate if it is not known whether it will arrive, nor when, such as the day a person marries.

**Article 1082:** What is assigned from a day that arrives before the testator's death shall be understood to be assigned for after their days and shall only be owed from the opening of the succession.

**Article 1083:** The uncertain and indeterminate day is always a true condition, and is subject to the rules of conditions.

**Article 1084:** An assignment from a certain and determined day gives the assignee, from the moment of the testator's death, the property of the assigned thing and the right to alienate and transmit it; but not the right to claim it before the day arrives.

If the testator expressly imposes the condition that the assignee exist on that day, it shall be subject to the rules of conditional assignments.

**Article 1085:** An assignment from a certain but indeterminate day is conditional and involves the condition that the assignee exist on that day.

If it is known that the assignee is to exist on that day, such as when the assignment is in favor of a permanent establishment, the provision of paragraph 1 of the preceding article shall apply.

**Article 1086:** An assignment from an uncertain day, whether determined or not, is always conditional.

**Article 1087:** An assignment until a certain day, whether determined or not, constitutes a usufruct in favor of the assignee.

An assignment of periodic payments is intransmissible by reason of death, and terminates, like usufruct, by the arrival of the day, and by the death of the pensioner. If in favor of a corporation or foundation, it may not last more than thirty years.

**Article 1088:** An assignment until an uncertain but determined day, joined to the existence of the assignee, constitutes a usufruct; except if it consists of periodic payments.

If the day is joined to the existence of another person than the assignee, the usufruct shall be understood to be granted until the date on which, the other person living, the day would arrive for them.

### § 4\. Of Modal Assignments

**Article 1089:** If something is assigned to a person for them to have it as their own with the obligation to apply it to a special purpose, such as doing certain works or subjecting themselves to certain burdens, this application is a mode and not a suspensive condition. The mode, therefore, does not suspend the acquisition of the assigned thing.

**Article 1090:** In modal assignments, the resolutory clause is the one that imposes the obligation to restore the thing and the fruits if the mode is not fulfilled.

They shall not be understood to involve a resolutory clause when the testator does not express it.

**Article 1091:** For the thing assigned modally to be acquired, it is not necessary to give security for restitution for the case of not fulfilling the mode.

**Article 1092:** If the mode is for the benefit of the assignee exclusively, it imposes no obligation, except if it carries a resolutory clause.

**Article 1093:** If the mode is impossible by its nature, or induces an illegal or immoral act, or is conceived in unintelligible terms, the disposition shall not be valid.

If the mode, without fault of the assignee, is only impossible in the special form prescribed by the testator, it may be fulfilled in another analogous form that does not alter the substance of the disposition, and which is approved by the judge with summons of the interested parties.

If the mode, without fault of the assignee, becomes entirely impossible, the assignment shall subsist without the burden.

**Article 1094:** If the testator does not sufficiently determine the time or special form in which the mode is to be fulfilled, the judge may determine them, consulting the testator's will as far as possible, and leaving the modal assignee a benefit that amounts at least to one-fifth of the value of the assigned thing.

**Article 1095:** If the mode consists of an act such that for the purpose the testator has proposed, the person performing it is indifferent, it is transmissible to the assignee's heirs.

**Article 1096:** Whenever the resolutory clause is to be carried out, a sum proportionate to the object shall be delivered to the person in whose favor the mode has been constituted, and the remainder of the value of the assigned thing shall accrue to the inheritance, if the testator has not ordered otherwise.

The assignee on whom the mode has been imposed shall not enjoy the benefit that may result from the preceding disposition.

### § 5\. Of Universal Assignments

**Article 1097:** Universal assignees, by whatever name they are called, and even if in the will they are called legatees, are heirs: they represent the person of the testator to succeed them in all their transmissible rights and obligations.

Heirs are also obliged to testamentary burdens, that is, to those constituted by the will itself, and not imposed on determined persons.

**Article 1098:** An assignee who has been called to the succession in general terms that do not designate shares, such as "Let so-and-so be my heir", or "I leave my property to so-and-so", is a universal heir.

But if they concur with heirs of a share, they shall be understood to be the heir of that share that with those designated in the will completes the unit or whole.

If there are many heirs instituted without designation of share, they shall divide among themselves in equal parts the inheritance or the part of it that corresponds to them.

**Article 1099:** If, after other assignments are made, the remainder of the property is disposed of, and all assignments except the remainder are singular, the assignee of the remainder is a universal heir; if some of the other assignments are of shares, the assignee of the remainder is the heir of the share remaining to complete the unit.

**Article 1100:** If there are no universal heirs, but only of a share, and those designated in the will do not all together complete the whole unit, the ab intestato heirs shall be understood to be called as heirs of the remainder.

If in the will there is no universal assignment, the ab intestato heirs are universal heirs.

**Article 1101:** If the shares designated in the will complete or exceed the unit, then the universal heir shall be understood to be instituted in a share whose numerator is the unit and denominator the total number of heirs; unless they are instituted as heir of the remainder, in which case they shall have nothing.

**Article 1102:** Once the shares are reduced to a common denominator, including those computed according to the preceding article, the inheritance shall be represented by the sum of the numerators, and the effective share of each heir by their respective numerator.

**Article 1103:** The provisions of this title are understood without prejudice to the action of reform that the law grants to legitimaries and the surviving spouse.

### § 6\. Of Singular Assignments

**Article 1104:** Singular assignees, by whatever name they are called, and even if in the will they are called heirs, are legatees: they do not represent the testator; they have no more rights nor burdens than those expressly conferred or imposed on them.

This, however, is understood without prejudice to their subsidiary responsibility to heirs, and to that which may arise in the case of the action of reform.

**Article 1105:** A legacy of a thing incapable of being appropriated, according to article 585, is not valid, nor legacies of things that at the time of the will are national or municipal property and of public use, or form part of a building so that they cannot be separated without damaging it; unless the cause ceases before the legacy is granted.

The same applies to legacies of things belonging to divine worship; but private individuals may bequeath to other persons the rights they have in them, and which are not according to canon law intransmissible.

**Article 1106:** The testator may order that another's species be acquired to give it to some person or to employ it for some charitable object; and if the assignee on whom this obligation is imposed cannot fulfill it because the owner of the species refuses to alienate it or asks an excessive price for it, the said assignee shall only be obliged to give the fair price of the species in money.

And if the foreign species bequeathed was previously acquired by the legatee or for the charitable object, its price shall not be owed, except insofar as the acquisition was for consideration and at an equitable price.

**Article 1107:** The legacy of a species that is not the testator's, or of the assignee on whom the obligation to give it is imposed, is null; unless in the will it appears that the testator knew the thing was not theirs or the said assignee's; or unless the foreign thing is bequeathed to a descendant or ascendant of the testator or to their spouse; in which cases proceedings shall be as in paragraph 1 of the preceding article.

**Article 1108:** If the foreign thing bequeathed passed, before the testator's death, into the ownership of the testator or of the assignee on whom the obligation to give it was imposed, the legacy shall be owed.

**Article 1109:** The assignee obliged to provide the legacy of a foreign thing, who acquires it after the testator's death, shall owe it to the legatee; who, however, may not claim it except by restoring what they have received for it, according to article 1106\.

**Article 1110:** If the testator has had only a part, share, or right in the bequeathed thing, they shall be presumed not to have intended to bequeath more than that part, share, or right.

The same applies to the thing an assignee is obliged to give and in which they only have a part, share, or right.

**Article 1111:** If in bequeathing a species the place where it is kept is designated and it is not found there, but is found elsewhere, the species shall be owed: if it is not found anywhere, a species of medium quality of the same kind shall be owed, but only to persons designated in article 1107\.

**Article 1112:** The legacy of a fungible thing whose quantity is not somehow determined is not valid.

If the fungible thing is bequeathed indicating the place where it is to be found, the quantity found there at the time of the testator's death shall be owed, given that the testator has not determined the quantity; or up to the amount of the quantity determined by the testator, and no more.

If the existing quantity is less than the designated quantity, only the existing quantity shall be owed; and if there is no quantity of said fungible thing there, nothing shall be owed.

This, however, is understood with the following limitations:

1. The legacy of the fungible thing whose quantity is determined by the testator shall always be valid, in favor of the persons designated in article 1107\.  
2. It shall not matter that the bequeathed thing is not found in the place indicated by the testator, when the legacy and the indication of place do not form an indivisible clause.

Thus the legacy of "thirty bushels of wheat, which are in such place", is valid, even if no wheat is found there; but the legacy of "the thirty bushels of wheat that will be found in such place" is only valid with respect to the wheat found there, and not exceeding thirty bushels.

**Article 1113:** The legacy of a future thing is valid, provided it comes to exist.

**Article 1114:** If of many species existing in the testator's estate, one is bequeathed without saying which, a species of medium quality or value among those included in the legacy shall be owed.

**Article 1115:** Legacies of kind that are not limited to what exists in the testator's estate, such as a cow, a horse, impose the obligation to give a thing of medium quality or value of the same kind.

**Article 1116:** If a thing was bequeathed among several that the testator believed to have, and they have left only one, the one they left shall be owed.

If they have left none, the legacy shall not be valid except in favor of persons designated in article 1107; who shall only have the right to ask for a medium thing of the same kind, even if the testator has granted them the choice.

But if a thing is bequeathed whose value has no limits, such as a house, a country estate, and none of the same kind exists among the testator's property, nothing shall be owed even to persons designated in article 1107\.

**Article 1117:** If the choice of a thing among many is expressly given to the obliged person or to the legatee, the former or the latter may respectively offer or choose at their discretion.

If the testator entrusts the choice to a third person, they may choose at their discretion; and if they do not fulfill their task within the time indicated by the testator or in default by the judge, the rule of article 1114 shall apply.

Once the choice is made, there shall be no place to make it again, except for cause of deceit or fraud.

**Article 1118:** The bequeathed species is owed in the state in which it existed at the time of the testator's death, including the utensils necessary for its use and existing with it.

**Article 1119:** If the bequeathed thing is an estate, the lands and new buildings the testator has added to it after the will shall not be included in the legacy; and if the newly added forms with the rest, at the time the succession opens, a whole that cannot be divided without serious loss, and the additions are worth more than the estate in its previous state, only this second value shall be owed to the legatee: if they are worth less, all shall be owed to the legatee with the obligation to pay the value of the additions.

But the legacy of a measure of land, such as one thousand square meters, shall not increase in any case by the acquisition of contiguous lands, and if the former cannot be separated from the latter, only what it is worth shall be owed.

If a building plot is bequeathed and the testator later builds on it, only the value of the plot shall be owed.

**Article 1120:** If part of an estate is left, servitudes necessary for its enjoyment or cultivation shall be understood to be bequeathed.
