**Article 1121:** If a house is bequeathed with its furniture or with everything found in it, the things enumerated in paragraph 2 of article 574 shall not be understood to be included in the legacy, but only those forming the household furniture and found in it; and if in the same manner a country estate is bequeathed, the legacy shall not be understood to include other things than those serving for the cultivation and benefit of the estate and found in it.

In either case, of the other objects contained in the house or estate, only those the testator expressly designates shall be owed.

**Article 1122:** If a carriage of any kind is bequeathed, the harnesses and the animals the testator used to use it, and existing with it at the time of their death, shall be understood to be bequeathed.

**Article 1123:** If a flock is bequeathed, the animals composing it at the time of the testator's death shall be owed, and no more.

**Article 1124:** If shares of the same thing are bequeathed to several different persons, the rules of the preceding paragraph shall apply for the division of it.

**Article 1125:** The bequeathed species passes to the legatee with its servitudes, ground rents, and other real burdens.

**Article 1126:** If a thing is bequeathed with the condition not to alienate it, and the alienation does not compromise any third party right, the clause not to alienate shall be deemed not written.

**Article 1127:** Not only corporeal things may be bequeathed, but rights and actions.

By the fact of bequeathing the title of a credit, the credit shall be understood to be bequeathed.

The legacy of a credit includes earned interest; but does not subsist except in the part of the credit or interest not received by the testator.

**Article 1128:** If the thing pledged to the testator is bequeathed to the debtor, the debt is not thereby extinguished, but the right of pledge is; unless it clearly appears that the testator's will was to extinguish the debt.

**Article 1129:** If the testator remits a debt in the will, and later judicially demands payment from the debtor, or accepts payment offered, the debtor may not take advantage of the remission; but if payment was made without the testator's knowledge or consent, the legatee may claim what was paid.

**Article 1130:** If a remission is made to a person of what they owe, without determining the sum, only debts existing at the date of the will shall be included in the remission.

**Article 1131:** What is bequeathed to a creditor shall not be understood to be on account of their credit, unless expressed, or if by circumstances it does not clearly appear that the testator's intention is to pay the debt with the legacy.

If so expressed or appears, the debt shall be recognized in the terms the testator has done so, or in which the obligation is justified as having been contracted; and the creditor may at their option demand payment in the terms to which the debtor was obliged or in those expressed in the will.

**Article 1132:** If the testator orders payment of what they believe they owe and do not owe, the disposition shall be deemed not written.

If by reason of a determined debt more than its amount is ordered to be paid, the excess shall not be owed unless the intention to donate it appears.

**Article 1133:** Debts confessed in the will and for which there is otherwise no beginning of proof in writing shall be considered as gratuitous legacies, and shall be subject to the same responsibilities and deductions as other legacies of this class.

**Article 1134:** If voluntary maintenance is bequeathed without determining its form and amount, it shall be owed in the form and amount in which the testator used to provide it to the same person; and in default of this determination, it shall be regulated considering the legatee's need, their relations with the testator, and the resources of the estate in the part of which the testator could freely dispose.

If the testator does not fix the time the maintenance contribution is to last, it shall be understood to last for the entire life of the legatee.

If an annual pension for the legatee's education is bequeathed, it shall last until they reach eighteen years, and shall cease if they die before reaching that age.

**Article 1135:** By the destruction of the bequeathed species, the obligation to pay the legacy is extinguished.

The alienation of the bequeathed species, in whole or in part, by act between living persons, involves the revocation of the legacy, in whole or in part; and the legacy shall not subsist or revive, even if the alienation was null, and even if the bequeathed species returns to the testator's power.

A pledge, mortgage, or ground rent constituted on the bequeathed thing does not extinguish the legacy, but encumbers it with said pledge, mortgage, or ground rent.

If the testator substantially alters the bequeathed movable thing, such as from wood makes a carriage, or from wool fabrics, the legacy shall be understood to be revoked.

### § 7\. Of Revocable Donations

**Article 1136:** A revocable donation is one that the donor may revoke at their discretion.

A donation by reason of death is the same as a revocable donation; and a donation between living persons, the same as an irrevocable donation.

**Article 1137:** Only a donation executed with the formalities that the law prescribes for its class, or one to which the law expressly gives this character, shall be valid as a revocable donation.

If a donation is made with the formalities of those between living persons, and the donor in the instrument reserves the faculty to revoke it, it shall be necessary for the donor to have expressly confirmed it in a testamentary act for it to subsist after the donor's death; except if the donation is from one spouse to the other.

Donations for which no instrument is executed shall be valid as donations between living persons as applicable; except those made between spouses, which may always be revoked.

**Article 1138:** Revocable donations by persons who cannot make a will or donate between living persons are null. Those between persons who cannot receive testamentary assignments or donations between living persons from each other are also null.

However, donations between spouses are valid as revocable donations.

**Article 1139:** The execution of revocable donations shall be subject to the rules of article 1000\.

**Article 1140:** By the revocable donation, followed by tradition of the donated things, the donee acquires the rights and contracts the obligations of a usufructuary.

However, they shall not be subject to giving the security for preservation and restitution to which usufructuaries are obliged, unless required by the donor.

**Article 1141:** Revocable donations by singular title are anticipated legacies, and are subject to the same rules as legacies.

Reciprocally, if the testator gives the legatee enjoyment of the bequeathed thing during life, the legacy is a revocable donation.

Revocable donations, including legacies in the case of the preceding paragraph, shall prevail over legacies for which enjoyment has not been given to the legatees during the testator's lifetime, when the property the testator leaves at their death is insufficient to cover them all.

**Article 1142:** A revocable donation of all property or of a share thereof shall be regarded as an institution of heir, which shall only take effect from the donor's death.

However, the donee of all property or of a share thereof may exercise the rights of usufructuary over the species delivered to them.

**Article 1143:** Revocable donations lapse by the mere fact of the donee dying before the donor.

**Article 1144:** Revocable donations are confirmed, and give the property of the donated object, by the mere fact of the donor dying without having revoked them, and without any cause of incapacity or unworthiness sufficient to invalidate an inheritance or legacy having arisen in the donee; except in the case of article 1137, paragraph 2\.

**Article 1145:** Their revocation may be express or tacit, in the same manner as the revocation of inheritances or legacies.

**Article 1146:** The provisions of this paragraph, insofar as they concern compulsory assignees, are subject to the exceptions and modifications to be stated in the title On compulsory assignments.

### § 8\. Of the Right of Accrual

**Article 1147:** A same object being destined to two or more assignees, the portion of one of them, which by the absence of the latter joins the portions of the others, is said to accrue to them.

**Article 1148:** This accrual shall not take place among assignees of different parts or shares into which the testator has divided the assigned object: each part or share shall in such case be considered as a separate object; and there shall be no right of accrual except among co-assignees of the same part or share.

If an object is assigned to two or more persons in equal parts, there shall be a right of accrual.

**Article 1149:** There shall be a right of accrual whether the co-assignees are called in the same clause or in separate clauses of the same testamentary instrument.

If the call is made in two different instruments, the previous call shall be presumed revoked in all the part not common with the later call.

**Article 1150:** Co-assignees in common shall be reputed as a single person to concur with other co-assignees; and the collective person formed by the former shall not be understood to fail except when all these fail.

Co-assignees associated by a copulative expression such as Peter and John, or included in a collective denomination such as the Children of Peter, shall be understood as in common.

**Article 1151:** The co-assignee may retain their own portion and repudiate the one granted to them by accrual; but they may not repudiate the former and accept the latter.

**Article 1152:** The portion that accrues carries all its burdens, except those supposing a personal quality or aptitude of the absent co-assignee.

**Article 1153:** The right of transmission established by article 957 excludes the right of accrual.

**Article 1154:** Co-assignees of usufruct, use, habitation, or a periodic pension, retain the right of accrual as long as they enjoy said usufruct, use, habitation, or pension; and none of these rights is extinguished until the last co-assignee fails.

**Article 1155:** The testator may in all cases prohibit accrual.

### § 9\. Of Substitutions

**Article 1156:** Substitution is vulgar or fiduciary.

Vulgar substitution is that in which an assignee is appointed to occupy the place of another who does not accept, or who, before the assignment is granted to them, fails by death or by another cause that extinguishes their eventual right.

The assignee who once accepted shall not be understood to fail, except if the acceptance is invalidated.

**Article 1157:** A substitution made expressly for some of the cases in which the assignee may fail shall be understood to be made for any of the others in which they may fail; except if the testator has expressed contrary will.

**Article 1158:** Substitution may be of several degrees, such as when a substitute is appointed for the direct assignee, and another for the first substitute.

**Article 1159:** One may substitute one for many and many for one.

**Article 1160:** If three or more assignees are reciprocally substituted, and one of them fails, the portion of the latter shall be divided among the others in proportion to the values of their respective assignments.

**Article 1161:** The substitute of a substitute who fails is understood to be called in the same cases and with the same burdens as the latter, without prejudice to what the testator may have ordered in this regard.

**Article 1162:** If the assignee is a descendant of the testator, the descendants of the assignee shall not for that reason be understood to be substituted to them; except if the testator has expressed contrary will.

**Article 1163:** The right of transmission excludes substitution, and substitution excludes accrual.

**Article 1164:** Fiduciary substitution is that in which a beneficiary is called, who in the event of a condition becomes absolute owner of what another person possessed in fiduciary property.

Fiduciary substitution is governed by the provisions of the title On fiduciary property.

**Article 1165:** If for the case of the beneficiary failing before the condition is fulfilled, one or more substitutes are appointed for them, these substitutions shall be understood as vulgar, and shall be subject to the rules of the preceding articles.

Neither the beneficiary of the first degree, nor any substitute called to occupy their place, transmit their expectation if they fail.

**Article 1166:** Substitution shall not be presumed fiduciary except when the tenor of the disposition manifestly excludes vulgar substitution.

---

## TITLE V: OF COMPULSORY ASSIGNMENTS

**Article 1167:** Compulsory assignments are those the testator is obliged to make, and which are supplied when they have not been made, even to the prejudice of their express testamentary dispositions.

Compulsory assignments are:

1. Maintenance owed by law to certain persons;  
2. Legitimate shares;  
3. The fourth of improvements in the succession of descendants, ascendants, and the spouse.

### § 1\. Of Alimentary Assignments Owed to Certain Persons

**Article 1168:** Maintenance the deceased owed by law to certain persons burdens the hereditary mass; except when the testator has imposed this obligation on one or more participants in the succession.

**Article 1169:** Repealed.

**Article 1170:** Alimentary assignees shall not be obliged to any restitution in reason of debts or burdens encumbering the deceased's estate; but future maintenance that appears disproportionate to the resources of the effective estate may be reduced.

**Article 1171:** Alimentary assignments in favor of persons who by law have no right to maintenance shall be charged to the portion of property the deceased could freely dispose of.

And if those made to compulsory recipients are more substantial than in the circumstances correspond, the excess shall be charged to the same portion of property.

### § 2\. Of the Spousal Portion

**Articles 1172-1180:** Repealed.

### § 3\. Of Legitimate Shares and Improvements

**Article 1181:** A legitimate share is that share of a deceased person's property that the law assigns to certain persons called legitimaries.

Legitimaries are therefore heirs.

**Article 1182:** The following are legitimaries:

1. Children, personally or represented by their descendants;  
2. Ascendants, and  
3. The surviving spouse.

The ascendants of the deceased shall not be legitimaries if the paternity or maternity constituting or from which their relationship derives has been judicially determined against the respective parent's opposition, except in the case of the final paragraph of article 203\. Nor shall the spouse be a legitary who by their fault has given cause for judicial separation.

**Article 1183:** Legitimaries concur and are excluded and represented according to the order and rules of intestate succession.

**Article 1184:** Half of the property, after the deductions indicated in article 959, and the additions to be expressed below, shall be divided by heads or roots among the respective legitimaries, according to the rules of intestate succession; what each receives in that division shall be their rigorous legitimate share.

If there are no descendants with the right to succeed, surviving spouse, nor ascendants, the remaining half is the portion of property the deceased could freely dispose of.

If there are such descendants, spouse, or ascendants, the mass of property, after the said deductions and additions, shall be divided into four parts: two of them, or half of the estate, for rigorous legitimate shares; another fourth, for improvements with which the deceased may have wished to favor their spouse or one or more of their descendants or ascendants, whether or not legitimaries, and another fourth, which they could freely dispose of.

**Article 1185:** To compute the fourths mentioned in the preceding article, all revocable and irrevocable donations made by way of legitimate shares or improvements shall be imaginarily added to the liquid estate, according to the state in which the donated things were found at the time of delivery, but prudentially updating their value to the time of the opening of the succession.

The said fourths refer to this imaginary estate.

**Article 1186:** If the one who had legitimaries at the time has made donations between living persons to strangers, and the value of all of them together exceeds the fourth part of the sum formed by this value and that of the imaginary estate, the legitimaries shall have the right for this excess to also be imaginarily added to the estate, for the computation of legitimate shares and improvements.

**Article 1187:** If the excess is such that it not only absorbs the portion of property the deceased could freely dispose of, but also diminishes the rigorous legitimate shares or the fourth of improvements, the legitimaries shall have the right to restitution of the excessively donated, proceeding against the donees, in an order inverse to the dates of the donations, that is, beginning with the most recent.

The insolvency of a donee shall not burden the others.

**Article 1188:** Only what remains after deducting the pecuniary burden to which the assignment is subject shall be considered a donation.

Moderate gifts, authorized by custom on certain days and cases, nor small manual gifts, shall not be taken into account.

**Article 1189:** If the sum of what has been given by way of legitimate shares does not reach half the imaginary estate, the deficit shall be taken from the property in preference to any other investment.

**Article 1190:** If a legitary does not receive all or part of their legitimate share due to incapacity, unworthiness, disinheritance, or because they have repudiated it, and they have no descendants with the right to represent them, said all or part shall be added to the legitimizing half and shall contribute to form the rigorous legitimate shares of the others.

**Article 1191:** To the rigorous legitimate shares accrues any portion of the property the testator could have disposed of by way of improvements, or with absolute freedom, and has not disposed of, or if they have, the disposition has been without effect.

Thus increased, rigorous legitimate shares are called effective legitimate shares.

If legitimaries concur with non-legitimaries as heirs, over what is prescribed in this article, the rules contained in Title II of this Book shall prevail.

**Article 1192:** The rigorous legitimate share is not susceptible to any condition, term, mode, or burden.

On the rest that has been or is left to legitimaries, except under the form of donations between living persons, the testator may impose whatever burdens they wish; without prejudice to the provisions of article 1195\.

**Article 1193:** If what has been or is given by way of legitimate shares exceeds half the imaginary estate, it shall be charged to the fourth of improvements, without prejudice to being divided in the proportion corresponding among the legitimaries.

If what has been assigned to the surviving spouse is not sufficient to complete the minimum portion corresponding to them according to article 988, the difference shall also be paid with charge to the fourth of improvements.

**Article 1194:** If the improvements (including the excess or difference mentioned in the preceding article, as applicable) do not fit in the fourth part of the imaginary estate, this excess or difference shall be charged to the remaining fourth part, in preference to any object of free disposition to which the deceased may have destined it.

**Article 1195:** Of the fourth of improvements, the donor or testator may make the distribution they wish among their descendants, their spouse, and their ascendants; they may therefore assign to one or more of them the entire said fourth to the exclusion of the others.

Burdens imposed on participants of the fourth of improvements shall always be in favor of the spouse, or of one or more of the testator's descendants or ascendants.

**Article 1196:** If there is no way to complete the legitimate shares and improvements, calculated in accordance with the preceding articles, both shall be reduced proportionally.

**Article 1197:** One who owes a legitimate share may in all cases designate the species in which payment is to be made; but may not delegate this faculty to any person, nor set the value of said species.

**Article 1198:** All legacies, all donations, whether revocable or irrevocable, made to a legitary, who then had the status of such, shall be charged to their legitimate share, unless in the will or in the respective deed or in a later authentic act it appears that the legacy or donation was by way of improvement.

However, expenses incurred for the education of a descendant shall not be taken into account for the computation of legitimate shares, nor of the fourth of improvements, nor of the fourth of free disposition, even if made with the quality of chargeable.

Nor shall presents made to a descendant on the occasion of their marriage, nor other customary gifts, be taken into account for said charges.

**Article 1199:** The accumulation of what has been given irrevocably by way of legitimate shares or improvements, for the computation provided by article 1185 and following, does not benefit hereditary creditors nor assignees who are so by other title than that of legitimate share or improvement.

**Article 1200:** If a donation, revocable or irrevocable, is made by way of legitimate share to a person who was not then a legitary of the donor, and the donee does not subsequently acquire the status of legitary, the donation shall be resolved.

The same shall be observed if the donation was made by way of legitimate share to one who was then a legitary, but later ceased to be so due to incapacity, unworthiness, disinheritance, or repudiation, or because another legitary of better right has arisen.

If the donee has failed in any of these ways, donations chargeable to their legitimate share shall be charged to that of their descendants.

**Article 1201:** The revocable or irrevocable donation made by way of improvement to a person believed to be a descendant or ascendant of the donor and was not, shall be resolved.

The same shall happen if the donee, descendant or ascendant of the donor, has failed due to incapacity, unworthiness, disinheritance, or repudiation.

The revocable donation made by way of improvement to a person believed to be a spouse and was not shall also be resolved, or if they have failed due to incapacity, unworthiness, or repudiation.

**Article 1202:** Donations or testamentary assignments the deceased made to another shall not be charged to a person's legitimate share, except in the case of article 1200, paragraph 3\.

**Article 1203:** Disbursements made for the payment of a legitary's debts, who is a descendant, shall be charged to their legitimate share; but only insofar as they were useful for the payment of said debts.

If the deceased has expressly declared by act between living persons or by will that it is their intention that these expenses not be charged to the legitimate share, in this case they shall be considered as an improvement.

If the deceased in the case of the preceding paragraph has assigned to the same legitary by way of improvement some share of the inheritance or some amount of money, they shall be charged to said share or amount; without prejudice to being valid insofar as they exceed it, as improvement, or as the deceased has expressly ordered.

**Article 1204:** If the deceased has promised by public deed between living persons to their spouse or to any of their descendants or ascendants, who at that time was a legitary, not to donate, nor to assign by will any part of the fourth of improvements, and later contravenes their promise, the favored person shall have the right for the assignees of that fourth to pay them what the fulfillment of the promise would have been worth to them, in proportion to what its infraction benefits them.

Any other stipulations on future succession, between a legitary and the one who owes them the legitimate share, shall be null and void.

**Article 1205:** Fruits of things donated, revocably or irrevocably, by way of legitimate share or improvement, during the donor's lifetime, shall belong to the donee from delivery of them, and shall not appear in the estate; and if the donated things have not been delivered to the donee, fruits shall not belong to them except from the donor's death; unless the donor has irrevocably and authentically donated not only the property, but the usufruct of the donated things.

**Article 1206:** If the donee of species chargeable to their legitimate share or improvement definitively receives an amount not less than the value of the same species, they shall have the right to keep them and demand the balance, and may not oblige the other assignees to change the species, or give them their value in money.

And if they definitively receive an amount less than the value of the same species, and are obliged to pay a balance, they may at their option make this payment in money, or restore one or more of said species, and demand the due pecuniary compensation for the current value of the species they restore exceeding the balance they owe.

### § 4\. Of Disinheritances

**Article 1207:** Disinheritance is a testamentary disposition ordering that a legitary be deprived of all or part of their legitimate share.

Disinheritance that does not conform to the rules expressed in this title shall not be valid.

**Article 1208:** A descendant may only be disinherited for one of the following causes:

1. For having committed serious injury against the testator in their person, honor, or property, or in the person, honor, or property of their spouse, or of any of their ascendants or descendants;  
2. For not having assisted them in a state of dementia or destitution, when able;  
3. For having used force or fraud to prevent them from making a will;  
4. Eliminated;  
5. For having committed a crime deserving afflictive punishment; or for having abandoned themselves to vices or exercised infamous trades; unless it is proven that the testator did not care for the disinherited person's education.

Ascendants and the spouse may be disinherited for any of the first three causes.

**Article 1209:** None of the causes of disinheritance mentioned in the preceding article shall be valid if not specifically expressed in the will, and if not moreover proven judicially during the testator's lifetime, or the persons interested in the disinheritance do not prove it after their death.

However, proof shall not be necessary when the disinherited person does not claim their legitimate share within four years from the opening of the succession; or within four years from the day their incapacity to administer ceased, if at the time the succession opened they were incapable.

**Article 1210:** The effects of disinheritance, if the disinheritor does not expressly limit them, extend not only to legitimate shares, but to all assignments by reason of death and to all donations made to the disinherited person.

But they do not extend to maintenance, except in cases of atrocious injury.

**Article 1211:** Disinheritance may be revoked, like other testamentary dispositions, and revocation may be total or partial; but it shall not be understood to be revoked tacitly by reconciliation having intervened; nor shall the disinherited person be admitted to prove that there was intention to revoke it.

---

## TITLE VI: OF THE REVOCATION AND REFORM OF THE WILL

### § 1\. Of the Revocation of the Will

**Article 1212:** A validly executed will cannot be invalidated except by the testator's revocation.

However, privileged wills lapse without need of revocation, in cases provided by law.

Revocation may be total or partial.

**Article 1213:** A solemn will may be expressly revoked in whole or in part by a solemn or privileged will.

But revocation made in a privileged will shall lapse with the will containing it, and the previous one shall subsist.

**Article 1214:** If the will revoking a previous will is itself revoked, the first will does not revive by this revocation, unless the testator manifests contrary will.

**Article 1215:** A will is not tacitly revoked in all its parts by the existence of another or later wills.

Later wills that do not expressly revoke previous ones shall leave subsisting in the latter the dispositions that are not incompatible with the later ones, or contrary to them.

### § 2\. Of the Reform of the Will

**Article 1216:** Legitimaries to whom the testator has not left what by law corresponds to them shall have the right to have the will reformed in their favor, and may bring the action of reform (they or the persons to whom their rights have been transmitted), within four years from the day they had knowledge of the will and of their status as legitimaries.

If the legitary, at the opening of the succession, did not have the administration of their property, the action of reform shall not prescribe in them before the expiration of four years from the day they take that administration.

**Article 1217:** In general, what by law corresponds to legitimaries and what they have the right to claim by the action of reform is their rigorous legitimate share, or the effective one as applicable.

The legitary who has been unduly disinherited shall have, in addition, the right for donations between living persons included in the disinheritance to subsist.

**Article 1218:** Having been passed over in silence by a legitary shall be understood as an institution of heir in their legitimate share.

They shall also retain revocable donations the testator has not revoked.

**Article 1219:** Legitimaries of the same order and degree shall contribute to form or integrate what by reason of their legitimate share is owed to the claimant.

**Article 1220:** If one who has descendants, ascendants, or spouse disposes of any part of the fourth of improvements in favor of other persons, the legitimaries shall also have the right for the will to be reformed in that regard, and that part be adjudicated to them.

**Article 1221:** Repealed.

---

## TITLE VII: OF THE OPENING OF THE SUCCESSION AND OF ITS ACCEPTANCE, REPUDIATION, AND INVENTORY

### § 1\. General Rules

**Article 1222:** From the moment a succession opens, anyone having an interest in it, or presumed to have, may request that the movable property and papers of the succession be kept under lock and seal until the solemn inventory of the hereditary property and effects proceeds.

Household movable property of daily use shall not be kept under lock and seal, but a list of them shall be made.

The keeping and affixing of seals shall be done by the judge with legal formalities.

**Article 1223:** If the succession property is scattered within the jurisdictional territory of other judges of letters, the judge of letters before whom the succession was opened, at the request of any of the heirs or creditors, shall send letters rogatory to the judges of the other jurisdictional territories, for them to proceed on their part to the keeping and affixing of seals, until the corresponding inventory, if applicable.

**Article 1224:** The cost of keeping and affixing seals and of inventories shall burden all property of the succession, unless they fall determinedly on a part of them, in which case they shall burden that part alone.

**Article 1225:** Every assignee may accept or repudiate freely.

Excepted are persons who do not have the free administration of their property, who may not accept or repudiate except through or with the consent of their legal representatives.

They are prohibited from accepting by themselves, even with benefit of inventory.

The husband shall require the wife's consent, married under the regime of conjugal partnership, to accept or repudiate an assignment granted to her. This authorization shall be subject to the provisions of the last two paragraphs of article 1749\.

**Article 1226:** No assignment may be accepted except after it has been granted.

But after the death of the person whose succession is concerned, every assignment may be repudiated, even if conditional and the condition is pending.

Permission granted by a legitary to the one who owes them the legitimate share to be able to make a will without consideration for it shall be regarded as untimely repudiation, and shall have no value.

**Article 1227:** One may not accept or repudiate conditionally, nor until or from a certain day.

**Article 1228:** One may not accept a part or share of the assignment and repudiate the rest.

But if the assignment made to a person is transmitted to their heirs according to article 957, each of these may accept or repudiate their share.

**Article 1229:** One may accept one assignment and repudiate another; but one may not repudiate the burdened assignment and accept the others, unless they are granted separately by right of accrual or transmission, or of vulgar or fiduciary substitution; or unless the assignee has been granted the faculty to repudiate it separately.

**Article 1230:** If an assignee sells, donates, or transfers in any way to another person the object granted to them, or the right to succeed to it, they are understood to accept by the same fact.

**Article 1231:** The heir who has extracted effects belonging to a succession loses the faculty to repudiate the inheritance, and notwithstanding their repudiation, shall remain heir; but shall have no share in the extracted objects.

The legatee who has extracted objects belonging to a succession loses the rights they may have as legatee over said objects, and not having dominion over them, shall be obliged to restore double.

Both shall also be subject criminally to the penalties corresponding to the crime.

**Article 1232:** Every assignee shall be obliged, by virtue of a claim by any interested person, to declare whether they accept or repudiate; and shall make this declaration within forty days following the day of the claim.

In case of absence of the assignee or of the property being situated in distant places, or other serious reason, the judge may extend this period; but never for more than one year.

During this period, every assignee shall have the faculty to inspect the assigned object; may request the conservatory measures concerning them; and shall not be obliged to pay any hereditary or testamentary debt; but the executor or curator of the vacant estate may be, in their cases.

The heir may also inspect the accounts and papers of the succession during the period.

If the absent assignee does not appear personally or by legal representative in due time, a curator of property shall be appointed to represent them and accept for them with benefit of inventory.

**Article 1233:** The assignee in default of declaring whether they accept or repudiate shall be understood to repudiate.

**Article 1234:** Acceptance, once made with legal requirements, may not be rescinded, except in the case of having been obtained by force or fraud, and in the case of serious injury by virtue of testamentary dispositions of which there was no knowledge at the time of accepting.

This rule extends even to assignees who do not have the free administration of their property.

Serious injury is that which decreases the total value of the assignment by more than half.

**Article 1235:** Repudiation is not presumed by law except in cases provided by law.

**Article 1236:** Those who do not have the free administration of their property may not repudiate a universal assignment, nor an assignment of real property, or of movable property worth more than one centavo, without judicial authorization with knowledge of the case.

**Article 1237:** No person shall have the right to have their repudiation rescinded, unless the same person or their legal representative has been induced by force or fraud to repudiate.

**Article 1238:** Creditors of the one who repudiates to the prejudice of their rights may be authorized by the judge to accept for the debtor. In this case, repudiation is rescinded only in favor of creditors and up to the amount of their credits; and in the surplus, it subsists.

**Article 1239:** The effects of acceptance or repudiation of an inheritance retroact to the moment it was granted.

The same applies to legacies of species.

### § 2\. Particular Rules Relating to Inheritances

**Article 1240:** If within fifteen days of the succession opening the inheritance or a share thereof has not been accepted, nor is there an executor to whom the testator has conferred the holding of the property and who has accepted their charge, the judge, at the request of the surviving spouse, or of any of the relatives or dependents of the deceased, or of another interested person, or ex officio, shall declare the estate vacant; this declaration shall be inserted in a newspaper of the commune, or of the capital of the province or of the capital of the region, if there is none in the commune; and proceedings shall proceed to the appointment of a curator of the vacant estate.

If there are two or more heirs and one accepts, they shall have the administration of all the hereditary property in common, after a solemn inventory; and as their co-heirs successively accept and subscribe to the inventory, they shall take part in the administration.

As long as not all have accepted, the faculties of the heir or heirs administering shall be those of curators of the vacant estate, but they shall not be obliged to give security, unless there is reason to fear that under their administration the property is endangered.

**Article 1241:** Acceptance of an inheritance may be express or tacit. It is express when the title of heir is assumed; and tacit when the heir performs an act that necessarily supposes their intention to accept, and which they would not have had the right to perform except in their capacity as heir.

**Article 1242:** One is understood to assume the title of heir when they do so in a public or private deed, obliging themselves as such heir, or in a judicial proceeding act.

**Article 1243:** Purely conservatory acts, of inspection and urgent provisional administration, are not acts that by themselves suppose acceptance.

**Article 1244:** The alienation of any hereditary effect, even for purposes of urgent administration, is an act of heir, if not authorized by the judge at the request of the heir, who protests that it is not their intention to oblige themselves in such capacity.

**Article 1245:** One who performs an act of heir without prior solemn inventory succeeds in all the transmissible obligations of the deceased in proportion to their hereditary share, even if they impose a burden exceeding the value of the property they inherit.

If a solemn inventory has preceded, they shall enjoy the benefit of inventory.

**Article 1246:** One who at the instance of a hereditary or testamentary creditor has been judicially declared an heir, or condemned as such, shall be understood to be so with respect to other creditors, without need for a new proceeding.

The same rule applies to the judicial declaration of having accepted purely and simply or with benefit of inventory.

### § 3\. Of the Benefit of Inventory

**Article 1247:** The benefit of inventory consists in not making heirs who accept responsible for hereditary and testamentary obligations except up to the total value of the property they have inherited.

**Article 1248:** If of many co-heirs some wish to accept with benefit of inventory and others not, all shall be obliged to accept with benefit of inventory.

**Article 1249:** The testator may not prohibit an heir from accepting with benefit of inventory.

**Article 1250:** Inheritances of the State and of all corporations and public establishments shall be accepted precisely with benefit of inventory.

Inheritances falling to persons who cannot accept or repudiate except through the ministry or with the authorization of others shall be accepted in the same manner.

If the provisions of this article are not complied with, the natural or juridical persons represented shall not be bound by the debts and burdens of the succession except up to the amount of what exists of the inheritance at the time of the claim or is proven to have been effectively employed for their benefit.

**Article 1251:** Fiduciary heirs are obliged to accept with benefit of inventory.

**Article 1252:** Every heir retains the faculty to accept with benefit of inventory as long as they have not performed an act of heir.

**Article 1253:** In the preparation of the inventory, the provisions for guardians and curators in articles 382 and following shall be observed, and what the Code of Procedure prescribes for solemn inventories.

**Article 1254:** If the deceased has been a partner in a society, and by a clause of the contract has stipulated that the society continue with their heirs after their death, the social property shall not for that reason cease to be included in the inventory to be made; without prejudice to the associates continuing to administer them until the expiration of the society, and without security being required from them for that reason.

**Article 1255:** The executor, the curator of the vacant estate, the presumptive testamentary or ab intestato heirs, the legatees, the business associates, the beneficiaries, and every hereditary creditor presenting the title of their credit shall have the right to attend the inventory.

The aforementioned persons may be represented by others exhibiting a public or private deed entrusting them with this charge, when they are not represented by their spouses, guardians, curators, or any other legal representatives.

All these persons shall have the right to challenge the inventory in what they deem inaccurate.

**Article 1256:** The heir who, in preparing the inventory, maliciously omits mentioning any part of the property, however small, or supposes non-existent debts, shall not enjoy the benefit of inventory.

**Article 1257:** One who accepts with benefit of inventory becomes responsible not only for the value of the property they then effectively receive, but for that subsequently arising to the inheritance on which the inventory falls.

The listing and valuation of this property shall be added to the existing inventory with the same formalities as those observed for making it.

**Article 1258:** They shall also be responsible for all credits as if they had effectively collected them; without prejudice to their justifying, for their discharge in due time, what without their fault they have failed to collect, putting at the disposal of the interested parties the insolvent actions and titles.

**Article 1259:** The debts and credits of the beneficiary heir shall not be confused with the debts and credits of the succession.

**Article 1260:** The beneficiary heir shall be responsible even for slight fault for the conservation of the species or certain bodies owed.

The risk of the other property of the succession is their responsibility, and they shall only be liable for the values in which they were valued.

**Article 1261:** The beneficiary heir may at any time relieve themselves of their obligations by abandoning to the creditors the succession property they must deliver in species, and the remaining balance, and obtaining from them or from the judge approval of the account of their administration they must present.

**Article 1262:** Once the succession property, or the part of it that fell to the beneficiary heir, is consumed in payment of debts and burdens, the judge, at the request of the beneficiary heir, shall summon the hereditary and testamentary creditors not covered, by means of three notices in a newspaper of the commune or of the capital of the province or of the capital of the region, if there is none in the commune, for them to receive from said heir the exact and as far as possible documented account of all investments made; and once the account is approved by them, or in case of discord by the judge, the beneficiary heir shall be declared free from all further responsibility.

**Article 1263:** The beneficiary heir who opposes a claim with the exception that the hereditary property or the portion of it that fell to them has already been consumed in payment of debts and burdens must prove it by presenting to the claimants an exact and as far as possible documented account of all investments made.

### § 4\. Of the Petition of Inheritance and Other Actions of the Heir

**Article 1264:** One who proves their right to an inheritance occupied by another person as heir shall have action to have the inheritance adjudicated to them, and the hereditary things restored, both corporeal and incorporeal; and even those of which the deceased was a mere holder, such as depositary, borrower for use, pledge creditor, lessee, etc., and which have not legitimately returned to their owners.

**Article 1265:** The same action extends not only to things that belonged to the deceased at the time of death, but to increases the inheritance has subsequently had.

**Article 1266:** To the restitution of fruits and to the allowance of improvements in the petition of inheritance, the same rules as in the reclamation action shall apply.

**Article 1267:** One who has occupied the inheritance in good faith shall not be responsible for alienations or deteriorations of hereditary things, except insofar as they have made them richer; but having occupied it in bad faith, they shall be responsible for the full amount of alienations and deteriorations.

**Article 1268:** The heir may also use the reclamation action over reclaimable hereditary things that have passed to third parties, and have not been prescribed by them.

If they prefer to use this action, they shall nevertheless retain their right for the one who occupied the inheritance in bad faith to complete what they could not obtain by recourse against third-party possessors, and leave them entirely indemnified; and they shall have the same right against the one who occupied the inheritance in good faith insofar as by the preceding article they are obliged.

**Article 1269:** The right of petition of inheritance expires in ten years. But the putative heir, in the case of the final paragraph of article 704, may oppose the prescription of five years to this action.

---

## TITLE VIII: OF TESTAMENTARY EXECUTORS

**Article 1270:** Testamentary executors or albaceas are those to whom the testator gives the charge of having their dispositions executed.

**Article 1271:** If the testator has not appointed an executor, or the appointed one fails, the charge of executing the testator's dispositions belongs to the heirs.

**Article 1272:** A minor may not be executor. Nor may persons designated in articles 497 and 498\.

**Articles 1273-1274:** Repealed.

**Article 1275:** Supervening incapacity puts an end to the executorship.

**Article 1276:** The judge, at the request of any of the interested parties in the succession, shall fix a reasonable period within which the executor appears to exercise their office, or to excuse themselves from serving it; and the judge may, if necessary, extend the period once.

If the executor is in default of appearing, their appointment shall lapse.

**Article 1277:** The appointed executor may freely decline this office.

If they decline it without proving serious inconvenience, they shall become unworthy to succeed the testator, in accordance with article 971, paragraph 2\.

**Article 1278:** Once they accept the office expressly or tacitly, they are obliged to discharge it, except in cases where it is lawful for an agent to relieve themselves of their agency.

Resignation of the office with legitimate cause deprives them only of a proportionate part of the assignment made to them in compensation for the service.

**Article 1279:** The executorship is not transmissible to the executor's heirs.

**Article 1280:** The executorship is non-delegable, unless the testator has expressly granted the faculty to delegate it.

The executor may, however, appoint agents to act under their orders; but shall be responsible for their operations.

**Article 1281:** If there are many executors, all are jointly and severally liable, unless the testator has exempted them from joint liability, or the same testator or the judge have divided their attributions and each confines themselves to those incumbent upon them.

**Article 1282:** The judge may divide the attributions, in favor of administration, and at the request of any of the executors, or of any of the interested parties in the succession.

**Article 1283:** If there are two or more executors with common attributions, all shall act jointly, in the same manner as provided for guardians in article 413\.

The judge shall resolve any discord that may arise among them.

The testator may authorize them to act separately; but by this sole authorization, they shall not be understood to be exempted from their joint liability.

**Article 1284:** The executor's duty is to watch over the security of the property; to have money, movable property, and papers kept under lock and seal as long as there is no solemn inventory, and to see that this inventory proceeds, with summons of the heirs and of other interested parties in the succession; except if, all heirs being capable of administering their property, they unanimously determine that no solemn inventory be made.

**Article 1285:** Every executor shall be obliged to give notice of the opening of the succession by means of three notices published in a newspaper of the commune, or of the capital of the province or of the capital of the region, if there is none in the commune.

**Article 1286:** Whether the testator has entrusted the executor with payment of their debts or not, the executor shall be obliged to require that in the partition of the property a lot or share sufficient to cover known debts be set aside.

**Article 1287:** Omission of the diligences provided in the two preceding articles shall make the executor responsible for any damage they cause to creditors.

The same obligations and responsibility shall fall on the present heirs who have the free administration of their property, or on the respective guardians or curators, and the husband of the wife heir who is not separated as to property.

**Article 1288:** The executor entrusted with paying hereditary debts shall do so precisely with the intervention of the present heirs or of the curator of the vacant estate, if applicable.

**Article 1289:** Even if the testator has entrusted the executor with payment of their debts, creditors shall always have their action against the heirs, if the executor is in default of paying them.

**Article 1290:** The executor shall pay legacies not imposed on a determined heir or legatee; for which they shall require from the heirs or from the curator of the vacant estate the necessary money and the movable or immovable species in which the legacies consist, if the testator has not left them the holding of the money or species.

Heirs may, however, make payment of said legacies themselves, and satisfy the executor with the respective receipts; unless the legacy consists of a work or act particularly entrusted to the executor and subject to their judgment.

**Article 1291:** If there are legacies for charitable objects, the executor shall give notice of them, with insertion of the respective testamentary clauses, to the public ministry; to whom they shall also denounce the negligence of the heirs or legatees obliged to them, or of the curator of the vacant estate, if applicable.

The public ministry shall judicially pursue the omissive persons, or delegate this management to the defender of pious works.

Of legacies destined for works of religious piety, such as suffrages, anniversaries, chaplaincies, houses of spiritual exercises, ecclesiastical feasts, and other similar, the executor shall inform the public ministry, and the ecclesiastical ordinary, who may in their case implore before the civil authority the necessary judicial measures for those obliged to provide these legacies to fulfill them.

The public ministry, the defender of pious works, and the ecclesiastical ordinary in their case, may also proceed spontaneously to the aforementioned diligence against the executor, heirs, or omissive legatees.

The same right is granted to municipalities with respect to legacies of public utility in which their respective neighborhoods are interested.

**Article 1292:** If payment of bequeathed species is not to be made immediately and there is well-founded fear that they will be lost or deteriorate by negligence of those obliged to give them, the executor whose duty it is to enforce the legacies may require security from them.

**Article 1293:** With the consent of the present heirs, the executor shall proceed to the sale of movable property, and subsidiarily of immovable property, if there is not sufficient money for the payment of debts or legacies; and heirs may oppose the sale by delivering to the executor the money they need for the purpose.

**Article 1294:** The provisions of articles 394 and 412 shall extend to executors.

**Article 1295:** The executor may not appear in court in such capacity, except to defend the validity of the will, or when necessary to carry out the testamentary dispositions incumbent upon them; and in all cases they shall do so with the intervention of the present heirs or of the curator of the vacant estate.

**Article 1296:** The testator may give the executor the holding of any part of the property or of all of them.

The executor shall in this case have the same faculties and obligations as the curator of the vacant estate; but shall not be obliged to give security except in the case of article 1297\.

However, from this holding, the provisions of the preceding articles shall apply.

**Article 1297:** Heirs, legatees, or beneficiaries, in case of just fear about the security of the property of which the executor is holder, and to which they respectively have current or eventual right, may request that due securities be required of them.

**Article 1298:** The testator may not extend the executor's faculties, nor exempt them from their obligations, as they are defined in this title.

**Article 1299:** The executor is responsible even for slight fault in the performance of their office.

**Article 1300:** They shall be removed for gross fault or fraud, at the request of the heirs or of the curator of the vacant estate, and in case of fraud shall be made unworthy to have any part in the succession, and in addition to indemnifying the interested parties for any damage, shall restore everything they have received by way of remuneration.

**Article 1301:** The executor is prohibited from carrying out any disposition of the testador contrary to law, under penalty of nullity and of being considered guilty of fraud.

**Article 1302:** The executor's remuneration shall be what the testator has indicated.

If the testator has not indicated any, it is for the judge to determine it, considering the estate and the more or less laborious nature of the office.

**Article 1303:** The executorship shall last for the certain and determined time fixed by the testator.

**Article 1304:** If the testator has not fixed a time for the duration of the executorship, it shall last one year from the day the executor began to exercise their office.

**Article 1305:** The judge may extend the period fixed by the testator or by law, if serious difficulties arise for the executor to discharge their office within it.

**Article 1306:** The period fixed by the testator or by law, or extended by the judge, shall be understood without prejudice to the partition of the property and its distribution among the participants.

**Article 1307:** Heirs may request the termination of the executorship from the time the executor has discharged their office; even if the period fixed by the testator or by law, or extended by the judge for its performance, has not expired.

**Article 1308:** The existence of legacies or trusts whose day or condition is pending shall not be a reason for the extension of the period, nor for the executorship not to terminate; unless the testator has expressly given the executor the holding of the respective species or of the part of property destined to fulfill them; in which case the executorship shall be limited to this sole holding.

The same applies to debts whose payment was entrusted to the executor, and whose day, condition, or liquidation is pending; and shall be understood without prejudice to the rights conferred on heirs by the preceding articles.

**Article 1309:** The executor, as soon as they cease in the exercise of their office, shall render an account of their administration, justifying it.

The testator may not relieve them of this obligation.

**Article 1310:** The executor, once the accounts are examined by the respective interested parties, and legitimate expenses deducted, shall pay or collect the balance resulting against them or in their favor, according to the provisions for guardians and curators in similar cases.

---

## TITLE IX: OF FIDUCIARY EXECUTORS

**Article 1311:** The testator may make secret and confidential charges to the heir, to the executor, and to any other person, for a certain amount of property of which they can freely dispose to be invested in one or more lawful objects.

The person charged with executing them is called a fiduciary executor.

**Article 1312:** Charges the testator makes secretly and confidentially, and in which some part of their property is to be employed, shall be subject to the following rules:

1. The person of the fiduciary executor must be designated in the will.  
2. The fiduciary executor must have the qualities necessary to be an executor and legatee of the testator; but the quality of secular ecclesiastic shall not be an obstacle, provided they are not in the case of article 965\.  
3. The species or the determined sum to be delivered to them for the fulfillment of their charge must be expressed in the will.

If any of these requirements is lacking, the disposition shall not be valid.

**Article 1313:** No more than half of the portion of property the testator could freely dispose of may be destined to said secret charges.

**Article 1314:** The fiduciary executor must swear before the judge that the charge does not have for its object to cause any part of the testator's property to pass to an incapable person, or to invest it in an unlawful object.

They shall at the same time swear to faithfully and lawfully perform their charge, subjecting themselves to the testator's will.

The taking of the oath must precede the delivery or allowance of the species or monies assigned to the charge.

If the fiduciary executor refuses to take the oath to which they are obliged, the charge shall lapse by the same fact.

**Article 1315:** The fiduciary executor may be obliged, at the instance of a general executor, or of an heir, or of the curator of the vacant estate, and with some just cause, to leave on deposit, or secure a fourth part of what by reason of the charge is delivered to them, to answer with this sum the action of reform or hereditary debts, in cases provided by law.

This sum may be increased, if the judge deems it necessary for the security of the interested parties.

Upon expiration of the four years following the opening of the succession, the remaining part shall be returned to the fiduciary executor, or the security cancelled.

**Article 1316:** The fiduciary executor shall not be obliged in any case to reveal the object of the secret charge, nor to give an account of their administration.

---

## TITLE X: OF THE PARTITION OF PROPERTY

**Article 1317:** None of the co-assignees of a universal or singular thing shall be obliged to remain in indivision; the partition of the assigned object may always be requested provided the co-assignees have not stipulated otherwise.

Indivision may not be stipulated for more than five years, but once this term is completed, the pact may be renewed.

The preceding provisions do not extend to privately owned lakes, nor to servitude rights, nor to things the law orders kept undivided, such as fiduciary property.

**Article 1318:** If the deceased has made the partition by act between living persons or by will, it shall be followed insofar as it is not contrary to the rights of others.

In particular, partition shall be considered contrary to the rights of others if it has not respected the right that article 1337, rule 10, grants to the surviving spouse.

**Article 1319:** If any of the co-assignees is such under suspensive condition, they shall not have the right to request partition while the condition is pending. But the other co-assignees may proceed to it, competently securing for the conditional co-assignee what, upon fulfillment of the condition, corresponds to them.

If the assigned object is a trust, the provisions of the title On fiduciary property shall be observed.

**Article 1320:** If a co-assignee sells or assigns their share to a stranger, the latter shall have the same right as the seller or assignor to request partition and intervene in it.

**Article 1321:** If one of several co-assignees dies after the assignment has been granted to them, any of the heirs of the latter may request partition; but they shall form a single person in it, and may not act except all together or through a common attorney.

**Article 1322:** Guardians and curators, and in general those who administer another's property by disposition of law, may not proceed to the partition of inheritances or of real property in which their wards have a share, without judicial authorization.

But the husband shall not need this authorization to provoke the partition of property in which his wife has a share: the consent of his wife, if she is not unable to give it, or that of the court in subsidium, shall suffice for him.

**Article 1323:** Only lawyers qualified to practice the profession and who have the free disposal of their property may be partitioners.

The grounds for disqualification and recusation that the Organic Code of Courts establishes for judges apply to partitioners.

**Article 1324:** The appointment of a partitioner made by the deceased by public instrument between living persons or by will shall be valid, even if the appointed person is an executor or co-assignee, or is included in any of the grounds for disqualification or recusation established by the Organic Code of Courts, provided they meet the other legal requirements; but any of the interested parties may request the judge where the partition proceeding is to be held to declare the partitioner disqualified for any of these reasons. This request shall be processed in accordance with the rules established for recusations by the Code of Civil Procedure.

**Article 1325:** Co-assignees may make the partition themselves if all concur in the act, even if there are persons among them who do not have the free disposal of their property, provided no questions to resolve arise and all agree on the manner of making the division.

In this case, however, appraisal of the property by experts and approval of the partition by the ordinary courts shall be necessary in the same way as if proceeding before a partitioner.

Co-assignees, even if they do not have the free disposal of their property, may appoint a partitioner by common agreement. This designation may also fall on any of the persons referred to in the preceding article, provided said person meets the other legal requirements.

Partitioners appointed by the interested parties may only be disqualified for causes of disqualification or recusation that have arisen after their appointment.

If they do not agree on the designation, the judge, at the request of any of them, shall proceed to appoint a partitioner who meets the legal requirements, subject to the rules of the Code of Civil Procedure.

**Article 1326:** If any of the co-assignees does not have the free disposal of their property, the appointment of a partitioner not made by the judge must be approved by the judge.

Excepted from this provision is the married woman whose property is administered by the husband; in such case, the consent of the wife, or that of the court in subsidium, shall suffice.

The curator of the absentee's property, appointed in accordance with article 1232, final paragraph, shall represent them in the partition and administer what is adjudicated to them in it, according to the rules of curatorship of property.

**Article 1327:** The partitioner is not obliged to accept this charge against their will; but if appointed in a will and does not accept the charge, the provisions regarding the executor in the same case shall be observed.

**Article 1328:** The partitioner who accepts the charge must declare it, and swear to perform it with due fidelity, and in the shortest possible time.

**Article 1329:** The partitioner's responsibility extends to slight fault; and in the case of prevarication, declared by the competent judge, in addition to being subject to damages and the legal penalties corresponding to the crime, they shall be rendered unworthy in accordance with the provisions for executors of last wills in article 1300\.

**Article 1330:** Before proceeding to partition, controversies over rights to succession by will or ab intestato, disinheritance, incapacity, or unworthiness of assignees shall be decided by the ordinary courts.

**Article 1331:** Questions regarding the property of objects on which someone alleges an exclusive right and which consequently should not enter into the divisible mass shall be decided by the ordinary courts; and the partition shall not be delayed by them. Once decided in favor of the divisible mass, proceedings shall be as in the case of article 1349\.

However, when they concern a considerable part of the divisible mass, the partition may be suspended until they are decided; if the judge, at the request of the assignees to whom more than half of the divisible mass corresponds, so orders.

**Article 1332:** The law sets for the partitioner, to effect the partition, the term of two years from the acceptance of their office.

The testator may not extend this period.

Co-assignees may extend or restrict it as they see fit, even against the testator's will.

**Article 1333:** Common costs of partition shall be for the account of the interested parties, in proportion.

**Article 1334:** The partitioner shall conform in the adjudication of property to the rules of this title; except that the co-assignees lawfully and unanimously agree otherwise.

**Article 1335:** The appraisal value by experts shall be the basis on which the partitioner shall proceed for the adjudication of species; except that the co-assignees have lawfully and unanimously agreed otherwise, or on the bidding of species, in cases provided by law.

**Article 1336:** The partitioner, even in the case of article 1318, and even if not required by the executor or heirs, shall be obliged to form the lot and share expressed in article 1286, and omission of this duty shall make them responsible for any damage with respect to creditors.

**Article 1337:** The partitioner shall liquidate what each of the co-assignees is owed, and shall proceed to the distribution of the hereditary effects, bearing in mind the following rules:

1. Among co-assignees of a species that admits no division, or whose division makes it lose value, the one who offers the most for it shall have better right to the species; any of the co-assignees shall have the right to request the admission of outside bidders; and the price shall be divided among all co-assignees proportionally.  
2. If no one offers more than the appraisal value or the conventional value mentioned in article 1335, and two or more assignees compete on the adjudication of a species, the legitary shall be preferred over the non-legitary.  
3. Portions of one or more estates adjudicated to a single individual shall be, if possible, continuous, unless the adjudicatee consents to receive separate portions, or that from continuity greater harm results to other interested parties than from separation to the adjudicatee.  
4. The same continuity shall be sought between the estate adjudicated to an assignee and another estate of which the same assignee is owner.  
5. In the division of estates, servitudes necessary for their convenient administration and enjoyment shall be established.  
6. If two or more persons are co-assignees of an estate, the partitioner may, with the lawful consent of the interested parties, separate from the property the usufruct, habitation, or use to give them for the account of the assignment.  
7. In the partition of an inheritance or of what remains of it, after the adjudications of species mentioned in the preceding numbers, possible equality must be maintained, adjudicating to each of the co-assignees things of the same nature and quality as to the others, or making shares or lots of the divisible mass.  
8. In forming the lots, not only equivalence but similarity of all of them shall be sought; but care shall be taken not to divide or separate objects that do not admit convenient division or from whose separation harm results; except that the interested parties unanimously and lawfully agree.  
9. Each of the interested parties may challenge the manner of composition of the lots before the draw is made.  
10. However, the surviving spouse shall have the right to have their hereditary share paid with preference by adjudication in their favor of the property of the immovable in which they reside and which is or has been the principal home of the family, as well as the furniture furnishing it, provided they form part of the deceased's estate. If the total value of said property exceeds the spouse's hereditary share, the spouse may request that over things not adjudicated to them in property, rights of habitation and use be constituted in their favor, according to the nature of the things, with a gratuitous and life-long character. The right of habitation shall not be opposable to third parties in good faith until the resolution constituting it is registered in the Registrar of Real Property. In all not provided for, use and habitation shall be governed by the provisions of Title X of Book II. The right to the preferential adjudication mentioned in this rule may not be transferred or transmitted.  
11. Once the provisions of articles 1322 and 1326 are complied with, judicial approval shall not be necessary to carry out what is provided in any of the preceding numbers, even if some or all of the co-assignees are minors or other persons who do not have the free administration of their property.

**Article 1338:** Fruits perceived after the testator's death, during indivision, shall be divided in the following manner:

1. Assignees of species shall have the right to fruits and accessions from the moment the succession opens; except if the assignment has been from a certain day, or under suspensive condition, in which cases fruits shall not be owed except from that day, or from the fulfillment of the condition; unless the testator has expressly ordered otherwise.  
2. Legatees of amounts or kinds shall have no right to any fruits except from the moment the person obliged to provide said amounts or kinds has been in default; and this allowance of fruits shall be at the expense of the defaulting heir or legatee.  
3. Heirs shall have the right to all fruits and accessions of the undivided hereditary mass, in proportion to their shares; deducting, however, the fruits and accessions belonging to assignees of species.  
4. The deduction mentioned in the preceding paragraph shall fall on the fruits and accessions of the entire mass, whenever there is no person directly charged with providing the legacy: if the testator has imposed this burden on some of their assignees, only the latter shall suffer the deduction.

**Article 1339:** Fruits pending at the time of adjudication of species to assignees of shares, amounts, or kinds, shall be regarded as part of the respective species, and shall be taken into account for the estimation of their value.

**Article 1340:** If any of the heirs wishes to take upon themselves a greater share of debts than that corresponding proportionally, under some condition the other heirs accept, they shall be heard.

Hereditary or testamentary creditors shall not be obliged to conform to this arrangement of the heirs to file their claims.

**Article 1341:** If the deceased's estate is confused with property belonging to other persons by reason of the spouse's own property or partnership gains, partnership contracts, previous undivided successions, or other reason, separation of estates shall first proceed, dividing the common species according to the preceding rules.

**Article 1342:** Whenever in the partition of the mass of property, or of a portion of the mass, absent persons who have not appointed attorneys, or persons under guardianship or curatorship, have an interest, it shall be necessary to submit it, once completed, to judicial approval.

**Article 1343:** Once partition is effected, particular titles of objects that fell to them shall be delivered to the participants.

Titles of any object that has suffered division shall belong to the person designated for the purpose by the testator, or in default of this designation, to the person to whom the greater part fell; with the obligation to exhibit them in favor of the other participants, and to allow them to have copies of them when requested.

In case of equality, the competition shall be decided by lot.

**Article 1344:** Each assignee shall be considered to have succeeded immediately and exclusively to the deceased in all effects that fell to them, and never to have had any part in the other effects of the succession.

Consequently, if any of the co-assignees has alienated a thing that in the partition is adjudicated to another of them, proceedings may be as in the case of the sale of another's thing.

**Article 1345:** The participant who is disturbed in the possession of the object that fell to them in the partition, or who suffers eviction from it, shall denounce it to the other participants to concur in stopping the disturbance, and shall have the right for them to warrant the eviction.

This action shall prescribe in four years from the day of eviction.

**Article 1346:** This action does not apply:

1. If the eviction or disturbance arises from a cause subsequent to the partition;  
2. If the warranty action has been expressly renounced;  
3. If the participant has suffered the disturbance or eviction through their fault.

**Article 1347:** Payment of warranty is divided among the participants in proportion to their shares.

The share of the insolvent burdens all in proportion to their shares; including the one to be indemnified.

**Article 1348:** Partitions are annulled or rescinded in the same manner and according to the same rules as contracts.

Rescission for cause of injury is granted to the one who has been harmed by more than half of their share.

**Article 1349:** Having involuntarily omitted some objects shall not be grounds for rescinding the partition. The one in which they were omitted shall be continued later, dividing them among the participants according to their respective rights.

**Article 1350:** The other participants may block one of them from the rescissory action by offering and securing the supplement of their portion in cash.

**Article 1351:** The participant who has alienated their portion in whole or in part may not bring the action for nullity or rescission, except if the partition has suffered from error, force, or fraud, from which they suffer harm.

**Article 1352:** The action for nullity or rescission prescribes with respect to partitions according to the general rules fixing the duration of this kind of action.

**Article 1353:** The participant who does not wish or cannot bring the action for nullity or rescission shall retain the other legal remedies to be indemnified.

---

## TITLE XI: OF THE PAYMENT OF HEREDITARY AND TESTAMENTARY DEBTS

**Article 1354:** Hereditary debts are divided among heirs in proportion to their shares.

Thus, the heir of one-third is not obliged to pay except one-third of the hereditary debts.

But the beneficiary heir is not obliged to pay any share of the hereditary debts except up to the value of what they inherit.

The foregoing is understood without prejudice to the provisions of articles 1356 and 1526\.

**Article 1355:** The insolvency of one of the heirs does not burden the others; except in the cases of article 1287, paragraph 2\.

**Article 1356:** Usufructuary or fiduciary heirs divide the debts with owner or beneficiary heirs according to the provisions of articles 1368 and 1372; and hereditary creditors have the right to direct their actions against them in accordance with the said articles.

**Article 1357:** If one of the heirs is a creditor or debtor of the deceased, only the share of this credit or debt corresponding to their hereditary share shall be confused, and they shall have action against their co-heirs proportionally for the remainder of their credit, and shall be obliged to them proportionally for the remainder of their debt.

**Article 1358:** If the testator divides the hereditary debts among the heirs in a different way than prescribed in the preceding articles, the hereditary creditors may exercise their actions either in accordance with said articles or in accordance with the testator's dispositions, as they see fit. However, in the former case, heirs suffering greater burden than that imposed on them by the testator shall have the right to be indemnified by their co-heirs.

**Article 1359:** The rule of the preceding article applies to the case where by partition or by agreement of the heirs, debts are distributed among them differently than as expressed in the said articles.

**Article 1360:** Testamentary burdens shall not be regarded as a common burden of the heirs except when the testator has not imposed them on some of the heirs or legatees in particular.

Those falling to the heirs in common shall be divided among them as the testator has provided, and if nothing has been said on the division, in proportion to their shares or in the form prescribed by the said articles.

**Article 1361:** Legacies of periodic pensions are owed day by day from the day they are granted, but may not be claimed except at the expiration of the respective periods, which shall be presumed monthly.

However, if the pensions are alimentary, each payment may be demanded from the beginning of the respective period, and there shall be no obligation to restore any part even if the legatee dies before the expiration of the period.
