**Article 1984:** Whenever an estate is leased with livestock and there is no special contrary stipulation about them, all utilities of said livestock shall belong to the lessee, and the livestock themselves, with the obligation to leave on the estate at the end of the lease an equal number of heads of the same ages and qualities.

If at the end of the lease there are not enough animals on the estate of the said ages and qualities to effect restitution, they shall pay the difference in money.

The lessor shall not be obliged to receive animals that are not acclimated to the estate.

**Article 1985:** If there is no fixed time for the duration of the lease, notice shall be given one year in advance to terminate it.

The year shall be understood in the following way: The day of the year on which delivery of the estate to the farmer began shall be regarded as the initial day of all successive years, and the year of notice shall be counted from this initial day, even if the notice was given some time before. The parties may agree to another rule if they deem it convenient.

**Article 1986:** If nothing has been stipulated on the time of payment, the custom of the department shall be observed.

### § 7\. Of the Lease of Domestic Servants

**Articles 1987-1991:** Repealed.

**Article 1992:** If it has been stipulated that for the service to cease it is necessary for one to give notice to the other, the one who contravenes without serious cause shall be obliged to pay the other an amount equivalent to the salary for the notice period or for the days lacking to complete it.

**Article 1993:** Serious cause with respect to the master shall be the servant's ineptitude, any act of infidelity or insubordination, and any habitual vice that prejudices the service or disturbs domestic order; and with respect to the servant, mistreatment by the master, and any attempt by the master or their family or guests to induce them to a criminal or immoral act.

Any contagious disease of one shall give the other the right to terminate the contract.

The master shall have the same right if the servant for any reason becomes unable for service for more than one week.

**Article 1994:** Upon the master's death, the contract shall be understood to subsist with the heirs, and they may not terminate it except as the deceased could have.

**Article 1995:** The person to whom service is provided shall be believed on their word (without prejudice to contrary proof):

1. Regarding the amount of salary;  
2. Regarding payment of the salary for the past month;  
3. Regarding what they say they have given on account for the current month.

### § 8\. Of Contracts for the Making of a Material Work

**Article 1996:** If the artisan provides the material for the making of a material work, the contract is of sale; but it is not perfected except by the approval of the person who ordered the work.

Consequently, the risk of the thing does not belong to the person who ordered the work except from its approval, except if they are in default of declaring whether they approve it or not.

If the material is provided by the person who ordered the work, the contract is of lease.

If the principal material is provided by the person who ordered the work, with the artisan providing the rest, the contract is of lease; in the contrary case, of sale.

The lease of work is subject to the general rules of the contract of lease, without prejudice to the following special rules.

**Article 1997:** If no price has been fixed, it shall be presumed the parties agreed on what is ordinarily paid for the same kind of work, and in default of this, on what is estimated equitable by experts.

**Article 1998:** If it has been agreed to give a third party the faculty to fix the price, and they die before execution of the work begins, the contract shall be null; if after execution has begun, the price shall be fixed by experts.

**Article 1999:** There shall be place for claims for damages, according to general rules of contracts, whenever on either side what was agreed has not been executed, or its execution has been delayed.

Consequently, the person who ordered the work, even in the case of a single total price having been stipulated for it, may terminate it, reimbursing the artisan all costs, and giving them what the work done is worth and what they could have earned in the work.

**Article 2000:** Loss of material falls on its owner.

Consequently, loss of material provided by the person who ordered the work belongs to the latter; and the artisan is not responsible except when the material perishes through their fault or through the fault of persons serving them.

Even if the material does not perish through their fault, nor through the fault of said persons, the artisan may not claim the price or wages, except in the following cases:

1. If the work has been examined and approved;  
2. If it has not been examined and approved due to the default of the person who ordered the work;  
3. If the thing perishes by a defect of the material provided by the person who ordered the work, except if the defect is one the artisan should by their trade have known, or knowing it did not give timely notice.

**Article 2001:** Examination may be made partially when it has been agreed that the work be approved in parts.

**Article 2002:** If the person who ordered the work alleges it has not been properly executed, experts shall be appointed by both parties to decide.

If the allegation of the person who ordered the work is well-founded, the artisan may be obliged, at the option of the person who ordered the work, to do it anew or to damages.

Restitution of materials may be made with others of equal quality or in money.

**Article 2003:** Contracts for construction of buildings, entered into with a contractor who undertakes the entire work for a single fixed price, are additionally subject to the following rules:

1. The contractor may not demand increase of price, under pretext of wages or materials having become more expensive, or additions or modifications having been made to the original plan; except if a particular price has been fixed for said additions or modifications.  
2. If unknown circumstances, such as a hidden defect of the ground, cause costs that could not be foreseen, the contractor must have themselves authorized for them by the owner; and if the owner refuses, may apply to the judge to decide whether the extra work should or should not have been foreseen, and fix the increase of price corresponding for this reason.  
3. If the building perishes or threatens ruin, in whole or in part, in the five years following its delivery, by a defect of construction, or by a defect of the ground that the contractor or the persons employed by them should have known by reason of their trade, or by a defect of materials, the contractor shall be responsible; if the materials have been supplied by the owner, the contractor's responsibility shall not apply except in accordance with article 2000, final paragraph.  
4. The receipt granted by the owner, after the work is completed, only means that the owner approves it as externally adjusted to the plan and to the rules of the art, and does not exempt the contractor from the responsibility imposed by the preceding paragraph.  
5. If the artisans or workers employed in construction of the building have contracted with the owner directly for their respective wages, they shall be regarded as independent contractors, and shall have direct action against the owner; but if they have contracted with the contractor, they shall have no action against the owner except subsidiarily, and up to what the owner owes the contractor.

**Article 2004:** Rules 3, 4, and 5 of the preceding article extend to those undertaking construction of a building as architects.

**Article 2005:** All contracts for construction of a work are resolved by the death of the artisan or contractor; and if there are work or materials prepared that may be useful for the work in question, the person who ordered it shall be obliged to receive them and pay their value; what corresponds in reason of the work done shall be calculated proportionally, taking into consideration the price stipulated for the entire work.

By the death of the person who ordered the work, the contract is not resolved.

### § 9\. Of the Lease of Immaterial Services

**Article 2006:** Immaterial works, or those in which intelligence predominates over manual work, such as a literary composition, or the typographical correction of a printed work, are subject to the special provisions of articles 1997, 1998, 1999, and 2002\.

**Article 2007:** Immaterial services consisting of a long series of acts, such as those of salaried writers for the press, secretaries of private persons, teachers, governesses, actors, and singers, are subject to the following special rules.

**Article 2008:** With respect to each of the partial works in which the service consists, the provisions of article 2006 shall be observed.

**Article 2009:** Either party may terminate the service whenever they wish, or with the notice stipulated.

If the remuneration consists of periodic pensions, either party shall give notice to the other of their intention to terminate the contract, even if no notice has been stipulated in it, and the advance shall be at least half a period.

**Article 2010:** If to provide the service the person providing it has been made to change residence, the other party shall pay reasonable expenses of travel both ways.

**Article 2011:** If the person providing the service withdraws untimely, or their bad conduct gives reason for dismissal, they may not claim anything in reason of notice or travel expenses.

**Article 2012:** The preceding articles apply to services that according to article 2118 are subject to the rules of agency, insofar as they are not contrary to them.

### § 10\. Of the Lease of Transport

**Article 2013:** The lease of transport is a contract in which one party undertakes, for a certain freight or price, to transport or have transported a person or thing from one place to another.

The one undertaking transport is generally called carrier and takes the names of carter, teamster, boatman, shipowner, according to the mode of transport.

The one exercising the industry of having transport of persons or cargoes executed is called transport entrepreneur.

The person sending or dispatching the cargo is called consignor, and the person to whom it is sent, consignee.

**Article 2014:** The obligations imposed here on the carrier are understood to be imposed on the transport entrepreneur, as responsible for the suitability and good conduct of the persons they employ.

**Article 2015:** The carrier is responsible for damage or harm occurring to the person by the bad quality of the carriage, boat, or ship in which transport is made.

They are also responsible for destruction and deterioration of the cargo, unless otherwise stipulated, or if defect of the cargo, force majeure, or fortuitous event is proved.

The carrier's responsibility shall apply not only for their own act, but for that of their agents or servants.

**Article 2016:** The carrier is obliged to deliver the thing in the place and time stipulated, except if they prove force majeure or fortuitous event.

The carrier may not allege force majeure or fortuitous event that could with medium prudence or care have been avoided.

**Article 2017:** The price of a woman's conveyance is not increased by the fact of giving birth during the journey, even if the carrier was ignorant of her pregnancy.

**Article 2018:** The one who has contracted with the carrier for transport of a person or cargo is obliged to pay the price or freight for transport and reparation of damages caused by act or fault of the passenger or their family or servants, or by defect of the cargo.

**Article 2019:** If for any reason the passenger or cargo fails to appear at the due time, the one who dealt with the carrier for transport shall be obliged to pay half the price or freight.

The carrier shall suffer the same penalty if they do not appear in the place and time agreed.

**Article 2020:** The death of the carrier or passenger does not terminate the contract: obligations are transmitted to their respective heirs; without prejudice to the general provisions on force majeure or fortuitous event.

**Article 2021:** The preceding rules shall be observed without prejudice to special rules for the same objects, contained in particular ordinances relating to each kind of traffic and in the Commercial Code.

---

## TITLE XXVII: OF THE CONSTITUTION OF GROUND RENT

**Article 2022:** A ground rent is constituted when a person contracts the obligation to pay another an annual income, recognizing the corresponding capital, and encumbering their property with the responsibility of the income and the capital.

This income is called ground rent or rent; the person who owes it, rent debtor, and their creditor, rent creditor.

**Article 2023:** Ground rent may be constituted by will, by donation, sale, or any other way equivalent to these.

**Article 2024:** Ground rent may only be constituted over rural or urban estates, and with inclusion of the land.

**Article 2025:** The capital must always consist of or be valued in money. Without this requirement, there shall be no constitution of ground rent.

**Article 2026:** The ratio between the rent and the capital may not exceed the rate determined by law.

The maximum of this rate, while the law does not fix another, is four percent per year.

**Article 2027:** The constitution of a ground rent must always appear in a public deed registered in the competent Registry; and without this requirement it shall not be valid as a constitution of ground rent; but the person obliged to pay the pension shall be so in the terms of the will or contract, and the obligation shall be personal.

**Article 2028:** It may not be stipulated that the rent be paid in a certain quantity of fruits. Infringement of this rule shall vitiate the constitution of ground rent.

**Article 2029:** Every ground rent, even stipulated as perpetual, is redeemable.

**Article 2030:** The rent debtor may not be obliged to redeem the ground rent within a certain time. Any stipulation of this kind shall be considered not written.

**Article 2031:** The pact not to alienate the charged estate, nor any other imposing more burdens on the rent debtor than those expressed in this title, is not valid in the constitution of ground rent.

Any contrary stipulation shall be considered not written.

**Article 2032:** The rent debtor shall have the obligation to pay the rent year by year, except if in the constitutive act another period for payments is fixed.

**Article 2033:** The obligation to pay the ground rent always follows ownership of the charged estate, even with respect to rents accrued before acquisition of the estate; saving always the rent creditor's right to proceed against the rent debtor in default, even if they cease to possess the estate, and saving also the warranty action of the new possessor of the estate against whomever it may apply.

**Article 2034:** The rent debtor is not obliged to pay the capital, nor the rents accrued before acquisition of the charged estate, except with this same estate; but for payment of rents due during the time they have been in possession of the estate, they are obliged with all their property.

**Article 2035:** The provisions of the two preceding articles shall apply even if the estate has lost much of its value, or has become entirely unproductive.

But the rent debtor shall be discharged from all obligation by putting the estate, in the state it is in, at the rent creditor's disposal, and paying the rents due according to the rule of the preceding article.

However, if by fraud or gross fault of the rent debtor the estate perishes or becomes unproductive, they shall be liable for damages.

**Article 2036:** Whenever the charged estate is divided by hereditary succession, the ground rent shall be understood to be divided in parts proportional to the values of the shares or new estates resulting from the division.

For the determination of the values of these, they shall be appraised, and the appraisal shall be approved by the judge with hearing of the rent creditor and the public ministry.

The judge shall order registered in the competent Registry, at the expense of each rent debtor, the judgment fixing the portion of capital with which the respective share is to remain encumbered.

Thus shall be constituted as many distinct and independent ground rents, and separately redeemable, as there are encumbered shares.

In the absence of the said registration, the primitive ground rent shall subsist, and each share shall be encumbered with responsibility for the entire ground rent.

If from the division it results that less than one escudo of the primitive capital falls to a share, the ground rent may not be divided, and each share shall be responsible for the entire ground rent.

**Article 2037:** The capital imposed on an estate may in all cases be reduced to a determined part of it, or transferred to another estate, with the formalities and under the conditions prescribed in the preceding article.

Just cause not to accept this transfer or reduction shall be insufficiency of the new estate or share to bear the encumbrance, and the estate or share shall be considered insufficient when the total of encumbrances it is to bear exceeds half its value.

The ground rents and special mortgages with which the estate is already encumbered shall be counted in the encumbrance.

The transfer or reduction shall be made with the formalities indicated above, and in default of them the primitive ground rent shall subsist.

**Article 2038:** Redemption of the ground rent is the consignation of the capital to the judge's order, who consequently shall declare it redeemed.

Once this declaration is registered in the competent Registry, the ground rent is completely extinguished.

The rent creditor shall be obliged to constitute it anew with the consigned capital.

**Article 2039:** The rent debtor who owes no back rents may redeem the ground rent whenever they wish.

**Article 2040:** The ground rent may not be redeemed in parts.

**Article 2041:** The ground rent perishes by complete destruction of the charged estate, complete destruction being understood as that which makes the land disappear entirely.

If the land reappears even only in part, the entire ground rent shall revive; but nothing shall be owed for pensions of the intervening time.

**Article 2042:** The rent creditor's personal action prescribes in five years; and this time expired, none of the rents accrued in it, nor the capital of the ground rent, may be demanded.

**Article 2043:** Of any ground rent belonging to a natural or juridical person, without burden of restitution or transmission, and without any other encumbrance, the rent creditor may dispose between living persons or by will, or transmit it ab intestato, according to general rules.

**Article 2044:** In cases of forced transmission where succession is to be perpetual or up to a designated limit, the order of succession shall be that established by the constitutive act of the ground rent or of the old entailment that has been converted into it; and insofar as said constitutive act has not provided, the regular order of succession described in the following article shall be observed.

**Article 2045:**

1. To the first called shall succeed their descendants degree by degree, personally or by representation, excluding in each degree the older from the younger.  
2. When the direct line expires, a rent debtor dying without descendants having the right to succeed them, one shall ascend to their nearest ascendant of the same line from whom descendants exist, and these shall succeed degree by degree, personally or by representation, excluding in each degree the older from the younger.  
3. Once all the descendants of the first called are extinct, the second and their descendants shall succeed in the same terms.  
4. Once the descendants of all expressly called by the constitutive act are exhausted, no person or line shall be understood to be called to succeed by virtue of a tacit or presumed substitution of any class, and the last rent debtor shall have the faculty to dispose of the ground rent between living persons or by will, or transmit it ab intestato according to general rules.

But this rule ceases in the following two cases:

1. If the ground rent was constituted in subrogation of an old family entailment;  
2. If the ground rent is encumbered in favor of a pious or charitable object.

**Article 2046:** In the first of the cases just indicated, one shall ascend to the founder of the entailment, and the persons who without them would have succeeded them ab intestato shall be understood to be tacitly substituted for those expressly called by them; these substitutes shall give beginning to as many lines, which shall succeed each other according to the regular order of age of the respective stocks; and within each line succession shall likewise be according to the regular order, even if different from that established by the founder for the expressly called lines.

Once all these lines of tacit substitution are exhausted, and the ground rent not being encumbered in favor of a pious or charitable object, no further substitution shall be admitted, and rule 4 of the preceding article shall apply.

**Article 2047:** In the second exceptional case of rule 4 of article 2045, the right of ground rent shall pass to a pious or charitable foundation or establishment chosen by the President of the Republic; and said foundation or establishment shall enjoy the ground rent with the encumbrances to which it is subject.

**Article 2048:** In cases where succession is by lines and with the right of representation, every person called, or excluded from the order of succession by the constitutive act, shall be presumed to be so with all their descendants forever; and this presumption may not be opposed except by express provisions of the constitutive act incompatible with it.

**Article 2049:** When children conceived or born in marriage concur with children born before the marriage of their parents, the age of the latter shall be counted from the day of the marriage. When children born before marriage concur among themselves, the age of each of them shall be counted from the day of their birth.

**Article 2050:** Repealed.

**Article 2051:** When two or more children called to succeed are born of the same birth, without the priority of birth being known, the ground rent shall be divided among them in equal parts, and in each of them succession to the stock shall be according to the constitutive act.

The encumbrance to which the ground rent is subject shall be divided in the same manner.

**Article 2052:** When by the order of succession two ground rents should fall to the same person, and one of them, according to its constitution, is incompatible with the other, the person in whom both fall, by whatever words the clause of incompatibility is conceived, shall have the faculty to choose whichever they wish, and shall be understood to be excluded forever from the other personally and by representation; and in this other succession shall proceed according to the respective constitutive act, as if said person had never existed.

---

## TITLE XXVIII: OF PARTNERSHIP

### § 1\. General Rules

**Article 2053:** Partnership or company is a contract in which two or more persons agree to put something in common with the aim of sharing among themselves the profits arising from it.

Partnership forms a juridical person, distinct from the partners individually considered.

**Article 2054:** In deliberations of partners entitled to vote, the majority of votes shall decide, computed according to the contract, and if nothing has been provided in it, the numerical majority of partners shall decide.

Excepted are cases where law or contract requires unanimity, or grants any partner the right to oppose the others.

Unanimity is necessary for any substantial modification of the contract, except insofar as the same contract provides otherwise.

**Article 2055:** There is no partnership if each partner does not put something in common, whether consisting in money or effects, or in an industry, service, or work appreciable in money.

Nor is there partnership without participation in profits.

Purely moral profit, not appreciable in money, is not understood as profit.

**Article 2056:** Every universal partnership, whether of present and future property, or of one or the other, is prohibited.

Every partnership of profits, by universal title, is likewise prohibited, except between spouses.

However, any amount of property may be put in partnership, specifying it.

**Article 2057:** If a partnership is in fact formed that cannot legally subsist, either as partnership, or as donation, or as any contract, each partner shall have the faculty to demand previous operations be liquidated and their contributions taken out.

This provision shall not apply to partnerships null for the unlawfulness of the cause or object, which shall be governed by the Criminal Code.

**Article 2058:** Nullity of the partnership contract does not prejudice actions corresponding to third parties in good faith against all and each of the associates for the operations of the partnership, if it exists in fact.

### § 2\. Of the Different Species of Partnership

**Article 2059:** Partnership may be civil or commercial.

Commercial partnerships are those formed for businesses that law qualifies as acts of commerce. Others are civil partnerships.

**Article 2060:** It may be stipulated that the partnership contracted, even if not commercial by its nature, be subject to the rules of commercial partnership.

**Article 2061:** Partnership, whether civil or commercial, may be collective, limited partnership, or corporation.

A collective partnership is one in which all partners administer personally or through a common agent.

A limited partnership is one in which one or more partners oblige themselves only up to their contributions.

A corporation is one formed by the gathering of a common fund, provided by shareholders responsible only for their respective contributions and administered by a board of directors composed of essentially revocable members.

**Article 2062:** Limited partners are prohibited from including their names in the firm or company name, and from taking part in administration.

Contravention of either of these provisions shall impose on them the same responsibility as on members of a collective partnership.

**Article 2063:** Collective partnerships may have one or more limited partners, with respect to whom the provisions relating to limited partnerships shall apply, the others being subject among themselves and with respect to third parties to the rules of collective partnership.

**Article 2064:** A corporation is always commercial, even if formed for the realization of civil business.

### § 3\. Of the Principal Clauses of the Partnership Contract

**Article 2065:** If no term or condition is expressed for the partnership to begin, it shall be understood to begin on the date of the same contract; and if no term or condition is expressed for it to end, it shall be understood to be contracted for the entire life of the associates, saving the right of renunciation.

But if the object of the partnership is a business of limited duration, it shall be understood to be contracted for all the time the business lasts.

**Article 2066:** Contractors may fix the rules they deem convenient for the division of profits and losses.

**Article 2067:** Contractors may entrust the division of profits and losses to another's discretion, and no claim may be brought against this, except when manifestly unfair, nor even for this cause shall any claim be admitted against said discretion, if three months have elapsed since it was known to the claimant, or if it has begun to be put into execution by them.

This discretion may not be entrusted to any of the partners.

If the person to whom it was entrusted dies before fulfilling their task, or for any other cause does not fulfill it, the partnership is null.

**Article 2068:** In the absence of express stipulation, the division of profits shall be understood to be in proportion to the values each partner has put into the partnership fund, and the division of losses in proportion to the division of profits.

**Article 2069:** If one of the partners contributes only with their industry, service, or work, and there is no stipulation determining their share in the partnership profits, this share shall be fixed by the judge if necessary; and if no stipulation determines their share in losses, they shall be understood to have no other than that of said industry, work, or service.

**Article 2070:** The distribution of profits and losses shall not be understood either with respect to each partner's management, or with respect to each particular business.

Businesses in which the partnership suffers loss shall be compensated with those in which it reports profit, and the stipulated shares shall fall on the definitive result of the partnership operations.

However, limited partners shall not be obliged to bring into collation dividends received in good faith, and shareholders of corporations shall in no case be obliged to return to the partnership treasury amounts received by way of profit.

### § 4\. Of the Administration of the Collective Partnership

**Article 2071:** The administration of the collective partnership may be entrusted to one or more partners, either by the partnership contract, or by a later unanimously agreed act.

In the former case, the administrative faculties of the partner or partners form part of the essential conditions of the partnership, unless otherwise stated in the same contract.

**Article 2072:** The partner to whom administration was entrusted by the constitutive act of the partnership may not renounce their office, except for cause provided in the constitutive act, or unanimously accepted by the partners.

Nor may they be removed from their office except in cases provided or for serious cause; and such shall be considered that which makes them unworthy of trust or incapable of administering usefully. Any partner may demand removal, justifying the cause.

In the absence of any of the aforesaid causes, renunciation or removal puts an end to the partnership.

**Article 2073:** In the case of just renunciation or just removal of the managing partner designated in the constitutive act, the partnership may continue, provided all partners agree on it and on the designation of a new administrator or that administration belongs in common to all partners.

If there are several managing partners designated in the constitutive act, the partnership may also continue, by unanimous agreement that those remaining exercise the administration.

**Article 2074:** Administration conferred by a later act to the partnership contract may be renounced by the managing partner and revoked by the majority of partners, according to the rules of ordinary agency.

**Article 2075:** The partner to whom administration has been conferred by the partnership contract or by later agreement may act against the opinion of the others; conforming, however, to legal restrictions, and to those imposed on them in the respective mandate.

The majority of partners may, however, oppose any act that has not produced legal effects.

**Article 2076:** If administration is conferred, by the partnership contract or by later agreement, on two or more partners, each of the administrators may execute by themselves alone any administrative act, unless otherwise ordered in the title of their mandate.

If they are prohibited from acting separately, they may not do so even under pretext of urgency.

**Article 2077:** The managing partner must confine themselves to the terms of their mandate, and insofar as it is silent, they are understood not to be permitted to contract in the name of the partnership other obligations, nor to make other acquisitions or alienations than those included in its ordinary course of business.

**Article 2078:** It is the managing partner's duty to care for the conservation, repair, and improvement of objects forming the fixed capital of the partnership; but they may not pledge them, nor mortgage them, nor alter their form, even if the alterations seem convenient.

However, if the alterations were so urgent that they had no time to consult the partners, they shall be considered with respect to them as an officious agent of the partnership.

**Article 2079:** In all that they act within legal limits or with special power from their partners, they shall bind the partnership; acting otherwise, they alone shall be responsible.

**Article 2080:** The managing partner is obliged to render account of their management in the periods designated for the purpose by the act conferring administration, and in default of this designation, annually.

**Article 2081:** If administration has not been conferred on one or more partners, each of them shall be understood to have received from the others the power to administer with the faculties expressed in the preceding articles and without prejudice to the following rules:

1. Any partner shall have the right to oppose another's administrative acts while their execution is pending or they have not produced legal effects.  
2. Each partner may use for their personal use things belonging to the partnership fund, provided they employ them according to their ordinary destination, and without prejudice to the partnership and the just use of others.  
3. Each partner shall have the right to oblige the others to make with them necessary expenses for the conservation of partnership things.  
4. None of the partners may make innovations in immovable property depending on the partnership without the consent of the others.

### § 5\. Of the Obligations of Partners Among Themselves

**Article 2082:** Contributions to the partnership fund may be made in property or in usufruct. In either case, fruits belong to the partnership from the moment of contribution.

**Article 2083:** The partner who even by slight fault has delayed delivery of what they are to put in common shall compensate the partnership for all damages caused by the delay.

This provision includes the partner who delays the industrial service in which their contribution consists.

**Article 2084:** If property is contributed, the risk of the thing belongs to the partnership according to general rules, and the partnership is exempt from the obligation to restore it in species.

If only usufruct is contributed, loss or deterioration of the thing, not imputable to the partnership's fault, shall belong to the partner making the contribution.

If the contribution consists of fungible things, things that deteriorate by use, appraised things, or whose price has been fixed by common agreement, factory materials, or articles of sale belonging to the partnership's business or trade, property shall belong to the partnership with the obligation to restore the partner their value.

This value shall be that the same things had at the time of contribution; but of things contributed appraised, the appraisal shall be owed.

**Article 2085:** One who contributes a certain body in property or usufruct is obliged, in case of eviction, to full warranty for all damage.

**Article 2086:** If by the constitutive act of the partnership a fixed amount is assured to a person offering their industry, to be paid to them in full even if the partnership is in loss, this amount shall be regarded as the price of their industry, and the one exercising it shall not be considered a partner.

If they are assigned a share of the eventual profit, they shall not have the right, as to it, to anything when the partnership is in loss, even if that share was assigned to them as the price of their industry.

**Article 2087:** No partner may be required to make a greater contribution than that to which they are obliged.

But if by a change of circumstances the object of the partnership cannot be obtained without increasing contributions, the partner who does not consent may withdraw, and must do so if their partners require it.

**Article 2088:** No partner, even exercising the fullest administrative faculties, may incorporate a third party into the partnership without their partners' consent; but they may without this consent associate them with themselves, and then a particular partnership shall be formed between them and the third party, which shall only relate to the old partner's share in the first partnership.

**Article 2089:** Each partner shall have the right for the partnership to reimburse them for sums they have advanced with its knowledge, for obligations they have legitimately and in good faith contracted for the partnership's business; and to compensate them for damages that inseparable dangers of their management have caused them.

Each partner shall be obliged to this indemnity in proportion to their partnership interest, and the share of the insolvent shall be divided in the same manner among all.

**Article 2090:** If a partner has received their share of a partnership credit, and their partners cannot later obtain their respective shares of the same credit, by insolvency of the debtor or other reason, the former shall share with the latter what they have received, even if it does not exceed their share and even if in the receipt they imputed it to it.

**Article 2091:** Products of various management activities of partners in the common interest belong to the partnership; and the partner whose management has been more profitable shall not thereby have the right to greater benefit in its product.

**Article 2092:** If a managing partner is creditor of a person who is at the same time debtor of the partnership, and if both debts are demandable, amounts received in payment shall be imputed to both credits proportionally, notwithstanding any other imputation made in the receipt to the prejudice of the partnership.

And if in the receipt the imputation is not to the prejudice of the partnership but of the creditor partner, the receipt shall be followed.

The preceding rules are understood without prejudice to the debtor's right to make imputation.

**Article 2093:** Every partner is responsible for damages even by slight fault caused to the partnership, and may not oppose in compensation the emoluments their industry has procured for the partnership in other businesses, except when this industry does not belong to the partnership fund.

### § 6\. Of the Obligations of Partners with Respect to Third Parties

**Article 2094:** The partner who contracts in their own name and not in the partnership's name does not bind it with respect to third parties, not even by reason of the benefit it derives from the contract; the creditor may only bring against the partnership the actions of the debtor partner.

The partner shall not be understood to contract in the partnership's name unless they express it in the contract, or circumstances manifest it in an unequivocal manner. In case of doubt, they shall be understood to contract in their private name.

If the partner contracts in the partnership's name but without sufficient power, they do not bind it to third parties except in subsidium and up to the benefit the partnership derived from the business.

The provisions of this article include even the partner exclusively charged with administration.

**Article 2095:** If the collective partnership is bound with respect to third parties, the totality of the debt shall be divided among the partners in proportion to their partnership interest, and the share of the insolvent partner shall burden the others.

Partners shall not be understood to be obliged jointly or otherwise than in proportion to their partnership interest, except when so expressed in the title of the obligation, and it has been contracted by all partners or with special power from them.

**Article 2096:** Creditors of a partner have no action over partnership property except by mortgage prior to the partnership, or by later mortgage when contribution of the immovable does not appear by registration in the competent Registry.

They may, however, bring against the partnership the indirect and subsidiary actions granted by article 2094\.

They may also request that assignments made to their debtor on account of partnership profits or contributions or shares be attached in their favor.

**Article 2097:** The responsibility of limited partners or shareholders is regulated by the provisions of § 2 of this title.

### § 7\. Of the Dissolution of Partnership

**Article 2098:** Partnership is dissolved by expiration of the term or by the event of the condition fixed for its end.

It may, however, be extended by unanimous consent of the partners; and with the same formalities as for the primitive constitution.

Co-debtors of the partnership shall not be responsible for acts it initiates during the extension, if they have not acceded to it.

**Article 2099:** Partnership is dissolved by completion of the business for which it was contracted.

But if a certain day has been fixed for the partnership to end, and that day arrives before the business is completed and is not extended, the partnership is dissolved.

**Article 2100:** Partnership is likewise dissolved by its insolvency, and by extinction of the thing or things forming its total object.

If extinction is partial, the partnership shall continue, saving partners' right to demand its dissolution if with the remaining part it cannot continue usefully; and without prejudice to the provisions of the following article.

**Article 2101:** If any partner by their act or fault fails in their promise to put in common the things or industry to which they are obliged in the contract, the others shall have the right to consider the partnership dissolved.

**Article 2102:** If a partner has contributed property of a thing, the partnership subsists even if this thing perishes, unless without it it cannot continue usefully.

If only usufruct has been contributed, loss of the fruit-bearing thing dissolves the partnership, unless the contributing partner replaces it to the satisfaction of the partners, or they determine to continue the partnership without it.

**Article 2103:** Partnership is likewise dissolved by death of any partner, except when by provision of law or by the constitutive act it is to continue among the surviving partners with the deceased's heirs or without them.

But even outside this case, the partnership shall be understood to continue as long as the managing partners do not receive notice of the death.

Even after such notice is received by them, operations initiated by the deceased that do not suppose a peculiar aptitude in them shall be carried out.

**Article 2104:** The stipulation of continuing the partnership with the deceased's heirs is understood in those formed for lease of an immovable, or for mining, and in corporations.

**Article 2105:** Heirs of the deceased partner who are not to enter into partnership with the survivors may only claim what belonged to their author, according to the state of partnership affairs at the time death was known; and shall not participate in subsequent emoluments or losses except insofar as they are consequence of operations already initiated at the time death was known.

If the partnership is to continue with the deceased's heirs, all shall have the right to enter into it, excepting only those who by age or other quality have been expressly excluded by law or contract.

Outside this case, those who do not have the administration of their property shall participate in partnership acts through their legal representatives or through the person having the administration of their property.

**Article 2106:** Partnership likewise expires by supervening incapacity or insolvency of one partner.

The partnership may, however, continue with the incapable or bankrupt, and in such case the curator or the creditors shall exercise their rights in partnership operations.

**Article 2107:** Partnership may expire at any time by unanimous consent of the partners.

**Article 2108:** Partnership may also expire by renunciation of one partner.

However, when the partnership has been contracted for a fixed time, or for a business of limited duration, renunciation shall not have effect if by the partnership contract the faculty to make it was not given, or if there is no serious motive, such as non-execution of another partner's obligations, loss of an intelligent administrator who cannot be replaced among the partners, habitual illness of the renouncing partner that incapacitates them for partnership functions, bad state of their affairs by unforeseen circumstances, or others of equal importance.

**Article 2109:** A partner's renunciation produces no effect except by its notification to all others.

Notification to the partner or partners who exclusively administer shall be understood to be made to all.

Those partners not notified of the renunciation may accept it later if they see fit, or consider the partnership subsisting in the intervening time.

**Article 2110:** Renunciation made in bad faith or untimely is not valid.

**Article 2111:** A partner renounces in bad faith who does so to appropriate a profit that should belong to the partnership; in this case, the partners may oblige them to share with them the profits of the business, or to bear the losses exclusively, if the business has bad success.

They may likewise exclude them from all participation in partnership profits and oblige them to bear their share in losses.

**Article 2112:** A partner renounces untimely who does so when their separation is prejudicial to partnership interests. The partnership shall then continue until completion of pending business in which the renouncing partner's cooperation is necessary.

Even if the partner has an interest in withdrawing, they must wait for an opportune moment.

Effects of renunciation in bad faith indicated in the final paragraph of the preceding article apply to untimely renunciation.

**Article 2113:** The provisions of the preceding articles include the partner who in fact withdraws from the partnership without renunciation.

**Article 2114:** Dissolution of the partnership may not be alleged against third parties except in the following cases:

1. When the partnership has expired by the arrival of the certain day fixed for its termination in the contract;  
2. When notice of dissolution has been given by means of three notices published in a newspaper of the department or of the capital of the province, if there is none in the department;  
3. When it is proved that the third party has had timely notice of it by any means.

**Article 2115:** Once the partnership is dissolved, division of objects composing its assets shall proceed.

Rules relating to partition of hereditary property and to obligations among co-heirs apply to division of partnership assets and to obligations among members of the dissolved partnership, except insofar as they oppose the provisions of this title.

---

## TITLE XXIX: OF AGENCY

### § 1\. Definitions and General Rules

**Article 2116:** Agency is a contract in which one person entrusts the management of one or more businesses to another, who undertakes them for the account and risk of the former.

The person conferring the charge is called the principal or mandant, and the one accepting it, attorney, agent, and in general, agent.

**Article 2117:** Agency may be gratuitous or remunerated.

Remuneration (called fee) is determined by agreement of the parties, before or after the contract, by law, custom, or the judge.

**Article 2118:** Services of professions and careers requiring long studies, or to which the faculty to represent and bind another person with respect to third parties is united, are subject to the rules of agency.

**Article 2119:** A business that concerns the agent alone is mere advice, producing no obligation.

But if this advice is given maliciously, it obliges to indemnification of damages.

**Article 2120:** If the business concerns jointly the one giving the charge and the one accepting it, or either of these two, or both and a third party, or a third party exclusively, there shall be true agency; if the principal acts without the third party's authorization, the quasi-contract of officious agency shall arise between these two.

**Article 2121:** Simple recommendation of another's affairs is not, in general, agency; the judge shall decide, according to circumstances, whether the terms of the recommendation involve agency. In case of doubt, it shall be understood as recommendation.

**Article 2122:** The agent executing in good faith a null agency or who by an imperative need goes beyond the limits of their agency becomes an officious agent.

**Article 2123:** The charge object of agency may be made by public or private deed, by letters, verbally, or in any other intelligible way, and even by a person's tacit acquiescence to management of their affairs by another; but testimonial proof shall not be admitted in court except in accordance with general rules, nor private deed when the laws require an authentic instrument.

**Article 2124:** The contract of agency is considered perfect by the agent's acceptance. Acceptance may be express or tacit.

Tacit acceptance is any act in execution of the agency.

Once accepted, the agent may withdraw, as long as the principal is still in a position to execute the business themselves or entrust it to another person. Otherwise, they shall become responsible in the terms of article 2167\.

**Article 2125:** Persons who by their profession or trade undertake others' affairs are obliged to declare as soon as possible whether they accept or not the charge an absent person makes to them; and after a reasonable time, their silence shall be regarded as acceptance.

Even if they excuse themselves from the charge, they shall take urgent conservatory measures required by the business entrusted to them.

**Article 2126:** There may be one or more mandants, and one or more agents.

**Article 2127:** If two or more agents are constituted, and the mandant has not divided the management, the agents may divide it among themselves; but if they have been prohibited from acting separately, what they do in this way shall be null.

**Article 2128:** If an adult minor is constituted agent, acts executed by the agent shall be valid with respect to third parties insofar as they bind them and the mandant; but the agent's obligations to the mandant and third parties may not take effect except according to the rules relating to minors.

**Article 2129:** The agent is responsible even for slight fault in fulfillment of their charge.

This responsibility falls more strictly on the remunerated agent.

On the contrary, if the agent has manifested repugnance to the charge, and has been in a certain way forced to accept it, yielding to the principal's insistence, the responsibility falling on them shall be less strict.

**Article 2130:** If the agency includes one or more businesses especially determined, it is called special; if given for all the mandant's businesses, it is general; and it shall be equally so if given for all, with one or more determined exceptions.

Administration is in all cases subject to the following rules.

### § 2\. Of the Administration of the Agency

**Article 2131:** The agent shall strictly adhere to the terms of the agency, outside cases where the laws authorize them to act otherwise.

**Article 2132:** Agency does not naturally confer on the agent more than the power to effect acts of administration; such as paying debts and collecting credits of the mandant, belonging one and the other to the ordinary administrative course; pursuing debtors in court, bringing possessory actions and interrupting prescriptions, concerning said course; contracting repairs of things they administer; and purchasing materials necessary for cultivation or benefit of lands, mines, factories, or other objects of industry entrusted to them.

For all acts outside these limits, they shall need special power.

**Article 2133:** When the agent is given the faculty to act as seems most convenient to them, they shall not be understood to be authorized to alter the substance of the agency, nor for acts requiring special powers or clauses.

By the clause of free administration, it shall only be understood that the agent has the faculty to execute those acts the laws designate as authorized by said clause.

**Article 2134:** Correct execution of the agency includes not only the substance of the business entrusted, but the means by which the mandant intended it to be carried out.

Equivalent means may, however, be employed if necessity obliges it and the object of the agency is fully obtained in that way.

**Article 2135:** The agent may delegate the charge if not prohibited; but not being expressly authorized to do so, they shall be responsible for the delegate's acts as for their own.

This responsibility shall apply even when they have been expressly granted the faculty to delegate, if the mandant has not designated the person, and the delegate was notoriously incapable or insolvent.

**Article 2136:** Unauthorized or not expressly or tacitly ratified delegation by the mandant gives no right to third parties against the mandant for the delegate's acts.

**Article 2137:** When delegation to a determined person has been expressly authorized by the mandant, a new agency is constituted between the mandant and the delegate, which may only be revoked by the mandant, and is not extinguished by the death or other accident occurring to the previous agent.

**Article 2138:** The mandant may in all cases exercise against the delegate the actions of the agent who has conferred the charge.

**Article 2139:** In the agent's incapacity to donate, light gratifications customarily given to servants are not naturally included.

**Article 2140:** The acceptance expressed by the agent of what is owed to the mandant shall not be regarded as the mandant's acceptance except when the thing or amount delivered has been sufficiently designated in the agency, and what the agent has received corresponds in all to the designation.

**Article 2141:** The faculty to transact does not include that to arbitrate, nor vice versa.

**Article 2142:** Special power to sell includes the faculty to receive the price.

**Article 2143:** The faculty to mortgage does not include that to sell, nor vice versa.

**Article 2144:** The agent may not, personally or through an interposed person, buy things the mandant has ordered them to sell, nor sell of their own to the mandant what the latter has ordered them to buy, except with the mandant's express approval.

**Article 2145:** Charged with borrowing money, they may lend it themselves at the interest designated by the mandant, or in default of this designation, at current interest; but authorized to place money at interest, they may not borrow it for themselves without the mandant's approval.

**Article 2146:** The agent may not place the mandant's money at interest without their express authorization.

Placing it at a higher interest than that designated by the mandant, they must pay it to them in full, except if authorized to appropriate the excess.

**Article 2147:** In general, the agent may take advantage of circumstances to carry out their charge with greater benefit or less burden than those designated by the mandant; provided that in other respects they do not depart from the terms of the agency. They are prohibited from appropriating what exceeds the benefit or reduces the burden designated in the agency.

On the contrary, if they negotiate with less benefit or more burden than those designated in the agency, the difference shall be imputable to them.

**Article 2148:** Faculties granted to the agent shall be interpreted with somewhat more latitude when they are not in a position to consult the mandant.

**Article 2149:** The agent must refrain from fulfilling the agency whose execution would be manifestly pernicious to the mandant.

**Article 2150:** The agent who finds themselves in the impossibility of acting in accordance with their instructions is not obliged to constitute themselves as officious agent; it suffices to take conservatory measures circumstances require.

But if it is not possible to refrain from acting without gravely compromising the mandant, the agent shall take the course closest to their instructions and most convenient to the business.

It is incumbent on the agent to prove the force majeure or fortuitous event preventing them from carrying out the mandant's orders.

**Article 2151:** The agent may, in the exercise of their office, contract in their own name or in the mandant's name; if they contract in their own name, they do not bind the mandant with respect to third parties.

**Article 2152:** The agent may by a special pact assume responsibility for the solvency of debtors and all uncertainties and difficulties of collection. They then become principal debtor to the mandant, and even fortuitous events and force majeure are their account.

**Article 2153:** Metallic species the agent has in their power on the mandant's account perish for the agent even by force majeure or fortuitous event, except if contained in closed and sealed boxes or bags on which the accident or force falls, or if by other unequivocal means identity can be incontestably proved.

**Article 2154:** The agent who has exceeded the limits of their agency is only responsible to the mandant; and is not responsible to third parties except:

1. When they have not given them sufficient knowledge of their powers;  
2. When they have obliged themselves personally.

**Article 2155:** The agent is obliged to render account of their administration.

Important items of their account shall be documented if the mandant has not relieved them of this obligation.

Relief from rendering accounts does not exempt the agent from charges the mandant proves against them.

**Article 2156:** The agent owes the mandant current interest on the mandant's money used for their own benefit.

They likewise owe interest on the balance resulting against them from accounts, from the time they have been in default.

**Article 2157:** The agent is responsible both for what they have received from third parties by reason of the agency (even if not owed to the mandant), and for what they have failed to receive through their fault.

### § 3\. Of the Mandant's Obligations

**Article 2158:** The mandant is obliged:

1. To provide the agent with what is necessary for execution of the agency;  
2. To reimburse them for reasonable expenses caused by execution of the agency;  
3. To pay them the stipulated or usual remuneration;  
4. To pay them advances of money with current interest;  
5. To indemnify them for losses incurred without fault, and by reason of the agency.

The mandant may not exempt themselves from fulfilling these obligations by alleging that the business entrusted to the agent has not had good success, or that it could have been performed at less cost; except if they prove fault.

**Article 2159:** The mandant who does not fulfill on their part what they are obliged to, authorizes the agent to withdraw from their charge.

**Article 2160:** The mandant shall fulfill obligations contracted in their name by the agent within the limits of the agency.

The mandant shall, however, be obliged if they have expressly or tacitly ratified any obligations contracted in their name.

**Article 2161:** When by the terms of the agency or by the nature of the business it appears that it should not have been partially executed, partial execution shall not bind the mandant except insofar as it benefits them.

The agent shall be liable for non-execution of the rest in accordance with article 2167\.

**Article 2162:** The agent may retain effects delivered to them on the mandant's account for security of the payments to which the mandant is obliged on their part.

### § 4\. Of Termination of Agency

**Article 2163:** Agency terminates:

1. By fulfillment of the business for which it was constituted;  
2. By expiration of the term or by the event of the condition fixed for termination of the agency;  
3. By revocation by the mandant;  
4. By renunciation by the agent;  
5. By death of the mandant or agent;  
6. By having the status of debtor in a concursal liquidation proceeding, the mandant or agent;  
7. By interdiction of either;  
8. Repealed.  
9. By cessation of the mandant's functions, if the agency was given in exercise of them.

**Article 2164:** Revocation by the mandant may be express or tacit. Tacit is the entrusting of the same business to another person.

If the first agency is general and the second special, the first agency subsists for businesses not included in the second.

**Article 2165:** The mandant may revoke the agency at their discretion, and revocation, express or tacit, produces its effect from the day the agent has had knowledge of it; without prejudice to the provisions of article 2173\.

**Article 2166:** The mandant who revokes shall have the right to demand from the agent restitution of instruments placed in their hands for execution of the agency; but of pieces that may serve the agent to justify their acts, they must give them a copy signed by their hand if the agent demands it.

**Article 2167:** The agent's renunciation shall not put an end to their obligations except after a reasonable time for the mandant to provide for the businesses entrusted.

Otherwise, they shall be responsible for damages the renunciation causes the mandant; unless they are in the impossibility of administering by illness or other cause, or without serious prejudice to their own interests.

**Article 2168:** Once the mandant's death is known, the agent shall cease their functions; but if suspending them causes harm to the mandant's heirs, they shall be obliged to finish the management begun.

**Article 2169:** Agency destined to be executed after the mandant's death is not extinguished by the mandant's death. The heirs succeed in this case in the mandant's rights and obligations.

**Article 2170:** The mandatary's heirs who are competent for the administration of their property shall give immediate notice of their death to the mandant, and shall do in favor of the latter what they can and circumstances require: omission in this respect shall make them responsible for damages.

The same responsibility shall be subject to executors, guardians, and curators, and all those who succeed in the administration of the deceased or incapacitated agent's property.

**Article 2171:** If the wife has conferred an agency before marriage, the agency subsists; but the husband may revoke it at their discretion whenever it refers to acts or contracts relating to property whose administration corresponds to the husband.

**Article 2172:** If there are two or more agents and by the constitution of the agency they are obliged to act jointly, the failure of one of them by any of the aforesaid causes shall put an end to the agency.

**Article 2173:** In general, whenever the agency expires by a cause unknown to the agent, what they have done in execution of the agency shall be valid and shall give third parties in good faith the right against the mandant.

The mandant shall likewise be obliged, as if the agency subsisted, to what the agent, knowing the cause that has made it expire, has agreed with third parties in good faith; but shall have the right for the agent to indemnify them.

When the fact that has given cause to the expiration of the agency has been notified to the public by newspapers, and in all cases where the third party's ignorance does not appear probable, the judge in their prudence may acquit the mandant.

---

## TITLE XXX: OF LOAN FOR USE

**Article 2174:** Loan for use is a contract in which one party delivers to the other gratuitously a species, movable or real, for them to make use of it, and with the obligation to restore the same species after the use is ended.

This contract is not perfected except by tradition of the thing.

**Article 2175:** The contract of loan for use may be proved by witnesses, whatever the value of the thing lent.

**Article 2176:** The lender retains over the thing lent all rights they had before, but not their exercise insofar as incompatible with the use granted to the borrower.

**Article 2177:** The borrower may not use the thing except in the agreed use, or in default of agreement, in the ordinary use of things of their kind.

In case of contravention, the lender may demand reparation of all damage, and immediate restitution, even if a term for restitution has been stipulated.

**Article 2178:** The borrower is obliged to employ the greatest care in conservation of the thing, and is responsible even for the slightest fault.

They are therefore responsible for any deterioration not arising from nature or legitimate use of the thing; and if this deterioration is such that the thing is no longer susceptible of use in its ordinary use, the lender may demand the previous price of the thing, abandoning their property to the borrower.

But they are not responsible for fortuitous event, except:

1. When they have used the thing in an improper use or have delayed its restitution, unless it appears or is proved that the deterioration or loss by fortuitous event would have equally occurred without the illegitimate use or default;  
2. When the fortuitous event has occurred by their fault, even if slightest;  
3. When in the alternative of saving from an accident the lent thing or their own, they have deliberately preferred their own;  
4. When they have expressly made themselves responsible for fortuitous events.

**Article 2179:** Notwithstanding the provisions of the preceding article, if the loan is for the benefit of both parties, the borrower's responsibility shall not extend except to slight fault, and if for the lender's benefit alone, to gross fault.

**Article 2180:** The borrower is obliged to restore the lent thing at the agreed time; or in default of agreement, after the use for which it was lent.

But restitution may be demanded even before the stipulated time, in three cases:

1. If the borrower dies, unless the thing was lent for a particular service that cannot be deferred or suspended;  
2. If an unforeseen and urgent need of the thing arises for the lender;  
3. If the service for which the thing was lent has ended or does not take place.

**Article 2181:** Restitution shall be made to the lender, or to the person having the right to receive it in their name according to general rules.

If the thing was lent by an incapable person who used it with their legal representative's permission, its restitution to the incapable person shall be valid.

**Article 2182:** The borrower may not excuse themselves from restoring the thing, retaining it for security of what the lender owes them, except in the case of article 2193\.

**Article 2183:** The borrower shall not have the right to suspend restitution, alleging that the lent thing does not belong to the lender; except if it has been lost, stolen, or robbed from its owner, or judicially attached in the borrower's hands.

If a lost, stolen, or robbed thing has been lent, the borrower knowing it and not denouncing it to the owner, giving them a reasonable period to claim it, shall become responsible for damages resulting to the owner from restitution.

And if the owner does not claim it opportunely, restitution may be made to the lender.

The owner, on their part, may not demand restitution without the lender's consent, or without judge's decree.

**Article 2184:** The borrower is obliged to suspend restitution of any species of offensive weapons and any other thing they know is to be used criminally; but must put them at the judge's disposal.

The same shall be observed when the lender has lost judgment and lacks a curator.

**Article 2185:** The obligation to restore ceases from the time the borrower discovers they are the true owner of the lent thing.

However, if the lender disputes ownership, they must restore; unless they are in a position to prove summarily that the lent thing belongs to them.

**Article 2186:** Obligations and rights arising from loan for use pass to the heirs of both contractors, but the borrower's heirs shall not have the right to continue in use of the lent thing, except in the exceptional case of article 2180, number 1\.

**Article 2187:** If the borrower's heirs, not having knowledge of the loan, have alienated the lent thing, the lender (not being able or not wishing to use the reclamation action, or being ineffective) may demand from the heirs that they pay them the fair price of the lent thing or assign the actions arising from the alienation, as they see fit.

If they had knowledge of the loan, they shall compensate all damage, and may even be criminally prosecuted according to circumstances of the fact.

**Article 2188:** If the thing does not belong to the lender and the owner claims it before the loan ends, the borrower shall have no action for damages against the lender; except if the lender knew the thing was another's and did not warn the borrower.

**Article 2189:** If the thing has been lent to many, all are jointly responsible.

**Article 2190:** Loan for use is not extinguished by the lender's death.

**Article 2191:** The lender is obliged to indemnify the borrower for expenses made without their prior notice for conservation of the thing, under the following conditions:

1. If the expenses have not been ordinary conservation expenses, such as feeding the horse;  
2. If they have been necessary and urgent, so that it was not possible to consult the lender, and it is reasonably presumed that having the thing in their power they would not have failed to make them.

**Article 2192:** The lender is obliged to indemnify the borrower for damages caused by the bad quality or condition of the lent object, provided the bad quality or condition has these three circumstances:

1. That it be of such a nature that it would probably cause the damages;  
2. That it was known and not declared by the lender;  
3. That the borrower could not with medium care know it or prevent the damages.

**Article 2193:** The borrower may retain the lent thing as long as indemnity mentioned in the two preceding articles is not paid; except if the lender secures payment of the amount in which they are condemned.

**Article 2194:** Loan for use takes the title of precarious if the lender reserves the faculty to demand restitution of the lent thing at any time.

**Article 2195:** Precarious is understood when the thing is not lent for a particular service nor a time for its restitution is fixed.

Precarious also constitutes holding of another's thing, without prior contract and by ignorance or mere tolerance of the owner.

---

## TITLE XXXI: OF LOAN FOR CONSUMPTION

**Article 2196:** Loan for consumption is a contract in which one party delivers to the other a certain quantity of fungible things with the obligation to return the same amount of the same kind and quality.

**Article 2197:** The contract of loan for consumption is not perfected except by tradition, and tradition transfers ownership.

**Article 2198:** If fungible things other than money have been lent, the same quantity of things of the same kind and quality shall be returned, whether their price has fallen or risen in the interval. And if this is not possible or not required by the creditor, the borrower may pay what they are worth in the time and place payment should have been made.

**Article 2199:** Repealed.

**Article 2200:** If no term for payment has been fixed, there shall be no right to demand it within ten days following delivery.

**Article 2201:** If it has been agreed that the borrower pay when possible, the judge, considering circumstances, may fix a term.

**Article 2202:** If the one who lent had no right to alienate, the species may be reclaimed as long as their identity appears.

Once identity disappears, the one who received them in bad faith shall be obliged to immediate payment with the maximum interest the law permits to stipulate; but the borrower in good faith shall only be obliged to payment with stipulated interest and after the term granted in article 2200\.

**Article 2203:** The lender is responsible for damages the borrower suffers by the bad quality or hidden defects of the lent thing, under the conditions expressed in article 2192\.

If the hidden defects were such that, known, the contract would probably not have been celebrated, the borrower may demand rescission.

**Article 2204:** The borrower may pay the entire sum lent, even before the stipulated term, except if interest has been agreed.

**Article 2205:** Interest may be stipulated in money or fungible things.

**Article 2206:** Conventional interest has no other limits than those designated by special law; except that, the law not limiting it, it exceeds by half that proved to have been current interest at the time of the agreement, in which case it shall be reduced by the judge to said current interest.

**Article 2207:** If interest is stipulated generally without determining the rate, legal interest shall be understood.

**Article 2208:** If interest has been paid, even if not stipulated, it may not be repeated nor imputed to principal.

**Article 2209:** If interest has been stipulated and the lender has given receipt for the principal, without expressly reserving interest, it shall be presumed paid.

**Article 2210:** Repealed.

---

## TITLE XXXII: OF DEPOSIT AND SEQUESTRATION

**Article 2211:** Deposit is called in general the contract in which a corporeal thing is entrusted to a person who undertakes to keep it and restore it in species.

The deposited thing is also called a deposit.

**Article 2212:** The contract is perfected by delivery the depositor makes of the thing to the depositary.

**Article 2213:** Delivery may be made in any way transferring holding of what is deposited.

The parties may also agree that one of them retain as a deposit what was in their power by another cause.

**Article 2214:** Deposit is of two kinds: deposit properly called, and sequestration.

### § 1\. Of Deposit Properly Called

**Article 2215:** Deposit properly called is a contract in which one party delivers to the other a corporeal and movable thing for them to keep it and restore it in species at the depositor's will.

**Article 2216:** Error about the personal identity of either contractor, or about the substance, quality, or quantity of the deposited thing, does not invalidate the contract.

The depositary, however, having suffered error about the depositor's person, or discovering that keeping the deposited thing brings danger to them, may immediately restore the deposit.

**Article 2217:** When according to general rules this contract must be executed in writing, and this formality has been omitted, the depositary shall be believed on their word, whether as to the fact of the deposit itself, or as to the deposited thing, or as to the fact of restitution.

**Article 2218:** This contract cannot have full effect except between persons capable of contracting.

If the depositor is not capable, the depositary shall nevertheless contract all obligations of such.

And if the depositary is not capable, the depositor shall only have action to claim the deposited thing while in the depositary's power, and in the absence of this circumstance, shall only have personal action against the depositary up to the amount in which they have become richer by the deposit; their right against third-party possessors remaining; and without prejudice to the penalty the laws impose on the depositary in case of fraud.

**Article 2219:** Deposit properly called is gratuitous.

If remuneration is stipulated for mere custody of a thing, the deposit degenerates into lease of service, and the one providing the service is responsible even for slight fault; but in all other respects they are subject to the depositary's obligations and enjoy the rights of such.

**Article 2220:** By mere deposit, the depositary is not granted the faculty to use the deposited thing without the depositor's permission.

This permission may sometimes be presumed, and it is left to the judge's discretion to qualify circumstances justifying the presumption, such as relationships of friendship and confidence between the parties.

This permission is more easily presumed in things not appreciably deteriorating by use.

**Article 2221:** In deposit of money, if not in a closed chest whose key the depositor has, or with other precautions making it impossible to take without breaking, it shall be presumed that using it is permitted, and the depositary shall be obliged to restore an equal amount in the same currency.

**Article 2222:** The parties may stipulate that the depositary be responsible for every kind of fault.

In the absence of stipulation, they shall be responsible only for gross fault.

But they shall be responsible for slight fault in the following cases:

1. If they have offered themselves spontaneously or have claimed to be preferred to another person as depositary;  
2. If they have some personal interest in the deposit, whether because they are permitted to use it in certain cases, or because remuneration is granted to them.

**Article 2223:** The obligation to keep the thing includes respecting the seals and locks of the package containing it.

**Article 2224:** If seals have been broken or locks forced by the depositary's fault, the depositor's declaration as to the number and quality of the deposited species shall be followed; but if there is no fault of the depositary, proof shall be necessary in case of disagreement.

The depositary's fault is presumed in any case of breaking or forcing.

**Article 2225:** The depositary must not violate the secrecy of a confidential deposit, nor may they be obliged to reveal it.

**Article 2226:** Restitution is at the depositor's will.

If a time for restitution is fixed, this clause shall only be binding on the depositary, who by virtue of it may not return the deposit before the stipulated time; except in determined cases the laws express.

**Article 2227:** The obligation to keep the thing lasts until the depositor asks for it; but the depositary may require the depositor to dispose of it, when the term stipulated for the duration of the deposit expires, or when, even without the term expiring, the deposit is in danger in their power or causes them harm.

And if the depositor does not dispose of it, it may be consigned at their expense with legal formalities.

**Article 2228:** The depositary is obliged to restitution of the same individual thing or things entrusted to them in deposit, even if they consist of money or fungible things; except in the case of article 2221\.

**Article 2229:** The deposited thing must be restored with all its accessions and fruits.
