An action or right is said to prescribe when it is extinguished by prescription.

**Article 2493:** The one wishing to take advantage of prescription must allege it; the judge may not declare it ex officio.

**Article 2494:** Prescription may be renounced expressly or tacitly; but only after fulfilled.

It is renounced tacitly when the one who can allege it manifests by an act of theirs that they recognize the owner's or creditor's right; for example, when, the legal conditions of prescription being fulfilled, the possessor of the thing takes it on lease, or the debtor pays interest or requests a term.

**Article 2495:** Only the one who can alienate may renounce prescription.

**Article 2496:** The surety may oppose to the creditor the prescription renounced by the principal debtor.

**Article 2497:** Rules relating to prescription apply equally in favor of and against the State, churches, municipalities, national establishments and corporations, and private individuals having the free administration of their property.

### § 2\. Of Prescription by Which Things Are Acquired

**Article 2498:** Dominion of corporeal real or movable property in human commerce, possessed with legal conditions, is gained by prescription.

Other real rights not specially excepted are gained in the same way.

**Article 2499:** Omission of acts of mere faculty, and mere tolerance of acts from which no encumbrance results, do not confer possession, nor give foundation to any prescription.

Thus the one who for many years failed to build on their land does not thereby confer on their neighbor the right to prevent them from building.

Likewise, the one who tolerates their neighbor's livestock passing through their uncultivated lands or grazing on them does not thereby impose the servitude of this transit or pasturage.

Acts of mere faculty are those anyone can execute on their own without need of another's consent.

**Article 2500:** If a thing has been possessed successively and without interruption by two or more persons, the predecessor's time may or may not be added to the successor's time, according to the provisions of article 717\.

Possession begun by a deceased person continues in the vacant estate, which is understood to possess in the heir's name.

**Article 2501:** Uninterrupted possession is that which has suffered no natural or civil interruption.

**Article 2502:** Natural interruption is:

1. When without possession having passed to other hands, exercise of possessory acts has become impossible, such as when an estate has been permanently flooded;  
2. When possession has been lost by another person entering into it.

Natural interruption of the first kind produces no other effect than discounting its duration; but natural interruption of the second kind causes all time of previous possession to be lost; unless possession has been legally recovered, in accordance with the provisions of the title On possessory actions, in which case there shall be understood to have been no interruption for the dispossessed.

**Article 2503:** Civil interruption is any judicial recourse brought by the one claiming to be the true owner of the thing against the possessor.

Only the one who has brought this recourse may allege interruption; and not even they in the following cases:

1. If notification of the claim has not been made in legal form;  
2. If the petitioner expressly withdrew the claim or the proceeding was declared abandoned;  
3. If the defendant obtained an acquittal judgment.

In these three cases, prescription shall be understood not to have been interrupted by the claim.

**Article 2504:** If the property belongs in common to several persons, everything that interrupts prescription with respect to one of them interrupts it also with respect to the others.

**Article 2505:** Against a registered title, acquisitive prescription of real property or of real rights constituted over it shall not apply except by virtue of another registered title; nor shall it begin to run except from the registration of the second.

**Article 2506:** Acquisitive prescription is ordinary or extraordinary.

**Article 2507:** To gain ordinary prescription, regular and uninterrupted possession is needed during the time the laws require.

**Article 2508:** The time necessary for ordinary prescription is two years for movable property and five years for real property.

**Article 2509:** Ordinary prescription may be suspended without extinguishing; in that case, once the cause of suspension ceases, the possessor is credited with the time prior to it, if any.

Ordinary prescription is suspended in favor of the following persons:

1. Minors; the demented; the deaf or deaf-mutes who cannot make themselves clearly understood; and all those under paternal authority, or under guardianship or curatorship;  
2. The married woman in conjugal partnership as long as it lasts;  
3. The vacant estate.

Prescription is not suspended in favor of the woman judicially separated from her husband, nor of the one subject to the regime of separation of property, with respect to those they administer.

Prescription is always suspended between spouses.

**Article 2510:** Dominion of commercial things not acquired by ordinary prescription may be acquired by extraordinary prescription, under the following rules:

1. For extraordinary prescription, no title is necessary.  
2. Good faith is presumed as a matter of law in it, notwithstanding the lack of an acquisitive title of dominion.  
3. But the existence of a title of mere holding shall create a presumption of bad faith, and shall not give place to prescription, unless these two circumstances concur: a. That the one claiming to be owner cannot prove that in the last ten years their dominion has been expressly or tacitly recognized by the one alleging prescription; b. That the one alleging prescription proves having possessed without violence, clandestinity, or interruption for the same space of time.

**Article 2511:** The lapse of time necessary to acquire by this kind of prescription is ten years against any person, and is not suspended in favor of those enumerated in article 2509\.

**Article 2512:** Real rights are acquired by prescription in the same manner as dominion, and are subject to the same rules, excepting the following exceptions:

1. The right of inheritance and the right of ground rent are acquired by extraordinary prescription of ten years.  
2. The right of servitude is acquired according to article 882\.

**Article 2513:** The judicial sentence declaring a prescription shall serve as a public deed for the property of real property or of real rights constituted over it; but shall not be valid against third parties without competent registration.

### § 3\. Of Prescription as a Means of Extinguishing Judicial Actions

**Article 2514:** Prescription extinguishing another's actions and rights requires only a certain lapse of time, during which said actions have not been exercised.

This time is counted from when the obligation became demandable.

**Article 2515:** This time is in general three years for executive actions and five for ordinary actions.

The executive action becomes ordinary by the lapse of three years, and once converted into ordinary, shall last only another two.

**Article 2516:** Mortgage action, and others proceeding from an accessory obligation, prescribe together with the obligation to which they accede.

**Article 2517:** Every action by which a right is claimed is extinguished by acquisitive prescription of the same right.

**Article 2518:** Prescription extinguishing another's actions may be interrupted, either naturally or civilly.

It is interrupted naturally by the debtor's act of recognizing the obligation, whether expressly or tacitly.

It is interrupted civilly by judicial claim; excepting cases enumerated in article 2503\.

**Article 2519:** Interruption operating in favor of one of several co-creditors does not benefit the others, nor that operating to the prejudice of one of several co-debtors prejudice the others, unless there is joint liability, and it has not been renounced in the terms of article 1516\.

**Article 2520:** Prescription extinguishing obligations is suspended in favor of persons enumerated in numbers 1 and 2 of article 2509\.

After ten years have elapsed, the suspensions mentioned in the preceding paragraph shall not be taken into account.

### § 4\. Of Certain Actions Prescribing in Short Time

**Article 2521:** Actions in favor of or against the Treasury and Municipalities arising from all kinds of taxes prescribe in three years.

Fees of judges, lawyers, attorneys; those of doctors and surgeons; of directors or professors of schools and colleges; of engineers and surveyors, and in general, of those exercising any liberal profession, prescribe in two years.

**Article 2522:** The action of merchants, suppliers, and artisans for the price of articles sold at retail prescribes in one year.

That of all kinds of persons for the price of services rendered periodically or accidentally; such as innkeepers, carriers, messengers, barbers, etc.

**Article 2523:** Prescriptions mentioned in the two preceding articles run against all kinds of persons, and admit no suspension.

They are interrupted:

1. From the time a promissory note or written obligation intervenes, or grant of term by the creditor;  
2. From the time a demand intervenes.

In both cases, the short-term prescription is succeeded by that of article 2515\.

**Article 2524:** Short-term prescriptions to which special actions arising from certain acts or contracts are subject are mentioned in the respective titles, and also run against every person; except if expressly otherwise established.

---

## FINAL TITLE: OF THE OBSERVANCE OF THIS CODE

**Final Article:** This Code shall begin to govern from January 1, 1857, and on that date pre-existing laws on all matters treated in it shall be repealed, even in the part not contrary to it.

However, pre-existing laws on proof of obligations, judicial proceedings, preparation of public instruments, and duties of ministers of faith shall only be understood to be repealed insofar as contrary to the provisions of this Code.

---

# LAW ON REGISTRY CIVIL

## TITLE I: GENERAL PROVISIONS

**Article 1:** Registrations of births, marriages, deaths, and other acts and contracts relating to the civil status of persons shall be made in the Civil Registry, by the officials determined by this law.

**Article 2:** The Civil Registry shall be kept in duplicate and shall be divided into three books, named:

1. Of births;  
2. Of marriages; and  
3. Of deaths.

**Article 3:** In the book of births shall be registered:

1. Births occurring in the territory of each commune. The father or mother, when requesting this registration, may request that, along with noting the commune where their child was born, the commune or locality where the mother of the newborn was domiciled be registered in the same record, which shall be recorded as the child's place of origin;  
2. Births occurring during travel within the Republic's territory or at sea, in the commune where the travel ends or in the first port of arrival;  
3. Births of children of Chileans occurring abroad, the father or mother being in the service of the Republic. These births shall be registered before the respective Chilean consul, who shall send the background information to the Ministry of Foreign Relations, which shall certify the authenticity of the documents and send them to the Civil Registry Registrar for purposes of registration in the Registry of the First Section of the commune of Santiago. Children of Chileans born abroad, not in the case of the preceding paragraph, may also be registered in the Chilean Civil Registry in the manner provided in said paragraph;  
4. Public deeds of adoption, those extinguishing it, and final judgments putting an end to it or declaring its nullity.

**Article 4:** In the book of marriages shall be registered:

1. Marriages celebrated in the territory of each commune before a Civil Registry Officer or before the minister of worship authorized by any of the religious entities referred to in article 20 of the Civil Marriage Law;  
2. Marriages celebrated in article of death within the Republic's territory in the commune corresponding to the place where this act occurs;  
3. Marriages celebrated outside the country by a Chilean with a foreigner or between two Chileans shall be registered in the Registry of the First Section of the commune of Santiago. To effect this registration, either of the contracting parties shall send the corresponding background information, duly legalized, to the Ministry of Foreign Relations. This Department shall verify the authenticity of the documents and send them to the Civil Registry Registrar, who shall order registration in the corresponding Registry; and  
4. Final judgments declaring the nullity of marriage or decreeing judicial separation or divorce; separation of property of spouses; instruments stipulating matrimonial agreements, and final judgments granting the wife or a curator the extraordinary administration of the conjugal partnership, and those declaring the husband's interdiction. These sub-registrations may also be requested from the Civil Registry Registrar, who shall order sub-registration in the book of the corresponding commune.

**Article 5:** In the book of deaths shall be registered:

1. Deaths occurring in the territory of each commune;  
2. Deaths occurring during travel, in the commune of the place where burial is to take place. If death occurs at sea, in the first port of arrival of the ship;  
3. Deaths of Chileans occurring abroad. To effect this registration, the duly legalized documents shall be sent to the Ministry of Foreign Relations. This Department shall verify the authenticity of the documents and send them to the Civil Registry Registrar, to order registration in the Registry of the corresponding commune. Deaths of children of Chileans occurring abroad may likewise be registered in the manner provided above;  
4. Deaths of military personnel in campaign, in the commune corresponding to the deceased's last domicile; and  
5. Final judgments declaring presumed death, in the commune corresponding to the court making the declaration.

**Article 6:** The following acts shall be sub-registered in the margin of the birth registration of the child to whom they refer:

1. Instruments by which the child is recognized or by which that recognition is repudiated;  
2. Judgments granting the claim to disclaim paternity of the child born before one hundred and eighty days following marriage expire;  
3. Judgments determining filiation, or granting the challenge of determined filiation;  
4. Agreements of parents relating to the personal care of the child or to the exercise of parental authority;  
5. Judicial resolutions ordering the personal care of the child, decreeing suspension of parental authority, or granting judicial emancipation;  
6. Judgments annulling the act of recognition or repudiation, and  
7. Other documents the laws order sub-registered in the margin of the birth registration.

**Article 7:** Final judgments ordering rectification of any record shall be registered in the respective book. Of this sub-registration, note shall be taken in the margin of the registration to be rectified.

**Article 8:** Judicial judgments and instruments that, in accordance with this law, must be registered or sub-registered in the registries, may not be enforced in court without the preceding registration or sub-registration.

Births, marriages, and deaths occurring abroad and whose registration is not authorized by the preceding articles shall be registered in the respective books of the National Civil Registry when necessary to effect any registration or annotation prescribed by law. These registrations shall be made in the Registry of the First Section of the commune of Santiago, for which the birth, marriage, or death certificate legalized shall be exhibited to the corresponding Civil Registry Officer.

**Article 9:** Registrations shall be made in numerical order, one after another, abbreviations shall be omitted, and amounts or dates shall be expressed in letters.

**Article 10:** Civil Registry books shall be numbered and sealed in each sheet with the dry seal of the Civil Registry Registrar. They shall be opened with a certificate of the officer expressing the number of sheets they contain and closed with another certificate indicating the number of registrations stamped and any particularity that may influence the substance of the registrations, conducive to preventing substitution or other fraud.

For each registration, a page shall be reserved in whose right margin the corresponding sub-registrations shall be annotated.

**Article 11:** Once the books are closed, one copy of them shall be sent to the Civil Registry Registrar for archiving. This official, within fifteen days, shall make observations they deem convenient, which shall be brought to the knowledge of the corresponding Civil Registry Officer and the Judge of Letters of the respective department for civil or criminal responsibilities they deem applicable.

**Article 12:** Every registration shall express:

1. The place, day, month, and year in which it is made;  
2. The name, surnames, age, profession, and domicile of the appearing parties;  
3. The circumstance that the appearing parties are known to the Civil Registry Officer or the manner in which personal identity has been accredited;  
4. The nature of the registration;  
5. The signature of the appearing parties in both registries, expressing, if they cannot, the reason they do not sign; and leaving, in this latter case, the fingerprint of the thumb of their right hand or, in default, of any other finger; and  
6. The signature of the Civil Registry Officer. This signature shall be stamped in both registries immediately upon completion of the registration. If not so done, the omitting official shall suffer the penalty of suspension from employment for up to three months and a fine of one-thousandth to one escudo. In case of recurrence, they shall be punished with loss of employment.

**Article 13:** Civil Registry Officers shall require from the appearing parties the information the General Directorate of Statistics requires for demographic purposes.

**Article 14:** The Civil Registry Officer shall limit themselves to receiving the declarations of the appearing parties, making observations if they declare evidently erroneous facts. But if they insist, the declarations shall be admitted and recorded as made, together with the Civil Registry Officer's observations, without prejudice to actions belonging against false declarants.

**Article 15:** Interested parties in a registration may be represented through an agent. The person presenting themselves in such capacity, stating they have received verbal commission, shall be considered an agent. If the Civil Registry Officer has doubts about the charge, they may require either proof of the power or the appearance of the persons referred to in articles 29 and 45\. The power to contract marriage must be granted in the manner indicated by article 103 of the Civil Code.

The provisions of the preceding paragraph shall not apply to the registrations referred to in article 20 of the Civil Marriage Law.

**Article 16:** Witnesses presented by interested parties for the purposes of a registration may be relatives or strangers.

The following may not be witnesses:

1. Persons under eighteen years of age;  
2. Persons under interdiction for dementia;  
3. Persons currently deprived of reason;  
4. The blind, the deaf, and the mute;  
5. Persons prosecuted or convicted for crimes punishable by more than four years imprisonment;  
6. Persons convicted of perjury;  
7. Foreigners not domiciled in Chile; and  
8. Persons who do not understand the Spanish language.

**Article 17:** Registrations may not be altered or modified except by final judicial judgment.

Notwithstanding the foregoing, the General Director of the National Civil Registry may order, administratively, rectification of registrations containing omissions or manifest errors.

Likewise, the Director may order, ex officio or at the request of a party, rectification of a registration in which appears sub-registered the recognition of a child or the judgment determining their filiation, with the sole object of assigning to the registrant the surname(s) corresponding to them and the names and surnames of the father, mother, or both, as applicable.

Manifest omissions or errors shall be understood to be all those that arise from the mere reading of the respective registration or of the background information that gave rise to it or complement it.

Rectifications ordered administratively shall be exempt from tax.

These rectifications shall be made in accordance with the provisions of article 104 of the Organic Regulations of the Civil Registry, approved by Decree with Force of Law No. 2,128 of August 10, 1930\.

**Article 17 bis:** Any person of legal age may, once, and in the form provided by this article, request before the Civil Registry and Identification Service the change of order of surnames determined in their birth registration.

The request referred to in the preceding paragraph shall indicate the new order of surnames with which the requesting person wishes to be identified, as well as the express request to rectify the registries with which they have been identified in the Civil Registry and Identification Service, whatever the support on which they are kept in said Service.

In the case of foreigners, they may only request the change of order of their surnames for purposes of issuance or rectification of their Chilean documents, fulfilling the requirements established in this law and accompanying documentation proving their stay in Chile. For this, they must previously register their birth in the Santiago office of the Civil Registry and Identification Service.

Without prejudice to the foregoing, persons currently prosecuted or formally charged, or against whom there are pending arrest or detention orders, or who are subject to other personal precautionary measures, or who have been convicted of a crime or simple offense deserving afflictive punishment, may not request the change of order of surnames provided in this article, without prejudice to the possibility of making the corresponding request in accordance with the rules contained in Law No. 17,344, which authorizes change of names and surnames in the cases it indicates and modifies Law No. 4,808 on Civil Registry, whose consolidated text was established by article 4 of Decree with Force of Law No. 1 of 2000, of the Ministry of Justice, provided it does not concern persons convicted of any of the crimes established in Paragraphs V and VI of Title Seven of Book Two of the Criminal Code.

Once the request is entered with the Civil Registry and Identification Service, the identity of the applicant shall be verified through their valid identity card or, if necessary, through fingerprint, or according to the procedure provided in article 92 of Decree with Force of Law No. 2,128 of 1930, of the Ministry of Justice, approving the organic regulations of the Civil Registry Service. In the case of a foreign applicant, compliance with the requirements set forth in paragraphs three and four of this article shall be verified.

Likewise, the National Director of the Civil Registry and Identification Service shall send official letters to Carabineros de Chile, the Investigative Police of Chile, and the Public Prosecutor's Office, for them to report whether the applicant is currently prosecuted or formally charged, or has pending convictions, or there are pending arrest or detention orders against them, or they are subject to other personal precautionary measures. In turn, the National Director of the Civil Registry and Identification Service shall arrange for review of information in the General Registry of Convictions and the Police Record, regulated in Decree Law No. 645 of 1925, of the Ministry of Justice, to verify whether the applicant has convictions, of which record shall be kept in the request file.

Once the reports referred to in the preceding paragraph are available, the National Director of the Civil Registry and Identification Service shall issue the corresponding service order, which may accept, reject, or declare inadmissible the request. In addition to the circumstances set forth in the preceding paragraphs, the request shall be rejected when the applicant does not prove their identity or compliance with the requirements indicated in this article. The resolutions shall contain the decision, which shall be reasoned. In accordance with the provisions of paragraph 4 of article 41 of Law No. 19,880, establishing the bases of administrative procedures governing acts of State Administration bodies, resolutions shall express the resources available against them, the administrative or judicial body before which they must be filed, and the period for filing them, without prejudice to interested parties being able to exercise any other they deem appropriate.

The National Director of the Civil Registry and Identification Service shall declare inadmissible the request made by a person who has not reached the age of majority or when the applicant is currently prosecuted or formally charged, or there are pending arrest or detention orders against them, or they are subject to other personal precautionary measures, or have been convicted of a crime or simple offense deserving afflictive punishment, in which case they shall inform them about the procedure contained in Law No. 17,344. Likewise, they shall declare inadmissible the request when the applicant has been convicted of any of the crimes established in Paragraphs V and VI of Title Seven of Book Two of the Criminal Code, in which case the procedure contained in Law No. 17,344 shall not apply.

**Article 17 ter:** Once the applicant's request is accepted, the Civil Registry and Identification Service shall proceed to make the pertinent modifications and sub-registrations, after which new identification documents shall be issued.

For such purposes, the interested party shall be summoned to appear personally to pick up the new identity documents, which shall replace, for all legal purposes, the previous identity documents.

The original identity documents may not be used, requested, or exhibited under any circumstance and in any public or private entity, without prejudice to the provisions of Law No. 19,628, on protection of private life.

Rectification of the birth record and identification documents shall not affect the applicant's national unique role number, which shall be maintained for all legal purposes.

The change of order of surnames shall only operate with respect to the applicant, without extending to ascendants, and shall not alter filiation. The change of order of the applicant's surnames shall cause the change of the respective transmission surname to minor children, proceeding equally with respect to all of them. Without prejudice to the foregoing, if the applicant has one or more children over 14 and under 18 years of age, they must manifest their consent, by written declaration made before the Official of the Civil Registry and Identification Service, in which case it shall also proceed equally with respect to all minor children. When it thus corresponds to proceed with the change of the respective transmission surname to minor children, the National Director of the Civil Registry and Identification Service, in the service order accepting the request for change of order of surnames of the applicant, must also order the corresponding rectifications in the birth records of all minor children, proceeding with modifications, pertinent sub-registrations, issuance of new identification documents, and reports to institutions, in accordance with the rules of this article.

Adult children of the person obtaining the change of order of their surnames by the procedure of this law or by the procedure of Law No. 17,344, authorizing the change of names and surnames, may request before the Civil Registry and Identification Service the change of the respective transmission surname, in which case rectification of the birth record, modifications, pertinent sub-registrations, issuance of new identification documents, and reports to institutions shall proceed in accordance with the rules of this article.

The Civil Registry and Identification Service shall inform of the rectification of the birth record and issuance of new documents, especially, to the following institutions, as applicable:

a) To the Electoral Service. b) To the Internal Revenue Service. c) To the General Treasury of the Republic. d) To the Investigative Police of Chile. e) To Carabineros de Chile. f) To Gendarmerie of Chile. g) To the Health Superintendency, for it to inform the Health Care Institution in which the applicant contributes, which shall record the new order of the contributor's surnames registered by said institution. h) To the Pensions Superintendency, for it to inform the respective Pension Fund Administrator or the Social Security Institute, according to where the applicant contributes, of the change of order of their surnames, which shall be registered by the respective pension institution. i) To the National Health Fund, for it to register the change of order of the applicant's surnames. j) To the Ministry of Education. k) To the Council of Rectors of Chilean Universities (CRUCH). l) To the Corporation of Private Universities (CUP). m) To the Council of Private Higher Education Institutions (CONIFOS). n) To associations of notaries, registrars, and judicial archivists, for them to inform their associates of the change of order of the applicant's surnames. ñ) To the municipalities.

The interested party may request, with reasoned grounds, the Civil Registry and Identification Service to inform another public or private institution of the rectification of the birth record and issuance of new documents, indicating the reasons justifying said communication.

Any information or communication between institutions, whether public or private, shall be treated in accordance with Law No. 19,628, on protection of private life.

**Article 17 quáter:** The legal effects of the rectification of the order of the applicant's surnames made by virtue of the provisions of articles 17 bis and 17 ter above shall be opposable to third parties from the moment the rectified registration is extended in accordance with article 104 of Decree with Force of Law No. 2,128 of 1930, of the Ministry of Justice, approving the organic regulations of the Civil Registry Service. Once the record is rectified, the applicant who has obtained the change of order of their surnames may only use in the future, in all their actions, their surnames in the form in which they have been rectified. Without prejudice to the foregoing, the corresponding rectification shall be published at the applicant's expense, in extract in the Official Gazette of the 1st or 15th of the month or the following business day if not published on those dates. The extract shall necessarily contain the identification of the applicant and the indication of the surnames they will use.

Rectification in the birth record shall not affect the ownership of patrimonial rights and obligations that may correspond to the person prior to registration of the change, nor shall it affect those arising from family law relationships in all their orders and degrees, which shall remain unmodified.

Rectification in the birth record shall also not affect guarantees, rights, and health benefits or others that may correspond to the person prior to registration of the change.

Use of surnames in the primitive order and the use of surnames in the form in which they have been rectified to exempt, impede, hinder, or evade fulfillment of any obligation shall be punished with the penalty of lower imprisonment in its minimum degree.

**Article 18:** Only persons to whom the registration refers, their legal representatives, or their heirs may request rectification of a registration.

The judge shall proceed with knowledge of the case and shall decide based on public instruments constituting civil status proving the error. In the absence of these instruments, they shall decide after summary information and hearing of relatives in the form prescribed in the Code of Civil Procedure.

If opposition is raised by a legitimate adversary, the matter shall become contentious and shall be subject to the proceedings of the corresponding lawsuit.

Whether opposition has occurred or not, the judge before issuing judgment shall hear the General Directorate of the National Civil Registry, for which they shall send them the complete background information.

However, the judge shall omit said procedure when the request for rectification of records is based on recognitions of children or when it concerns correcting errors or omissions that have the character of manifest, in the terms of the preceding article. In this case, the judge must leave record of this fact in the judgment, stating the cause of the omission.

**Article 19:** The sub-registration made to comply with what is judicially resolved shall be annotated in the margin of the respective record, and must be signed and dated by the Civil Registry Officer in both registries, if they are in their power.

If the registry is closed, the annotation may be requested from the corresponding Civil Registry Officer or from the Civil Registry Registrar. The official making the annotation must communicate it, within three days, to the official in whose power the other copy of the registry is, to proceed to make the same annotation.

**Article 20:** Copies issued by Civil Registry Officers shall contain all registrations and sub-registrations corresponding to the person to whom this document refers. However, certificates relating to one or more facts appearing in a registration may be requested, and in this case, express testimony of this circumstance shall be left in the same certificate.

Notwithstanding the foregoing, in certificates issued by Civil Registry Officers relating to birth records of recognized children, no record shall be left of the sub-registrations referred to, except upon express request that said sub-registration(s) be recorded.

**Article 21:** If one or both copies of a registry are lost or destroyed, or if it is noticed that some of its records are missing or have deficiencies, the General Director of the National Civil Registry shall order in writing the Chief of the General Archive of the Service or the respective Civil Officer, as applicable, to replace the registries or for the omitted or defective records to be extended or completed, according to the following rules:

a) If all copies of a registry are missing or it is noticed that some registrations or sub-registrations do not exist or are incomplete, they shall be reconstituted with the help of fragments or remains remaining of the primitive registries or records, of the background information filed in the respective files of registrations or sub-registrations, indexes, certificates, and any other proof issued by the Service. Statistical cards, lists, or bulletins, burial permits, and any other background information allowing faithful restoration of the primitive registration or sub-registration may also be considered for the purposes indicated in the preceding paragraph.

b) If only one copy of the registry is preserved, or if both exist but one of them lacks one or more records, the reconstitution of these or of the former shall be made by copying the existing records or copy. The authenticity and purity of the copy or record to serve as the basis for reconstitution must always be previously established, for which purpose the elements or background information mentioned in the preceding letter may be used. If the defects noted in the existing registry do not allow extending the copy referred to in the first paragraph of this letter, the General Director of the National Civil Registry shall order, with the indicated background information or elements, rectification by administrative means of the respective registration.

c) Reconstitution of a registration may only be made when the accumulated background information allows recording the district, number, and year of the registration, the names and surnames of the registered persons or of the contracting parties, and the date of the fact or act that motivated it.

d) As long as the requirements indicated in the preceding letter are not met, the registration shall not be extended, but the background information that has been gathered shall be annotated in the sub-registration box, mentioning the person or persons to whom they may correspond. Said annotations shall not constitute proof of civil status, but may serve as a basis for a presumption. Copies may only be granted from them at judicial request.

e) If it is not possible to reconstitute a birth or death registration in accordance with the rules indicated above, the persons designated in articles 18, 29, and 44 of this law may request that they be made anew in the registries in use on the date of the request, complying with the same formalities required for every first registration. These actions shall be exempt from tax.

In these cases, if the background information allows, note shall be taken of the new registration in the corresponding blank page.

f) Reconstitution of registrations relating to the same class of facts or acts, whether births, marriages, or deaths, and originally annotated in various registries, may preferably be recorded in one, covering one or more years of operation. Testimony of this operation shall be left in the opening and closing certificates of the new registry.

Once reconstitution of a record or registry is completed, it must be vised with the signature or seal of the General Director of the National Civil Registry or of an Inspector Visitor of the Service or of a Civil Officer of the provincial capital.

For the purposes indicated in this article, the Courts of Justice, Notaries, and Public Archives, fiscal, semi-fiscal, or municipal departments, and any natural or juridical person shall be obliged to facilitate to the General Director of the National Civil Registry, when requested by this official, certificates, booklets, or proofs of Civil Registry registrations in their power.

The same obligation shall weigh on departments or establishments that have received from the Service lists, bulletins, statistical cards, and any other reports relating to their actions.

Once the authenticity of the document is verified, the Chief of the General Archive of the Civil Registry shall proceed as soon as possible to reproduce it, adding this copy to the files of background information formed for reconstitution of the registries. The original shall be returned.

The copies indicated above shall be effective instruments for reconstitution of registrations.

Registries in the power of the General Archive of the Civil Registry shall be considered originals for purposes of payment of taxes.

Reconstitution of registrations referred to in this article shall be under the responsibility of the General Director of the National Civil Registry, without prejudice to that corresponding to officials in charge of execution.

Errors committed in records rectified or reconstituted administratively, in accordance with the provisions of this law, shall be claimed before the General Director of the Service. Against the resolution this official issues, an appeal may be lodged within sixty days from its administrative notification by official letter noted on mail guide before the Judge of Letters of Minor Civil Jurisdiction of the petitioner's domicile, or before the First Court of Letters of Minor Civil Jurisdiction of Santiago.

In places where the mentioned court does not exist, an appeal may be made before the Judge of Letters of Major Civil Jurisdiction.

The Judge shall hear, before deciding, the General Director of the National Civil Registry, who may become a party in the proceeding. In this case, the rules of the summary proceeding shall apply, with the presentation made by the petitioner before the Judge being considered as the claim.

The General Director of the National Civil Registry, in all actions derived from this law, shall be exempt from payment of stamp tax, stamps, and stamped paper, and notary fees.

**Article 22:** The loss or destruction of a registry or part of it shall affect the official in charge of its custody, who shall be obliged to immediately report it to the corresponding Criminal Judge, to proceed ex officio with the investigation of the case.

If the author of the loss or destruction is the official in charge of its custody, they shall suffer the penalties of lower imprisonment in its medium to maximum degrees and absolute permanent disqualification for the office.

If the author is not that official, they shall suffer the penalty of lower imprisonment in its medium degree.

With respect to other responsible parties, the corresponding penalty shall be applied in accordance with the provisions of the Criminal Code.

Public action is granted to denounce the crime referred to in this article.

**Article 23:** If the signature of the Civil Registry Officer has been omitted in one or more registrations or sub-registrations or in their background information, the one noticing the omission shall report it within three days to the Civil Registry Registrar, who shall order them signed by the Officer who should have done so, and if this is not possible, by the one in whose charge the registry is, after verification of its authenticity and purity. This official shall also authorize registrations and sub-registrations and their background information in the Registrar's power suffering the same omission.

**Article 24:** Certificates or copies of registrations or sub-registrations issued by the Registrar or Civil Registry Officers shall have the character of public instruments.

Only the certificates or copies referred to in the preceding paragraph shall produce the effects of the records mentioned in articles 305, 306, 307, and 308 of the Civil Code.

**Article 25:** Civil Registry Officers shall monitor in their respective communes that registrations of facts constituting civil status are made and shall denounce before ordinary justice those who have omitted registration of a birth or death.

**Article 26:** Three days after death, registration may not proceed without judicial decree. The judge shall qualify the reasons that prevented registration, and if this omission was made with fraud or malice, they shall apply the sanctions established in the Criminal Code.

**Article 27:** One who maliciously provides false data on a civil status in a public deed shall suffer the penalties the Criminal Code applies to the one who fails to tell the truth in the narration of substantial facts in public documents.

---

## TITLE II: OF BIRTHS

**Article 28:** Within sixty days from the date of birth, registration of the newborn shall be made, at the verbal or written request of one of the persons indicated in the following article.

**Article 29:** The following persons are obliged to request registration:

1. The father, if known and able to declare;  
2. The closest relative over eighteen years of age living in the house where the birth occurred;  
3. The doctor or midwife who assisted the birth or, in their default, any person over eighteen years of age;  
4. The head of the public establishment or the owner of the house where the birth occurred, if occurring at a place other than the parents' residence;  
5. The mother, insofar as she is able to make said declaration;  
6. The person who has picked up the abandoned newborn; and  
7. The owner of the house or head of the establishment within whose enclosure the exposure of any foundling has occurred.

**Article 30:** Registration of a child may be requested within thirty days following birth only by the father or mother, personally or through an agent. After this period, the other persons indicated above are obliged to request said registration.

**Article 31:** Birth records shall contain, in addition to the indications common to every registration, the following:

1. Hour, day, month, year, and place of birth;  
2. Sex of the newborn;  
3. The name(s) of the newborn indicated by the person requesting registration, and the surname(s) of the newborn corresponding, in accordance with the provisions of Paragraph 2 of Title I of Book I of the Civil Code. In the case of registration of a newborn whose filiation is not determined, they shall be registered with the surname(s) indicated by the person requesting registration;  
4. The names, surnames, nationality, profession or trade, and domicile of the parents, or those of the father or mother who recognizes or has recognized them. Record shall be left of the names and surnames of the mother, even if there is no recognition, when the declarant's statement coincides with the proof of the doctor who assisted the birth, regarding the identities of the newborn and the woman who gave birth. No extravagant, ridiculous, improper for persons, ambiguous regarding sex, or contrary to good language name may be imposed on the newborn. If the Civil Registry Officer, in compliance with the provisions of the preceding paragraph, opposes registration of a name and the one requesting it insists, they shall immediately send the background information to the Judge of Letters or of the Department, who shall decide in the shortest possible time, without form of trial, but with hearing of the parties, whether the proposed name is included or not in the prohibition. These actions shall be exempt from taxes, and  
5. The commune or locality where the mother of the newborn was domiciled shall be recorded both in this record and in the birth certificate, as the child's place of origin.

**Article 32:** In the birth registration, the father, mother, or both may recognize the child as their own. The Civil Registry Officer must leave testimony in the birth registration of the declarations parents or their representatives make in accordance with number 1 of article 187 and paragraph 1 of article 188 of the Civil Code, certify the identity of the applicant, and require them to affix their signature, or, if unable to sign, their fingerprint.

Likewise, the Civil Registry Officer must inform in writing the mother or the person registering a child of non-determined filiation of the children's rights to claim legal determination of paternity or maternity and how to enforce them before the courts.

**Article 33:** Essential requirements of a birth registration are the date of birth and the name, surname, and sex of the newborn.

---

## TITLE III: OF MARRIAGES

**Articles 34-36:** Suppressed.

**Article 37:** The Civil Registry Officer shall not proceed to register the marriage without having privately informed the contracting parties that they may recognize common children born before marriage, for purposes of the provisions of the following article.

**Article 38:** In the act of marriage or requesting the registration referred to in article 20 of the Civil Marriage Law, the contracting parties may recognize children previously born, and the registration containing that declaration shall produce the effects indicated in paragraph 2 of article 185 of the Civil Code. They may also agree to total separation of property or participation in the partnership gains.

The Civil Registry Officer shall also inform the contracting parties that they may celebrate the agreements referred to in the preceding paragraph and that if they do not do so or say nothing about it, they shall be understood to be married under the regime of conjugal partnership.

**Article 39:** Registrations of marriages celebrated before a Civil Registry Officer, without prejudice to the indications common to every registration, shall contain:

1. The name and surnames of each of the contracting parties and the place of celebration;  
2. The place and date of their birth;  
3. Their status as single, widowed, or divorced. In these last two cases, the name of the deceased spouse or of the one with whom they contracted previous marriage, and the place and date of death or divorce judgment, respectively;  
4. Their profession or trade;  
5. The names and surnames of their parents, if known;  
6. The fact that neither spouse has any legal impediment or prohibition to contract marriage;  
7. The names and surnames of the witnesses and their testimony, under oath, on the fact that there are no impediments or prohibitions to celebrate the marriage and on the place of domicile or residence of the contracting parties;  
8. The name and surname of the person whose consent is necessary;  
9. Reliable testimony of consent for marriage, if needed;  
10. The names of children they have recognized in this act;  
11. Testimony of having agreed to separation of property or participation in the partnership gains, when agreed by the contracting parties in the act of marriage;  
12. Names and surnames of persons whose approval or authorization is necessary to authorize the agreement referred to in the preceding number;  
13. Reliable testimony of that approval or authorization, if necessary; and  
14. Signature of the contracting parties, of the witnesses, and of the Civil Registry Officer.

If either contracting party does not know how or cannot sign, testimony of this circumstance shall be left, without prejudice to the provisions of number 5 of article 12\.

**Article 40:** Essential requirements of a marriage registration are those indicated in numbers 1 of article 12, and 1, 7, and 14 of article 39\.

**Article 40 bis:** The record referred to in article 20 of the Civil Marriage Law shall be signed by the minister of worship before whom the contracting parties contracted religious marriage, and shall express the following information:

1. The identification of the religious entity before which the marriage was celebrated, with express mention of the number of the decree by virtue of which it enjoys public law legal personality. In the case of religious entities recognized by article 20 of Law 19,638, they shall cite this legal norm;  
2. The date and place of celebration of the marriage;  
3. The name and surnames of the contracting parties, as well as their identity card numbers;  
4. The date and place of birth of the contracting parties;  
5. Their status as single, divorced, or widowed and, in these last two cases, the name of the deceased spouse or of the one with whom they contracted previous marriage, and the place and date of death or divorce judgment, respectively;  
6. Their profession or trade;  
7. The names and surnames of their parents, if known;  
8. The names and surnames of two witnesses, as well as their identity card numbers, and their testimony, under oath, on the fact that none of the contracting parties has any legal impediment or prohibition to contract marriage;  
9. The name and surnames of the minister of worship, as well as their identity card number;  
10. The fact that the requirements established by law for the validity of civil marriage have been fulfilled, and  
11. The signature of the contracting parties, the witnesses, and the minister of worship.

If either contracting party does not know how or cannot sign, testimony of this circumstance shall be left.

The document proving the personhood of the respective minister of worship must be attached to the record.

**Article 40 ter:** For purposes of the provisions of article 20 of the Civil Marriage Law, registrations of marriages celebrated before religious entities shall contain or express, as applicable:

1. The record referred to in the preceding article;  
2. The document proving the personhood of the respective minister of worship;  
3. The fact that the record complies with the requirements established in the preceding article;  
4. The identification of the religious entity before which the marriage was celebrated, with mention of the decree or legal provision by virtue of which it enjoys public law legal personality;  
5. The names and surnames of the contracting parties;  
6. The mentions indicated in numbers 6, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12, and 13 of article 39 of this law;  
7. The fact that the period referred to in article 20 of the Civil Marriage Law has been complied with;  
8. The fact that the applicants for registration have been informed of the rights and duties corresponding to spouses according to law;  
9. The fact that the applicants for registration have granted before the Civil Registry Officer ratification of the consent given before the minister of worship, in accordance with article 20 of the Civil Marriage Law, and  
10. The signature of the applicants for registration and of the Civil Registry Officer.

Essential requirements of registration of a religious marriage are those indicated in numbers 1, 2, 9, and 10\.

**Article 41:** Marriages in article of death may be celebrated before any Civil Registry Officer and in any place.

The Civil Registry Officer shall note in the respective registration the circumstances in which the marriage was celebrated, and especially that it was celebrated in article of death.

**Articles 42-43:** Repealed.

---

## TITLE IV: OF DEATHS

**Article 44:** Registration of death shall be made by virtue of the verbal or written report that relatives of the deceased or inhabitants of the house where death occurred must give, or in their default, neighbors. Likewise, it shall be effected by virtue of a judicial resolution, in cases determined by law.

If death has occurred in a convent, hospital, lazaretto, hospice, prison, ship, barracks, or other public establishment, the head of it shall be obliged to request the license or burial permit and complete necessary requirements for the respective registration in the Registry.

The same obligation corresponds to the police authority in the case of finding a body not claimed by anyone or of the death of an unknown person.

**Article 45:** Upon requesting registration of a death, a certificate issued by the doctor in charge of verifying deaths or by the one who attended the deceased in their last illness must be presented, unless registration is made by virtue of the judicial resolution referred to in paragraph 1 of the preceding article.

If it concerns the death of a young child, the Civil Registry Officer shall investigate whether the birth has been previously registered, and if not, shall also proceed to make this registration.

In said certificate, as far as possible, the name, surname, status, profession, domicile, nationality, and effective or approximate age of the deceased shall be indicated; the name and surname of their spouse and parents; the hour and day of death, if known, or otherwise, those considered probable, and the illness or cause of death.

Verification of the circumstances indicated in the preceding paragraph, whenever there is no physician in the locality, may be substituted by the declaration of two or more witnesses, made before the Civil Registry Officer or before any judicial authority of the place where death occurred. This declaration shall preferably be made by persons who have treated the deceased most closely or who were present in their last moments, of all which express testimony shall be left in the registration.

**Article 46:** The Officer shall make the respective registration in the registry, and issue the license or permit and indicate in it the hour from which burial may be made, which shall not be before twenty-four hours after death. In case of epidemic, burial shall be in accordance with instructions issued by the health authority.

For burial in a cemetery located in a place other than where death occurred, the provisions of the corresponding health laws or regulations shall apply.

In the case of funerals classified as at-risk by resolution of the respective Regional Presidential Delegate, the Civil Registry Officer shall issue the license or permit in which the number and date of the said resolution shall be recorded, establishing the obligation to carry out burial or cremation within twenty-four hours following notification of the said resolution, unless the corpse is in the Legal Medical Service, in which case said period shall begin to run once the prosecutor of the Public Prosecutor's Office in charge of the respective criminal investigation has issued the order for delivery of the corpse.

A copy of the resolution must be attached to the death registration.

**Article 47:** Those in charge of cemeteries, of any kind, and owners and administrators of any place where a body is to be buried, shall not permit burial without the license or permit of the Civil Registry Officer of the commune where death occurred.

**Article 48:** Doctors referred to in paragraph 1 of article 45 who refuse to give the certificate indicated there free of charge, or one who buries a body without the prior license mentioned in article 46, shall suffer the penalty indicated in article 496 of the Criminal Code.

**Article 49:** The death of a creature dying in the mother's womb, or perishing before being completely separated from their mother, or not having survived separation for even a moment, shall not be registered in this registry.

In these cases, issuance of the burial permit shall be subject to the formalities prescribed by articles 46 and 47 as applicable.

**Article 50:** Essential requirements of a death registration are the date of death and the name, surname, and sex of the deceased.

---

## TITLE V: STILLBORN REGISTRY

**Article 50 bis:** A national, special, and voluntary registry is created, in which stillborns shall be registered.

The registration referred to in the preceding paragraph shall contain the identification of the stillborn by assigning a given name, followed by the surname(s) indicated by the applicant, and the sex of the child, if determined or determinable. Likewise, the registry may contain the identification of the gestating person, and of the progenitor, if they authorize it.

For the registration provided in this article, the medical death certificate and fetal mortality statistics shall be necessary.

In these cases, issuance of the burial license or permit shall be subject to the formalities prescribed by articles 46 and 47, as applicable.

---

## TITLE VI: MEASURES FAVORING LEGAL CONSTITUTION OF FAMILY

**Article 51:** Civil Registry Officers shall visit their respective commune or section, in the manner determined by regulation, to procure celebration of marriage of the man and woman who, cohabiting, have common children.

During their visit, they shall make birth registrations that apply, denounce those not registered in a timely manner, and ensure that these registrations are made.

**Article 52:** Owners and administrators of rural estates or mines, heads, directors, administrators, or managers of workshops, factories, workshops, hospitals, lazarettos, hospices, milk banks, public assistance, prisons, correctional homes, charitable establishments, and barracks, who prevent Civil Registry Officers from fulfilling obligations imposed by this law, shall be penalized with a fine of up to ten centavos.

**Article 53:** The Judge of Letters of the respective department shall appoint, at the proposal of the owner officer and under their responsibility, an Assistant Civil Registry Officer, for the duration of the visit, who shall have the same faculties and equal remuneration.

When the office of Civil Registry Officer is held by a woman, or by a Communal Judge, or when the titleholder is incapacitated, the visits referred to in article 51 shall be made by the Assistant Officer.

**Article 54:** Marriage and birth registrations the Civil Registry Officer makes during visits shall be made in special registries and in the form determined by this law.

**Article 55:** Sums allocated for funeral expenses of persons subject to the social security regime established by laws shall be paid to the surviving spouse, up to two escudos, with no other requirement than presentation of the marriage certificate and death certificate.

**Articles 56-70:** (Organizational provisions, largely repealed or superseded.)

---

# LAW NO. 17,344: CHANGE OF NAMES AND SURNAMES

**Article 1:** Every person has the right to use the names and surnames with which they have been identified in their respective birth registration.

Without prejudice to cases where laws authorize rectification of Civil Registry registrations, or the use of names and surnames different from the original as a consequence of legitimation, adoptive legitimation, or adoption, any person may request, once, to be authorized to change their names or surnames, or both at once, in the following cases:

a) When they are ridiculous, laughable, or morally or materially undermine them; b) When the applicant has been known for more than five years, for plausible reasons, by names or surnames, or both, different from their own; c) In cases of non-matrimonial filiation or where filiation is not determined, to add a surname when the person was registered with only one or to change one of the surnames imposed on the newborn when they were equal; d) When the applicant wishes to invert the order of surnames established in their birth registration; e) When the applicant wishes to use either surname of an ascendant in the direct line up to the second degree.

In cases where a person has been known for more than five years by one or more of the given names appearing in their birth record, the holder may request that the name(s) not used be suppressed in the registration, in their marriage record, and in the birth records of their minor descendants, as applicable.

Without prejudice to the foregoing, persons whose names or surnames, or both, are not of Spanish origin, may request authorization to translate them into Spanish. They may also request authorization to change them if their pronunciation or spelling is manifestly difficult in a Spanish-speaking environment.

If a minor lacks a legal representative or, if having one, they are impeded for any reason or refuse to authorize the minor to request the change or suppression of names or surnames referred to in this law, the judge shall decide, hearing the minor, at the request of any relative of the minor or the defender of minors and even ex officio.

**Article 2:** The competent judge to hear the proceedings referred to in this law shall be the Judge of Letters in Civil Law of the petitioner's domicile.

The court, upon processing the request of a person over 18 years of age, shall inform them of the administrative procedure to request change of order of surnames before the Civil Registry and Identification Service, regulated in articles 17 bis, 17 ter, and 17 quáter of Law No. 4,808 on Civil Registry.

The request shall be published in extract in the Official Gazette of the 1st or 15th of each month, or the following business day if not published on those dates.

The extract, drafted by the Court Secretary, shall necessarily contain the identification of the applicant and the indication of the names and surnames they intend to use in replacement of their own.

Within thirty days from the date of the notice, anyone having an interest may oppose the request. In such case, the opponent shall provide, together with their opposition, the background information justifying it, and the judge shall proceed without form of trial, weighing the evidence in conscience and based on the proceedings ordered to be carried out.

If there is no opposition, the court shall proceed with knowledge of the case, after summary information.

In all cases, it shall be mandatory to hear the General Directorate of Civil Registry and Identification. The Directorate shall report whether the applicant has convictions, data on the birth records of each of their children, data on their spouse or the person with whom they are united by civil union agreement, and any other relevant background information. Likewise, the court shall require reports from Carabineros de Chile, the Investigative Police of Chile, and the Public Prosecutor's Office to report whether the applicant is currently prosecuted or formally charged, or has pending convictions, or there are pending arrest or detention orders against them, or they are subject to other personal precautionary measures.

The change of name or surname or suppression of given names shall not be authorized if from the respective extract of filiation that the Directorate will send as part of its report, and from the reports of Carabineros de Chile, the Investigative Police of Chile, and the Public Prosecutor's Office, it appears that the applicant is currently prosecuted or formally charged, or there are pending arrest or detention orders against them, or they are subject to other personal precautionary measures, or has been convicted of a crime or simple offense deserving afflictive punishment, unless, in the latter case, more than ten years have elapsed from the date the conviction became final and the sentence has been served. In no case shall the change or suppression be authorized when the applicant has been convicted of any of the crimes established in Paragraphs V and VI of Title Seven of Book Two of the Criminal Code, in which case the procedure contained in this law shall not apply. Nor shall the change or suppression be authorized when, from the background information in the proceeding, the judge appreciates that there is a risk of affecting the security of other persons, or that there is a risk of affecting the development of pending proceedings, or that there is a risk of fraud being committed.

The publication referred to in paragraph 2 of this article shall not be necessary, nor shall opposition be admitted in the case of paragraph 3 of article 1\.

The publication to be made in the Official Gazette shall be free of charge.

**Article 3:** The judgment authorizing the change of names or surnames, or both at once, or the suppression of given names shall be executed in accordance with DFL No. 2,128 of August 10, 1930, and shall only produce legal effects once the new registration is extended in accordance with article 104 of the cited legal body.

For these purposes, in the case of persons born abroad and whose birth is not registered in Chile, it shall be necessary to first proceed to registration of birth in the Registry of the First Section of the commune of Santiago.

When the court authorizes the change of names or surnames, or both at once, or suppression, of a person who has been convicted of a crime or simple offense deserving afflictive punishment, in the same judgment it shall order the Civil Registry and Identification Service to update the applicant's data contained in the General Registry of Convictions and the Police Record, regulated in Decree Law No. 645 of 1925, of the Ministry of Justice.

**Article 4:** Once the birth record is modified, the person who has changed their name or surnames in accordance with the provisions of the preceding articles may only use, in the future, in all their actions, their new given name or surnames, in the form ordered by the judge.

The change of surname may not be extended to the applicant's parents, and shall not alter filiation; but shall extend to their descendants subject to parental authority, and also to other descendants consenting.

If the applicant is married or has minor descendants, they must also request, in the same act as requesting the change of their surname, the pertinent modification in their marriage record and in the birth records of their children.

The change of order of surnames authorized under letter d) of article 1 shall only operate with respect to the applicant, without extending to ascendants, and shall not alter filiation. The change of order of the applicant's surnames shall cause the change of the respective transmission surname to minor children, proceeding equally with respect to all of them. Without prejudice to the foregoing, if the applicant has one or more children over 14 and under 18 years of age, they must manifest their consent by written declaration made before the court, in which case it shall also proceed equally with respect to all minor children. For these purposes, the applicant must also request, in the same act as requesting the change of order of their surnames, the pertinent modification in the birth records of their minor children, the consent of all children over 14 and under 18 years of age being manifested, if applicable. In the judgment authorizing the change of order of surnames, the court shall inform of the possibility of requesting the change of the respective transmission surname by adult children of the person who obtained the change of order of surnames, before the Civil Registry and Identification Service, in accordance with article 17 ter, paragraph 6, of Law No. 4,808 on Civil Registry.

**Article 5:** Use of primitive names or surnames and the use of the new name or surname to exempt, impede, hinder, or evade fulfillment of any obligation shall be punished with the penalty of lower imprisonment in its minimum degree.

**Article 6:** The following final paragraphs are added to article 31 of Law No. 4,808 on Civil Registry:

"No extravagant, ridiculous, improper for persons, ambiguous regarding sex, or contrary to good language name may be imposed on the newborn.

"If the Civil Registry Officer, in compliance with the provisions of the preceding paragraph, opposes registration of a name and the one requesting it insists, they shall immediately send the background information to the Judge of Letters of the department, who shall decide in the shortest possible time, without form of trial, but with hearing of the parties, whether the proposed name is included or not in the prohibition. These proceedings shall be exempt from taxes."

---

# LAW NO. 16,618: LAW ON MINORS (Selected Provisions)

**Article 1:** This law shall apply to minors, without prejudice to special provisions establishing another age for determined effects.

In case of doubt about the age of a person apparently a minor, they shall be provisionally considered as such, while their age is verified.

**Article 15:** A department called "Minors Police" is created in the General Directorate of Carabineros, with personnel specialized in work with minors. This department shall establish in each provincial capital city and in places that are seats of a Juvenile Court, Commissariats or Sub-Commissariats of Minors.

The Minors Police shall have the following purposes:

a) To collect minors in irregular situations needing assistance or protection; b) To exercise, in accordance with instructions issued by the National Minors Service, control of sites considered centers of minor corruption; c) To inspect public shows, entertainment centers, or any place with public attendance, to prevent the attendance of minors when not appropriate for them, and d) To report to the Public Prosecutor's Office facts punishable by article 62\. e) To provide immediate protection to a child or adolescent in a situation of grave, direct, and imminent danger to their life or physical integrity. To do so, when such circumstances concur, they may enter a closed place and remove the child or adolescent, in all cases immediately bringing the facts to the knowledge of the Juvenile Judge, the Criminal Judge, or the Public Prosecutor's Office Fiscal, as applicable.

**Article 16:** Repealed.

**Article 16 bis:** In cases where a minor's rights appear gravely violated or threatened, Carabineros de Chile shall take them to the home of their parents or caregivers, as applicable, and deliver them to them, informing them of the facts motivating police action.

If, to protect the minor's physical or psychological integrity, it is indispensable to separate them from their family environment or from persons having them in their care, Carabineros de Chile shall take them to a Transit and Distribution Center and report the facts at the first hearing to the respective juvenile judge.

In the case of commission of a crime of which a minor is the victim, Carabineros shall also submit the background information to the Public Prosecutor's Office according to general rules.

In all hypotheses provided in this article where Carabineros has taken a minor to a Transit and Distribution Center, the person in charge of the Center receiving the minor must bring them before said judge at the first hearing, for them to adopt measures that apply in accordance with this law.

**Article 17:** Heads of detention establishments are prohibited from keeping minors under eighteen years of age in communication with other detainees or prisoners over that age.

The official not complying with this provision shall be punished administratively with suspension from office for up to one month.

**Article 30:** In cases provided in article 8, numbers 7 and 8, of the law creating family courts, the juvenile judge, by reasoned resolution, may decree measures necessary to protect minors whose rights are gravely violated or threatened. In particular, the judge may:

1) Order attendance at programs or actions of support, reparation, or orientation for minors, their parents, or persons having them in their care, to face and overcome crisis situations, and issue pertinent instructions, and  
2) Order admission of the minor to a Transit or Distribution Center, substitute home, or residential establishment.

If adopting the measure referred to in number 2, the judge shall prefer, for provisionally taking care of the minor, their consanguineous relatives or other persons with whom they have a trusting relationship.

Admission to a protection establishment shall only proceed in cases where, to protect the minor's physical or psychological integrity, it is indispensable to separate them from their family environment or persons having them in their care, and in default of persons referred to in the preceding paragraph. This measure shall be essentially temporary, shall not be decreed for more than one year, and must be reviewed by the court every six months, for which it shall request reports from the person in charge of the respective Center or home. Without prejudice, it may be renewed in the same terms and conditions as long as the cause giving rise to it subsists. In any case, the court may substitute or set aside the measure before expiration of the period for which it was ordered.

**Article 42:** For the sole purpose of article 226 of the Civil Code, one or both parents shall be understood to be in the case of physical or moral inability:

1. When mentally incapacitated;  
2. When suffering from chronic alcoholism;  
3. When they do not care for the upbringing, personal care, or education of the child;  
4. When they consent to the child being given to vagrancy or mendicancy in the street or public places, whether openly or under pretext of profession or trade;  
5. When they have been convicted of kidnapping or abandonment of minors;  
6. When they mistreat or set bad examples to the minor, or when the minor's stay in the home constitutes a danger to their morality;  
7. When any other causes place the minor in moral or material danger.

**Article 43:** Loss of parental authority, suspension of its exercise, and loss or suspension of guardianship of minors do not free parents or guardians from obligations to provide for their education and maintenance.

The juvenile judge shall determine the amount and form in which these obligations will be fulfilled, considering the obliged person's means and domestic circumstances.

The judgment issued shall have executive merit and allow enforcement before the corresponding court.

**Article 49:** The departure of minors from Chile shall be subject to the rules set forth in this article, without prejudice to the provisions of Law No. 18,703.

If the child's guardianship has not been entrusted by the judge to either parent or a third party, they may not leave without authorization from both parents, or from the one who recognized them, as applicable.

If guardianship has been entrusted by the judge to one parent or a third party, the child may not leave except with authorization from the person to whom it was entrusted.

If the right referred to in article 229 of the Civil Code is regulated by judicial judgment or agreement approved by the court, the authorization of the parent in whose favor it was established shall also be required.

The permission referred to in the preceding paragraphs must be given by public deed or private deed authorized by a Notary Public. Said permission shall not be necessary if the minor leaves the country in the company of the person or persons who must give it.

In the event that the authorization cannot be granted or is unreasonably refused by one of those who by virtue of this article must give it, it may be granted by the juvenile judge of the place where the minor resides. The judge, to authorize the minor's departure in these cases, shall consider the benefit it may bring them and indicate the period for which authorization is granted.

If the period referred to in the preceding paragraph expires without the minor unjustifiably returning to the country, the judge may decree suspension of maintenance payments that have been decreed.

In other cases, for a minor to leave the country, authorization from the juvenile court of their residence shall be required.

Without prejudice to the provisions of the preceding paragraphs, in the case of minor children of official resident foreigners, the permission or authorization referred to in this article may also be granted by the Consul of the country of the father's, mother's, or both parents' nationality, as applicable. This permission or authorization shall indicate the destination(s) of the minor, and must also send a copy thereof by the most expeditious means to the Ministry of Foreign Relations of Chile.

However, the provision of the preceding paragraph shall not apply if the minor or either of their parents has Chilean nationality.

Notwithstanding the foregoing, if the maintenance provider does not give their authorization and is listed in the Registry of Debtors of Maintenance, the judge may subsidiarily grant said permission without considering their reasons for refusal, which shall not apply if departure abroad is for the purpose of establishing permanent residence.

---

# LAW NO. 14,908: ON FAMILY ABANDONMENT AND PAYMENT OF MAINTENANCE

**Article 1:** Family courts shall hear maintenance lawsuits, at the domicile of the maintenance provider or the maintenance recipient, at the latter's choice. These proceedings shall be processed in accordance with Law No. 19,968, with the modifications established in this legal body.

The same court that decreed maintenance or the court of the new domicile of the maintenance recipient, at their choice, shall be competent to hear requests for increase of maintenance.

The court of the maintenance recipient's domicile shall hear requests for reduction or cessation of maintenance.
