The court must declare inadmissible the request for reduction or cessation of maintenance in the case where the person has a current registration in the National Registry of Maintenance Debtors, unless qualified background information is presented, in accordance with the provisions of paragraph 4 of article 3\.

The mother, whatever her age, may request maintenance for the child already born or unborn. If she is a minor, the judge must exercise the faculty granted by article 19 of Law No. 19,968, in the mother's interest.

**Article 2:** The claim may omit indication of the defendant's domicile if unknown. In such case, the court shall proceed in accordance with the provisions of article 23 of Law No. 19,968.

The defendant must inform the court of any change of domicile, employer, and place of work or services within thirty days from the change.

A defendant failing to comply with the preceding paragraph shall be fined 1 to 15 monthly tax units, at fiscal benefit.

The sponsoring attorney, in compliance with the parties' legal duty to update the form of electronic notification offered to the court, even in the compliance stage and prior to renouncing representation, must inform the court of a valid electronic notification form for their client. The sponsoring attorney failing to comply shall be fined 3 to 15 monthly tax units, at fiscal benefit.

**Article 3:** For purposes of decreeing maintenance when a minor requests it from their father or mother, the maintenance provider is presumed to have the means to provide it.

By virtue of this presumption, the minimum amount of maintenance decreed in favor of a minor recipient may not be less than forty percent of the minimum remunerational income corresponding to the provider's age. In the case of two or more minors, said amount may not be less than 30% for each of them. Without prejudice to the foregoing, the judge, in the resolution fixing or approving maintenance, must express its amount in monthly tax units, in accordance with paragraph 2 of article 6\.

All the foregoing is without prejudice to the provisions of paragraph 1 of article 7 of this law.

If the provider justifies to the court that they lack the means to pay the minimum amount established in the preceding paragraph, the judge may prudentially reduce it.

When decreed maintenance is not paid or is insufficient to cover the child's needs, the maintenance recipient may demand maintenance from grandparents, in accordance with article 232 of the Civil Code, unless their sole source of income is an old-age, disability, or survivorship pension.

**Article 4:** In maintenance lawsuits, the judge shall rule on provisional maintenance along with admitting the claim, with the sole merit of documents and background information presented.

The defendant shall have five days to oppose the provisional amount decreed. In the notification of the claim, they shall be informed of this faculty. Once opposition is filed, the judge shall decide summarily, unless from the background information they deem it necessary to summon a hearing, which must be held within ten days.

If no opposition exists within the period indicated in paragraph 2, the resolution fixing provisional maintenance shall become final.

The court may provisionally grant a request for increase, reduction, or cessation of maintenance when it deems there are sufficient background information justifying it.

The resolution decreeing provisional maintenance or the one ruling provisionally on the request for increase, reduction, or cessation of maintenance shall be subject to the remedy of reconsideration with subsidiary appeal, which shall be granted only in the devolutive effect and shall have priority for hearing and judgment.

Immediately after decreeing provisional maintenance, the court must ex officio order the corresponding financial entity to open a savings account or other equivalent instrument exclusively for fulfillment of the obligation.

The judge failing to comply with the provisions of the preceding paragraph shall incur fault or abuse that the injured party may pursue in accordance with article 536 of the Organic Code of Courts.

**Article 5:**
